- `signal changed(property)` after every edit: the TuningData property's name; `&""` after a preset load, a reset or `apply()` (anything may have changed); `"moves/<move id>/<field>"` after a move edit. Code that keeps a tunable re-reads it on `changed`: a training dummy updates `max_health` from `dummy_health` (keeping its share of health) and drops its wind-up-scaled jab after a `dummy_jab` edit. The cameras read theirs (FOV and the rest) every frame.
- `tuning_properties()`: TuningData's exported properties in declaration order, each property-list entry with its `group` and `subgroup`.
- Moves: `move_ids()`; `get_move(id)` returns the loaded, shared MoveData (every `.tres` in `game/combat/moves/`, kept for the session, so edits survive a restart with R). `move_properties()` lists the editable fields, the groups in `MOVE_GROUPS`: frame data, damage and reaction (damage, hitstun, knockback, reaction, tier...), blocking, movement and camera. Hitboxes and combo branches are structure and stay out. `set_move_value(id, field, value)` edits one; `shipped_move_value()` (from a fresh load of the `.tres`), `move_overrides()` (the fields that differ), `reset_move(id)` and `reset_moves()`. An edit applies on the move's next use: move sets and branches reference the same resources, and the rig keys its move animations by frame data, so a startup edit gets a re-timed animation.
- Presets are JSON: `{"preset": name, "format": 2, "tuning": {property: value}, "moves": {id: {field: value}}}`, in TuningData declaration order, with vectors and colors as arrays. A preset holds every TuningData value outside the "Debug" group and only the move fields that differ from the shipped `.tres`.
  - The F4 cheats stay out: `PRESET_SKIPPED_GROUPS` ("Debug": `god_mode`, `one_hit_ko`) is never saved, loaded or reset, so a preset can't turn a cheat on and Reset to default doesn't turn one off. `preset_properties()` lists what a preset holds.
  - Names are lower case and file-safe (`sanitize_preset_name()`), so one name is one file on every OS, including this case-insensitive macOS disk.
  - `save_preset(name)` writes `user_dir/<name>.json` (`user://tuning`). It refuses (`ERR_ALREADY_IN_USE`) the reserved names `default` and `_backup` (`is_reserved_preset_name()`).
  - `export_preset(name)` writes `export_dir/<name>.json` (`res://tuning`, only when running from the project folder). It refuses `default`, in any case, unless every value is the shipped default.
  - `load_preset(name)` reads the user's copy first, then the shipped one; `default` is always the shipped one. `load_preset_file(path)`, `apply_preset(dict)` and `preset_dict(name)` are the parts underneath.
  - Loading starts from the shipped defaults (TuningData and moves), so a value a preset lacks keeps its default. Unknown names, unreadable values and enum ids the hint lacks are skipped; a number past its `@export_range` end is clamped to it (unless `or_greater` / `or_less`). Each is listed in `last_warnings`.
  - A value whose meaning changed is migrated from an older `format` (`_migrate()`, listed in `last_warnings`; a preset with no `format` counts as the current one). Format 2 (M2 phase 3): `dummy_revive_s` counts from the KO's blink-off instead of from the KO, so a format-1 preset saved before the blink-off (it has no `ko_linger_s`) loads its old count less `ko_linger_s` and `ko_blink_s`, at least 0, and the dummy is back as long after the KO as it was. A format-1 preset with `ko_linger_s` was saved since and loads as it is.
  - `backup_unsaved()` writes the current values to the user preset `_backup` when they hold edits no saved, exported or loaded preset holds (`has_unsaved()`). The panel calls it before Load and Reset to default, so loading `_backup` undoes either.
  - `user_dir` and `export_dir` default to `USER_DIR` and `EXPORT_DIR`. The tests point them at a scratch folder of their own run, so they never touch the `user://tuning` that every worktree and Game-boy's own presets share.
  - `reset_to_default()`, `is_default()`, `list_presets()` and `sanitize_preset_name()` complete the API.
  - `res://tuning/default.json` is the shipped defaults. `godot --headless -s tools/gen_default_preset.gd` regenerates it after a TuningData default changes or a tunable is added, and `tests/test_tuning_panel.gd` fails until it matches.

**`TuningOverlay`** (`game/ui/tuning_overlay.gd`/`.tscn`; the test room adds one in `_build_presentation()`):
- It hosts the panel and the cheat menu in one full-screen, transparent, embedded `Window` over the game. Nothing pauses.
- **Input:** an embedded window's input never reaches the game's `_input` / `_unhandled_input` handlers. A click on the panel therefore can't recapture the mouse (`GameInput._input`) or punch, and keys typed into a field don't reach the debug keys. The window covers the screen, so clicks around the menus land on it too.
- **Player:** while open, the live player's `PlayerInput` is switched to scripted, so it gets idle frames with no look. It is switched back `UNFREEZE_TICKS` (2) physics ticks after closing, so the key or button that closed the overlay (gamepad B is also the dodge) never reaches the player. A player that is already scripted (the combo bot, tests) is left alone.
- **Mouse:** released while open, and captured again on closing unless the game is paused.
- **Bindings:** while open, every `GameInput.BINDINGS` action except `tuning_panel` and `cheats` has its events lifted out of the InputMap (and is released); closing puts them back. Code that polls the Input singleton, such as the free-fly camera's arrow and Page keys, therefore stays still while the panel uses those keys.
- **Keys:** F1 / View toggles the panel and F4 the cheat menu, from the game or the window. Esc (`ui_cancel`, also gamepad B) closes both and does not pause. The other debug keys (F2, F3, F5, H) work once the overlay is closed.
- **Gamepad UI:** Godot 4.7's default `ui_accept` and `ui_cancel` have no joypad buttons. The overlay adds A and B to them (`add_gamepad_ui_events()`), so A presses buttons, flips toggles and opens drop-downs, and B closes a drop-down, then the menus.
- **Help:** opening the panel hides the HUD's startup help, which sits where the panel docks. H shows it again.

**`TuningPanel`** and **`TuningField`** (`game/ui/tuning_panel.gd`, `tuning_field.gd`): the panel is docked left, clear of the view center and the combat log.
- **Built from the data:** one category per TuningData `@export_group`, in declaration order, with a row per exported property and a heading per `@export_subgroup` (`Tuning.exported_properties()` records each property's `group` and `subgroup`). New tunables, the M2 VFX, Audio and ragdoll groups included, appear with no UI code; a long group is organized by wrapping its sections in `@export_subgroup`, which renames nothing (presets key on property names).
- **Editors by type:**
  - a ranged number gets a slider plus a number field (the field allows the hint's `or_greater` / `or_less`);
  - a bool gets a toggle that says on or off, and an enum a drop-down;
  - a Vector2 or Vector3 gets one number field per axis, a Color a color button, and a String a text field.
- **Changed values:** a value away from its default turns yellow and lights its "reset". Categories and moves holding one are marked " *".
- **Moves page:** pick a move, edit its `move_properties()`, or reset the move. The picker row sits above the scrolling rows, so it stays in view.
- **Presets:** a name field with Save and Export, a list of presets with Load, and Reset to default, with a status line for the result. Load and Reset first keep unsaved values as `_backup` and say so.
- **Markers:** an edit updates only its own row and category mark; the move markers are rebuilt only on a move edit or a full refresh.
- **Tooltips:** the `##` doc comments, plus the default and the range. They are read from the script source, so an exported build shows only the default and the range.
- **Gamepad:** focus links are set explicitly, never left to where rows happen to line up.
  - The category buttons chain up and down; focusing one shows its page. Right enters the page's first row.
  - A page's rows (sliders, toggles, drop-downs) chain up and down; the first goes up to Load, and the last holds. Left goes back to the category button, except on a slider, where left and right step the value.
  - LB / RB switch to the previous / next category from anywhere in the panel, keeping the focus in the page or in the list.
  - The focused row's name gets a bar behind it.
  - Number fields and vectors take the mouse and keyboard only, because up and down would step them instead of moving on.

**`CheatMenu`** (`game/ui/cheat_menu.gd`, docked top right): a debug tool. Like `TimeController`, it is an exception to "presentation never writes game state". Every action is a public method:
- `set_god_mode()` and `set_one_hit_ko()` set Tuning's "Debug" group, which presets leave out (session state).
- `set_time_scale()` sets `TimeController.debug_scale` to 0.25× or 1×, which also stretches hitstop and slow-mo (see "Time model").
- `spawn_dummy()` places a training dummy at the crosshair (`spawn_point()`), or says "No room" when there is no clear spot:
  - The ray runs from the first-person camera's view center. In side view and free fly (no crosshair) or with no camera, it runs from the player's eye and look. It reaches `spawn_max_distance_m` (6 m) and meets the world and actor bodies.
  - On a body, the spot is `spawn_beside_m` (1 m) to its side, whichever side is clear, so the new dummy stands in view rather than behind it. On a wall, the spot is `spawn_wall_clearance_m` (0.5 m) off it along its normal. On the floor, it's where the ray lands. A ray that meets nothing ends `spawn_open_distance_m` (3 m) out.
  - The spot drops to the floor and stays at least `spawn_min_distance_m` (1 m) from the player.
  - A spot is clear when a capsule the dummy's size overlaps no world geometry, no actor stands within `spawn_actor_clearance_m` (0.7 m), its floor is within `spawn_max_rise_m` of the player's, and the player can see it. Otherwise the nearest clear spot within `spawn_search_radius_m` (2 m) is used, trying beside it first, then toward the player, then behind.
  - The dummy faces the player.
- `reset_dummies()` puts every dummy back on the post recorded when the menu first saw it, standing, at full `dummy_health`. `remove_spawned_dummies()` removes the spawned ones.
- "Skip to arena N" shows disabled until Milestone 5.

## Enemy AI and the rear warning (Milestone 3)

The contract of Milestone 3 ([docs/milestones/M3-plan.md](milestones/M3-plan.md) Part C section 2, with Part A's amendments applied). S0-scaffold landed every name below as a skeleton or hook, with no change to the M2 game; the packages fill them in. The headings after the contract are one per package, each filled by its owner and nobody else.

### The contract

**Player look** (`PlayerCharacter`; the maths is `ViewGeometry`, `game/combat/view_geometry.gd`, static and simulation-safe, no camera):
- `get_look_forward() -> Vector3`: flat unit vector of the look (`look_yaw` only).
- `look_bearing_deg(point) -> float`: signed flat degrees from the look to `point`, measured from `global_position`: 0 ahead, positive left, +-180 behind.
- `is_outside_view(point, half_angle_deg) -> bool`. "Outside the player's view" means `is_outside_view(p, Tuning.data.rear_warning_angle_deg)` everywhere: the director's blind spot and the warning.
- `is_holding(victim) -> bool`: the holder hook, true while `victim` is held (`_held == victim and _holding_valid()`).
- `ViewGeometry`: `forward(look_yaw)`, `bearing_deg(look_yaw, from, point)`, `is_outside_view(look_yaw, from, point, half_angle_deg)` and `warning_corner(bearing, distance_m, reach_m, angle_deg, behind_deg, margin_m) -> StringName` (`&""`, `&"left"`, `&"right"`, `&"both"`: the rule of the warning, SPEC §8). The director, the HUD's `RearWarning` and the bots' oracle all call it. `tests/test_view_geometry.gd` holds its truth table, written by hand.

**`CombatActor` hooks** (S0 only; nobody else edits `combat_actor.gd` in M3):
- `_apply_armor(hit)` (virtual, no-op): called in `receive_hit()` after damage, `killed`, `health_changed` and the flash, before the move is cancelled. It may set `hit.armor_absorbed = true` (and must then set `hit.reaction = NONE` and `hit.knockback = ZERO`) or change `hit.reaction` or `hit.knockback`. If `hit.armor_absorbed and not hit.killed`, `_on_armor_absorbed(hit)` (virtual, no-op) is called and `receive_hit()` returns true, skipping the cancel, `_on_hit_received()` and `_apply_reaction()`: the move, a wind-up above all, runs on. A killing hit is never skipped.
- `HitDetector`: an armored victim that absorbed a hit (`armor_absorbed`, not killed) never goes into hitstop; only the attacker does (the strike still feels solid, and a jab never stretches a Bruiser's wind-up or its warning). A blocked-only frame still recoils the attacker, as before.
- `_on_grabbed(by)` (virtual): the end of `start_grabbed()`. `_held_anim() -> StringName` (virtual, `&"held"`): the clip `start_grabbed()` and the held jolt of `_apply_reaction()` play. `is_holding(victim)` (virtual, false): the holder side.
- `break_free(invuln_frames := Tuning.grab_break_invuln_frames)`: the victim side of mashing out. The holder's `_on_hold_broken(victim)` (virtual, no-op: where the holder staggers away at `grab_break_push_mps`) is called, the victim is released on its feet where it stands and made invulnerable for `invuln_frames` sim frames, and `CombatEvents.grab_escaped(victim, holder)` fires. A no-op unless GRABBED.
- `_parry_staggers() -> bool` (virtual, true): `react_to_block()` staggers a parried attacker only when it is true; otherwise the attacker takes the ordinary blocked-attack recoil (a move with `blocked_recoil_frames` 0 then does nothing).
- `abort_move()` (public): `_cancel_move()` then `_return_to_neutral()`.
- Pin order: `start_grabbed(by)` sets `process_physics_priority = by.process_physics_priority + 1` (the victim pins after its holder's rig advanced, so no tick of lag); it is restored on release, launch, KO and `reset_state()`. `_pin_to_holder()` also releases when the holder `is_gone()`.
- There is no `_keeps_pose_after_strike()`: a hit applied by code never sets `_pull_strike`.

**Events** (`CombatEvents`): `grab_escaped(victim, holder)`, `boss_phase_changed(boss, phase)` (phase 1 is implicit). No other signal in M3: presentation reads tokens, AI states and slots through the director's read API and `debug_state_text()`. `HitInfo.describe()` ends with " ARMOR" for an absorbed hit. `released(holder, victim)` and `grab_escaped(victim, holder)` carry `null` for the holder when it was freed mid-hold (an F4 remove-all, a bot's end-of-act clear; a typed listener cannot take a freed object): a listener must not assume the holder is there.

**Move data**: `MoveData.reach_m` (0 to 10 m, group "Movement", so F1 edits it and presets save it): the flat distance from the attacker's origin to a standing 1.8 m target's origin at which the move's strike connects, **travel included** (`free_lunge`); 0 means "not an AI attack". The AI closes to it before winding up and the rear warning fires within `reach_m + rear_warning_range_margin_m`. `lunge` and `free_lunge` now go to 8 m. `dummy_jab.reach_m` is 1.05 (the only shipped move with a reach). Enemy move rules, enforced by `tests/test_move_data.gd` from each archetype's `tests/enemy_moves/<archetype>_table.gd`: its own animation id (`get_anim() == id`), no magnetism, no `branches` or `auto_next` (the AI starts each hit of a chain itself), startup at least the archetype's wind-up floor, a reach, **no FINISHER tier** (the finisher fires the global slow-mo and impact frame), `blocked_recoil_frames` 0 for the Bruiser, the boss and the grabs, grabs and the ground pound unblockable, and the 15% rule: a chain that one token buys (`ATTACKS`) with a knockdown, launch or throw in it costs the player at most 15 damage, unless it is the boss's (SPEC §4). One telegraph per attack: a chain link (the Punk's cross, the Thug's second and third swings) starts with no wind-up of its own, and only if the previous hit landed or was blocked; a whiffed opener ends the attack.

**`EnemyActor`** (`game/enemies/enemy_actor.gd`, `class_name EnemyActor extends CombatActor`; `TrainingDummy` does not extend it): `enum AiState { ENTER, APPROACH, POSTURE, ENGAGE, WINDUP, ATTACK, RECOVER, REACT, KO }` (an overlay on `ActorState`, never written into it); exports `archetype`, `heavy`, `prefers_blind_side`, `voice_pitch_mult`; read-only state `ai_state`, `has_token`, `blind_priority_granted`, `posture_slot` (`Vector3.INF` = none); `ai_state_name()`, `wants_token()`, `has_token_priority()` (false; true for an enemy waking in place, whose token the director grants first), `grant_token(blind)` and `revoke_token()` and `set_slot(pos)` and `set_flanking(on)` (director only; read with `is_flanking()`, false by default), `attack_range_m()`, `force_attack(index := 0)` (bots and tests: skip the token and wind up now from FREE), `enter_from(spawn_pos, lane_point)`, and `static windup_move(move, heavy)` (the wind-up multiplier and floors, cached per move id). Groups: `"actor"`, `"enemy"`, `"ai_enemy"` (the boss also `"boss"`). A dead enemy is `health <= 0`, not `is_ko()`; once `is_gone()` it leaves every group, then `queue_free()`s. Behaviour runs only in FREE, plus turning during WINDUP. The S0 skeleton stands still.

**`EnemyDirector`** (`game/enemies/enemy_director.gd`, `class_name EnemyDirector extends Node`, group `"enemy_director"`): `find(tree)`, `ensure(host)` (find, or add one under `host`: an enemy spawned by F4 in the training room), `release_token(enemy)`, `slot_for(enemy)`, `may_start_windup(enemy) -> bool` (an enemy with a token calls it right before winding up; true means go now and the director records the wind-up start; it enforces `director_min_windup_gap_s`, so no two wind-ups start together), and the read API for F3 and tests, which presentation reads and never writes: `debug_token_holders()`, `debug_slot_of(enemy)` (`Vector3.INF` = none), `debug_ring() -> Vector2`, `attacker_cap()` (0 = unlimited), `debug_waiting()`. It runs its own `_physics_process` on `delta * TimeController.sim_scale()` with a seeded RNG (`rng_seed`), and holds while `GameInput.menu_open`. The S0 skeleton answers "nobody holds anything" and always says go.

**Menus hold the fight**: `GameInput.menu_open` is set by `TuningOverlay` while F1 or F4 is open. While it is true the director grants no tokens and takes back any token whose wind-up has not started, an enemy with no move running stands, faces the player and asks for nothing, and waves start no spawns; an attack already winding up finishes.

**Other APIs**: `EncounterSpawner` (P1-room): `spawn_enemy(id, at, yaw, from_wave := false)`, `spawn_adds(id, count, near)`, `alive_count()`, `set_waves_enabled(on)`, `spawn_points`, `scene_override`; group `"encounter_spawner"`. `EnemyCatalog` (S0, `game/enemies/enemy_catalog.gd`): `ENTRIES` (`{id, label, scene}` for `dummy`, `punk`, `kicker`, `thug`, `bruiser`, `boss`), `available()` (only scenes that exist), `scene(id)`, `body_size(id) -> Vector2` (radius, height; cached): the cheat menu and the spawner read it and nothing else hard-codes an enemy path. `RearWarning` (P1-hud, `game/ui/rear_warning.gd`): `shown(side, count, heavy)`, `cleared(side)` (not `hidden`: CanvasItem owns a native `hidden` signal), `corner_state(side) -> Dictionary`, `advance(delta)`.

**Rooms and keys**: `test_room.gd`/`.tscn` is frozen for M3. F6 (`GameInput.switch_room()`) clears hitstop and slow-mo, unpauses and changes scene to the other of `GameInput.ROOM_SCENES` (the crowd street, the training room); it does nothing when the target scene does not exist, or when the running scene is neither room (a bot or test host). The crowd room's contract for bots and tests (direct-child HUD, directors and `TuningOverlay`; `player`, `player_start`, `player_yaw_deg`, `with_presentation`, `waves_enabled_on_start`, `rng_seed`, `spawner`, `director`; `enemies()`) is P1-room's.

**Tuning layout**: group "Enemies" (`attacker_cap`, `blind_spot_priority`, `windup_mult`) then one `@export_subgroup` per owner, in this order: Wind-up, Posture, Navigation (P1-ai), Director (P1-director), Waves (P1-room), Street Punk (P1-ai), Kicker (P2-kicker), Pipe Thug (P2-thug), Enemy grabs (P2-grab), Bruiser (P3-bruiser), Boss (P3-boss). Group "Rear warning" is P1-hud's, group "Debug" gains `ai_frozen`. Every tunable the plan names is declared; a package appends only inside its own subgroup. `tuning/default.json` carries `merge=keep-ours` and is regenerated after every merge.

**Placeholder poses and animation dispatch**: `MovePoses.windup_hold_at` (default -1) and `windup_hold` (a delta) key a second wind-up key at `windup_hold_at x startup` (the held telegraph; enemy moves use `windup_at` 0.4-0.6 and a hold near 0.8), mirrored by `mirrored()`; `PlaceholderAnims.windup_hold_time()` and `build_move_with(poses, move, scale)` are its seams. `PlaceholderPoses._recipe()` ends in `PlaceholderEnemyPoses.recipe(id, g, wg)` (`game/actors/poses/`), which tries `EnemyPosesPunk`, `EnemyPosesKicker`, `EnemyPosesThug`, `GrabPoses`, `EnemyPosesBruiser`, `EnemyPosesBoss`, each with `MOVE_IDS` and `recipe(id, g, wg)`; `HumanoidRig.has_anim()` asks `has_recipe()`. The shared helpers (`held()`, `tremble()`, `stance_named()`) live in the dispatcher. New named animations: `taunt` (`EnemyNamedPoses`), `brace` (`EnemyPosesBruiser`), `run_forward` (looping, `EnemyPosesBoss`), `hold_collar`, `held_collar` and `held_lift` (looping, `GrabPoses`), placeholders that hold the stance until their packages author them. `tools/gen_moves.gd` ends `_build_moves()` with `_build_enemy_moves()`, which calls one stub per archetype and a helper `_enemy(move, reach_m, recoil_frames, unblockable := false)`.

**Tests and tools**: `tests/enemy_moves/<archetype>_table.gd` (design-fixed rows and attack chains; empty until each package fills its own), `tests/enemy_move_checks.gd` (clip timing, ragdoll joint limits, measured reach, first-person telegraph), `tests/fixtures/stub_enemy.gd`/`.tscn` (an `EnemyActor` that stands still: `want_token`, `attack`, `force_attack()`, optional `attack_on_token`) and `tests/fixtures/stub_director.gd` (an `EnemyDirector` whose holders, slots and waiting lists a test sets, recording `release_token()` and `may_start_windup()` calls), `tests/test_bot_scripts_compile.gd` (every script under `tests/bot` compiles), `tools/capture_bot.sh <bot scene> <out dir> ["<act filter>"] [stills fps]` (records with Movie Maker from whichever worktree it is run in, behind a machine-wide lock of two slots).

### Enemy base and the Street Punk

`EnemyActor` (`game/enemies/enemy_actor.gd`) is the brain every AI enemy shares; an archetype is its data (`StreetPunk`, `game/enemies/punk/street_punk.gd`, is the first). The brain runs in `_tick_control()` and only in `ActorState.FREE`, plus turning during a WINDUP; every other state is `CombatActor`'s. `ai_state` is an overlay, never written into `ActorState`.

**Life of an enemy** (each arrow is a sim-frame decision; every number is a Tuning value, groups Wind-up, Posture and Navigation):
- `enter_from(spawn, lane_point)`: ENTER walks to the lane point (a point that is not finite skips it), then APPROACH walks to `posture_slot` (the director's), or, with no slot, to the nearest spot of the posture ring on the line from the player. APPROACH and RECOVER do not walk the goal itself but `_posture_target()`: the goal (a detour, the slot, the nearest ring spot) or, inside `posture_ring_min_m`, or with the goal more than `posture_orbit_gap_deg` round the ring and the chord to it cutting the ring's near edge, a point on the ring (`_orbit_toward()`: a back-out while inside the ring, then legs of `posture_orbit_lead_deg` along it, each point checked against the navmesh), facing the player once within the ring's far edge. So no archetype walks through the player to a far-side slot (final review, crowd-02; it was the Kicker's way alone).
- POSTURE on the ring (`posture_ring_min_m`..`max`), facing the player: strafe for `posture_strafe_switch_s` +- jitter at `posture_circle_speed_mps` (the archetype's `_circle_mult()` scales it) along the tangent, then stand for `posture_pause_s`, in which it taunts with `posture_taunt_chance` (the `taunt` clip, about 1.1 s, open-handed, never a raised fist). A strafe stays within `posture_wander_m` of the slot and keeps the radius inside the ring (it backs out when the player walks in); past the leash it goes back to APPROACH (a taunt in progress plays out before the stray rule acts). `wants_token()` is true only here (not mid-taunt, cooldown over, not `ai_frozen`, no `GameInput.menu_open`).
- With a token: ENGAGE. It stops strafing and squares up for `commit_pause_frames` ("I'm next"). **It then waits where it stands, on the ring, until its go is near** (P1-engage; before it, a holder stepped in at once and stood at the player's nose for the director's go, 0.8-2.6 s with rear hits landing, and a holder was at the nose a third of the time): every tick it asks `EnemyDirector.windup_eta_s(self)` (how long until `may_start_windup()` would say yes) and walks in only when that is no more than the time the rest of the walk takes (`distance / engage_speed_mps`) plus `engage_go_lead_s`, so it arrives as the go does; otherwise it stands squared up, facing the player, with its token (the wait is not a failed chase: `engage_timeout_s` counts only the frames it spends walking, and the director does not run its token timeout down while the go is held back). A go that slips away mid-walk (a hit lands on the player) stops it where it is, short of the nose. The walk steps in at `engage_speed_mps` to `attack_range_m() - engage_margin_m` (the opener's `reach_m`) and aims `engage_aim_offset_m` to one side of the player's origin (a side per enemy, from its name): the avoidance sees the player as a circle, and a wish pointing exactly at its centre is slowed to the gap over the avoidance horizon (0.4 m/s at about 1.05 m), so a lone Punk exactly on the player's axis never got within range and timed out of ENGAGE; a hand's width to one side the tie is broken. It gives the token back after `engage_timeout_s` of walking without getting there (or at once when the player is down, flying, held or KO'd), then, at its range, asks `may_start_windup()` (still the one gate, readability metric R2) and starts the attack. (`engage_margin_m` is 0.05: measured over six-Punk fights, a Punk standing 5 cm further out covers half the lens a third as often as at 0.1, and still lands 95-97% of its openers on a standing player; 0 landed only 69%.) `last_strike_eta_s()` (read by the director) is how long until the last strike of the attack it is throwing begins, through the chain; 0 when none is under way, its last strike has begun or it whiffed.
- WINDUP is the attack's opener started with `start_move(windup_move(move, heavy), 1, &"root", true)`: the startup is the held telegraph. It keeps turning toward the player for the first `windup_track_share` of it at `windup_track_deg_per_s`, then the facing is locked, so a late sidestep or dodge makes it miss. Nothing slides through it (the velocity is zeroed at the start; a travelling move steps in with `set_lunge()` from `_on_move_frame()` on the last startup frame (the body starts moving on the first active frame, so all of `free_lunge` is covered while the strike is active), never over the startup).
- ATTACK, then the next link of the chain, only if the last hit landed or was blocked (`_move_landed_hit`; a link starts with no wind-up of its own, `attack_started` with chain step 2 and timing `&"chain"`, no second `windup_started`). A whiffed opener ends the attack. A blocked move with recoil frames is cut short by `react_to_block()` and ends it too.
- RECOVER: the token is released and the attack cooldown (the archetype's `_cooldown_tunable()`) starts when it begins; it holds `recover_hold_s`, then walks backward to its slot (facing the player) and POSTUREs again. The cooldown runs through the hold and the walk back, so below about 4 s it is spent before the Punk is back in POSTURE and never binds (the cadence is then the director's gap, hit grace and cap): the Punk's `punk_attack_cooldown_s` is 5 s, locked at the M3 slice gate.
- REACT is any other `ActorState` (hitstun, stagger, launched, downed, get-up, grabbed, block); the token is released the moment it is hit (`_on_hit_received()`), grabbed (`_on_grabbed()`), its attack is blocked (`_on_move_interrupted()`), it dies, or it leaves the tree with one (`_exit_tree()`). A hit during an attempt also starts the cooldown. When it is FREE again it goes to APPROACH (an enemy whose `_wakes_in_place()` is true, the boss, and that is within the ring's far edge takes up POSTURE where it stands for `posture_resume_s`, ignoring its slot, and is served first by the director: before this the boss walked back to its slot after every knockdown and never attacked). KO: `health <= 0` (`ai_state` KO at the killing blow); once `is_gone()` `_tick_ko()` removes it from `ai_enemy`, `enemy`, `boss` and `actor`, then `queue_free()`s.
- `Tuning.data.ai_frozen`, an open menu, or no player: it stands, faces the player and asks for nothing; `force_attack(index)` still starts that attack at the next control tick (a scripted act's way of placing an attacker).
- Posture tunables added at the final review: `posture_orbit_lead_deg` (40; it replaced the Kicker's `kicker_orbit_lead_deg`), `posture_orbit_gap_deg` (60) and `posture_resume_s` (3.0).

**Wind-up** (`EnemyActor.windup_move(move, heavy)`): a copy of `move` with `startup = max(floor, round(startup x windup_mult))`, the floor `windup_min_frames_heavy` (30) or `_grunt` (18); cached in a static dictionary per move id, multiplier and floor and cleared when `windup_mult`, a floor or any move (F1) changes. A move whose scaled startup is its own is returned as it is. `hit.move` is the copy: compare moves by id.

**Archetype hooks** (override in the archetype's own class; the control tick is never replaced: the Bruiser and the boss wrap `_tick_control()` and call `super`): `_build_attacks() -> Array[EnemyAttack]`, `_health_tunable()`, `_speed_tunable()`, `_cooldown_tunable()`, `_circle_mult()`, `_choose_attack()` (the default rolls each attack's chance), `_wakes_in_place()` (false; the boss's `true` makes it take up POSTURE where it stands after a reaction within the posture ring's far edge, and the director serves it first, `has_token_priority()`), `_configure_rig()`; the exports `archetype`, `heavy`, `prefers_blind_side`, `voice_pitch_mult` and the armor flags below. An archetype that overrides `_ready()`, `_on_hit_received()`, `_on_move_interrupted()`, `_on_grabbed()`, `_on_move_frame()`, `_on_move_finished()`, `_tick_ko()` or `_apply_armor()` must call `super()`: the token, the chain and the cleanup live there. Beyond the hooks, some archetypes override private members, each logged in M3-calls and held by a test: the boss's `_face()` (turn rate) and `react_to_block()` (the parry's recoil), the Bruiser's `_stop()` and `_animate_legs()` (the brace clip) and `_pin_spot()`, the Kicker's `_pick_strafe_dir()`, the Pipe Thug's `_attacks`/`_next_attack`. `EnemyAttack` (`game/enemies/enemy_attack.gd`): `moves` (the chain, loaded from `game/combat/moves/<id>.tres`), `kind` (`&"strike"`; `&"grab"` is P2-grab's seam in `_start_attack()`), `weight_property` (a TuningData name holding this attack's chance 0..1; attacks with none share the rest) and `heavy`; `EnemyAttack.of(label, ids, weight_property, heavy, kind)`.

**Armor** (data on the actor, used by the Bruiser and the boss): `armor_light` absorbs a LIGHT hit (`hit.armor_absorbed`, reaction NONE, no knockback: the damage lands, the wind-up and its warning run on), `armor_heavy_to_stagger` turns a HEAVY hit into a STAGGER and `armor_finisher_to_stagger` a FINISHER into one. A move with `breaks_armor` (the haymaker) and a killing hit pass through untouched, and so does any hit from the player who holds it (`_apply_armor()` returns early while the victim is GRABBED and the attacker is its holder: the knee jolts a held Bruiser, and the throw throws it instead of staggering it pinned to a free player; final review, code-01). Grunts leave all three off, so a light hit interrupts their wind-up. `_on_armor_absorbed()` does nothing (the contact flash is the sign; there is no additive pose layer).

**Moving**: a `NavigationAgent3D` child with avoidance, configured from the Navigation tunables (re-applied on `Tuning.changed`). The latched pattern: each tick the desired velocity goes to the agent and last tick's safe velocity (`velocity_computed` only stores it) becomes `sim_velocity`; `_integrate()` moves the body, so hitstop and slow-mo apply. Two facts measured here: the agent passes a velocity through its avoidance only while it has somewhere to go (a `target_position` that is not reached), so every move aims it (the path target for a walk, a point one second ahead for a strafe), and the first run of a process starts the avoidance one tick earlier than later runs (a determinism test needs a warm-up run). The agent also keeps its own velocity from the last walk, and setting the wish (`velocity`) does not touch it, so after a stop or a reaction its answers went on along the old course (a Punk that had attacked walked back past the player's shoulder at 4-6 m/s). `_reset_avoidance(velocity)` clears it with `set_velocity_forced()` and the latched answer with it: at every `_stop()`, `_stop_reporting()`, at RECOVER's start and at every entry into APPROACH (from ENGAGE, a reaction), and `_report_velocity()` passes the body's real velocity the same way, so the neighbours see what it really does. A net in `_apply_velocity()` keeps only the sideways part of a latched answer that is against this tick's wish (a squeezed body slides out; a zero held one at the player's nose for up to 1.5 s) and limits its length to the wish's (the avoidance asks for up to `enemy_max_speed_mps` when two bodies overlap). With no navmesh on the map it steers straight at the target (the training room). Distances are flat (the navmesh floats above the floor). A walk that gets no nearer by `enemy_slot_reach_m` for `enemy_stuck_s`, or whose body has not moved a slot reach in that time (the wedge: a reslot or a new way-round point restarts the first count, so a Bruiser pinned at the player's nose never ran it out), is a stall: it calls `report_stalled(enemy)` on the director if that has the method and tries a spot `enemy_detour_deg` around the player for `enemy_detour_s`. The detour alternates sides on a repeat stall and skips a side with no navmesh under it. In every non-FREE state it submits its real velocity so neighbours steer around it. The first control tick adds a (skeleton) director if the tree has none (an F4 spawn in the training room).

**The Street Punk**: 30 HP (`punk_health`), no armor, the grunt floor; a deep teal jacket with yellow cap and shoes (not red: SPEC §10 keeps red for blood, the rear warning and the finisher's accents). Two attacks, picked by `punk_shove_chance`; cooldown `punk_attack_cooldown_s`, speed `punk_speed_mult`. Moves (`tools/gen_moves.gd` `_punk_moves()`, rows in `tests/enemy_moves/punk_table.gd`, recipes in `EnemyPosesPunk`):

| Move | Frames S/A/R | Damage | Reaction | Hitbox | `reach_m` | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `punk_jab` | 22/3/14 | 6 LIGHT | FLINCH | RightFistPoint 0.14 | 1.05 | the opener of the two-hit punch: the right fist cocked by the ear, held; blocked recoil 20 |
| `punk_cross` | 8/3/16 | 6 LIGHT | FLINCH | LeftFistPoint 0.14 | 1.21 | the link: no wind-up of its own, steps in 0.15 m over its active frames (`free_lunge`), so it reaches a player the jab pushed back; recoil 14 |
| `punk_shove` | 20/3/18 | 6 LIGHT | STAGGER | both FistPoints 0.16 | 0.97 | both hands drawn to the chest, palms forward, torso rocked back, held; knockback 3.5 m/s; recoil 14 |

The wind-up of each telegraph is keyed at 50% of the startup and held (`windup_hold_at` 0.8) with a small tremble. Judged from the defender's lens (sheets from `tests/bot/punk_bot.gd`): at 1 m the cocked fist is beside the head in the top corner and the cap and face stay in view; at 2.5 m the arm drawn up and back and the turned shoulders read against the guard; the shove's two raised palms are plainly not a punch. `tests/test_enemy_moves_punk.gd` holds the head visible through the telegraph and the striking hand in view at the held key, at 1.0 and 2.5 m.

**Tests and tools**: `tests/test_enemy_actor.gd` (the whole life on a flat floor and in a small code-built arena with a wall and a navmesh; wind-up lengths, tracking and lock, no slide, the chain, the token on every exit path, armor, death and vanishing, menus, `ai_frozen`, determinism), `tests/test_enemy_moves_punk.gd`, `tests/bot/punk_bot.gd` (extends the combo bot, the training room; log lines `AI f<frame> <name> <old>-><new>` and `WU+`/`WU-`): `tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/punk_bot.tscn captures/<dir> "Punk wind-up at 1 m" 12`. **The engage meter** (P1-engage, `tests/bot/engage_meter.gd`, read-only test tooling): per token hold, the frames in ENGAGE within 1.3 m of the player (the nose), standing still there, and squared up beyond it; and the lens samples over half covered, by what the most covering enemy was doing. `tests/bot/acts/engage_acts.gd` runs the slice's six-Punk acts again with it on ("Engage: observed", "passive", "fight", "waves", "blind spot"; only when the act filter names "engage") and prints a `METER` line next to the probe's summary; `tests/test_slice_crowd.gd` asserts it.

### Director

`game/enemies/enemy_director.gd` (`EnemyDirector`, group `"enemy_director"`) implements the contract's API: tokens with the cap, the blind-spot roll, the wind-up gap and the posture slots. It is not an actor. Its `_physics_process` steps on `delta * TimeController.sim_scale()` (hitstop does not stop it, slow-mo slows it), its randomness is one `RandomNumberGenerator` seeded from `rng_seed` (setting the export reseeds), and it never reads a camera: the player is found by group `"player"` and read through `PlayerCharacter`'s look API (`is_outside_view()`, `look_yaw`) and CombatActor's state. It writes only through the enemies' hooks `grant_token()`, `revoke_token()`, `set_slot()` and `set_flanking()`. Only the director's own script runs the loop: a subclass with its own script (`tests/fixtures/stub_director.gd`) decides nothing, so a test can set that fixture's lists without the real loop undoing them.

**Each tick**, in this order:
1. **Discover.** The members of `"ai_enemy"` with `health > 0`, not `is_gone()` and not queued for deletion. Freed enemies are dropped from every book the director keeps.
2. **Prune the holders.** A holder that is dead, gone, freed, no longer `has_token`, or reacting (an actor state other than FREE with no move running: hitstun, stagger, launched, downed...) loses its token. Every exit path of an attack therefore ends here at the latest, even if the enemy forgot to call `release_token()`.
3. **Track the waiting.** `wants_token()` is asked of every member without a token; `debug_waiting()` is those, longest waiting first (ties in tree order).
4. **Hold.** While `GameInput.menu_open` (F1 or F4), `Tuning.ai_frozen`, or the player is **closed**, every token whose attack has not started is taken back. Closed means: no player, `health <= 0`, KO, downed, flying (LAUNCHED or THROWN), GRABBED, or inside `director_revive_grace_s` after `CombatEvents.revived` for the player. Otherwise only a token held past `director_token_timeout_s` without starting its attack is taken back. An attack that has started (WINDUP, ATTACK or GRABBING, any running move, or the player in the enemy's grip) always keeps its token: that is what keeps R1 honest, the cap counting attackers, not wishes.
5. **Slots** (below), then
6. **Grant.** Unless held, closed or the player `is_invulnerable()` (a dodge or a get-up withholds new tokens but takes none back): while fewer than `attacker_cap` enemies are attacking (holders, plus any enemy already in an attack without a token, such as a bot's forced one; cap 0 = unlimited), pick the next: a waiter that `has_token_priority()` (the boss waking in place) first, with no blind-spot roll drawn; else, if any waiter is outside the player's view (`is_outside_view(position, rear_warning_angle_deg)`) and `rng.randf() < blind_spot_priority`, the longest-waiting blind one goes (`grant_token(true)`, shown "(blind)" in F3); else the longest waiting of all (`grant_token(false)`). Priority 1.0 therefore always jumps the queue and 0 never does (readability metric R9, tested at 90% or better; in fact 100%).

**Wind-up pacing** (`may_start_windup(enemy) -> bool`): an enemy with a token calls it every tick right before winding up. It answers true, and records the start, only when the enemy is a holder, the fight is not held, the player is not closed, and `director_min_windup_gap_s` of sim time has passed since the last wind-up start of **any** enemy; a `CombatEvents.windup_started` of an `EnemyActor` that never asked (`force_attack()`) is recorded too. So no two wind-ups start together (readability metric R2, hard) however the grants fall: a volley of three tokens becomes three wind-ups one after the other, the ones waiting standing squared up on the ring (P1-engage) until their go is near. The gap compares with a thousandth of a second of slack, so 0.35 s is exactly 21 ticks at 60 Hz. Grants themselves are not paced.

**When the go comes** (`windup_eta_s(enemy) -> float`, P1-engage): how many sim seconds until `may_start_windup(enemy)` would say yes if nothing else happened; 0 = now, `INF` = not while the fight is held (a menu, `ai_frozen`) or the player cannot be hit, or when `enemy` holds no token. **Read-only**: it records no wind-up and changes nothing, so asking every tick is free. A holder (`EnemyActor._tick_engage()`) uses it to wait squared up on the ring and step in only when its go is about to come, instead of standing at the player's nose through the pacing gap, a flinch and the hit grace; `may_start_windup()` stays the one gate at the wind-up itself, so R2 is as guaranteed as before. It is the largest of:
- the wind-up gap still to run (`director_min_windup_gap_s` from the last wind-up start of any enemy);
- the hit grace still to run (`director_hit_grace_s` after the last unblocked hit on the player), and the rest of the player's hitstun or stagger (`_state_length - state_frame`), and his remaining invulnerability (a dodge or a get-up: an enemy waits those out too);
- **strikes already on their way**: for every other live enemy, `last_strike_eta_s()` (the time until the last strike of its attack, through the chain, begins; 0 when none is under way, its last strike has begun or it whiffed) plus the hit grace, because that strike will land and start the grace. Before this a holder walked in while another attack was winding up, got to the nose a frame before the first hit landed and stood there through the jab's flinch, the cross's flinch and the grace after it (a second or more, with a Punk at the player's nose);
- **the line**: only the head of the line, the first holder (in the order the tokens were granted: blind-spot priority acts at the grant, so a blind waiter granted with others is ahead of them) whose attack has not started, has a finite answer; the others get `INF` and wait on the ring, and their turn comes with the head's wind-up (the strike above). One holder at a time walks in, and the one that waited longest does not lose the race to one granted later (a first version let every holder whose go was near walk in, and the one whose tick came last in the tree starved for 12 s).

A director that decides nothing (a test double with its own script, `tests/fixtures/stub_director.gd`) answers 0: the enemy steps in at once, as it did before. A holder whose go is held back this way is waiting for a reason, not sitting on its token, so `director_token_timeout_s` does not run down for it (`_stretch_waits()`: the time it waits with `windup_eta_s() > 0` is added back to its grant clock); a holder whose go is open and does not start still times out. The tokens of a held fight or a closed player (`INF`) are taken back anyway.

**Hit grace** (P1-int; the flinch-lock guard): `may_start_windup()` also says no while the player is in HITSTUN or STAGGER and for `director_hit_grace_s` (0.5 s) after a hit that was not blocked landed on him (the director listens to `CombatEvents.hit_landed`). A flinch is 17 frames and wind-ups start 21 frames apart, so on the gap alone the next hit landed 4 frames after the player recovered: six Punks round a player who does nothing chained hits for up to 4.8 s. With the grace the longest chain of reactions is 1.15 s (a wind-up already running still lands: at most two of them and their chain links), with about the same number of hits. A blocked hit starts no grace.

**Slots (M3's even spread; since Milestone 5 this is only what the Debug switch `director_even_spread` brings back; the default is "Attack arcs (ARC)" under "Level blockout (Milestone 5)" below)** (`slot_for()`, `debug_slot_of()`, the enemy's `set_slot(pos, kind)`): every live enemy has a posture slot, ENTER, RECOVER and token holders included: a holder keeps its slot (it is where it backs off to), so an attack neither moves the others nor loses its enemy its place. The slots are reassigned when the set of enemies changes (a spawn, a death), when the player has moved more than `director_reslot_move_m` since the last assignment, every `director_reslot_s`, or when an enemy calls `report_stalled(enemy)`. Reassigning:
- The n enemies that are not Kickers get n angles `360 / n` apart around the player. The arrangement is turned the least it can be from where the enemies *sit*: an enemy that has a seat from last time sits there (its claimed angle, wherever it stands now), a new one sits where it stands. So an attacker at the player's nose, or a crowd that walks past each other, does not reshuffle the ring, and a settled crowd keeps its slots (tested). The turn is the L1 optimum over the cyclic assignments in angular order, anchored on each enemy in turn; an enemy with no claim (a newcomer) weighs `director_newcomer_weight` (0.1) against one with a claim in that cost, so a spawn never takes the seat of an enemy already on the ring (final review: the call-in's first Punk gave the boss the seat across the player). Each enemy then takes the slot closest to its seat, greedily, seated enemies choosing before newcomers, plus its own jitter of `director_spread_jitter_deg` and its own radius between `posture_ring_min_m` and `posture_ring_max_m`, both from a hash of its name (stable across runs).
- A `prefers_blind_side` enemy (the Kicker) is no part of the arrangement: it takes a seat of its own as near directly behind `look_yaw` as it can be (searching outward in 5 degree steps, on the side it sat on last time), at least `director_flank_gap_deg` (20) from every other seat; the others' seats never move for it, so it follows the player's turning at the next reassignment without turning the whole ring. The cost: its seat can be up to about twice that gap off directly behind when another seat is near it, still well outside the view (55 degrees). (Before the final review the Kicker was one of the evenly spaced seats and the ring turned round it: a fought mixed crowd had 0.4-0.8 of its 5.8 enemies posturing, against 1.6-2.0 now.)
- With a synced navmesh (`map_get_iteration_id > 0` and a region on the map) each slot is snapped onto it. A slot that snaps further than `director_slot_snap_tolerance_m` away (inside a crate, past a wall) tries the next angle step, `director_slot_retry_deg`, to either side, and so on around the circle. Without a navmesh, or if no angle works, the slot is the raw point on the ring. No map query is made before the first sync.
- `report_stalled(enemy)` (not in the S0 skeleton; P1-ai's stall detection calls it, `enemy_stuck_s`) nudges that enemy's angle by `director_slot_retry_deg`, alternating sides and growing, until its next token.

**Read API** (presentation reads, never writes): `debug_token_holders()` (grant order), `debug_waiting()`, `debug_slot_of(enemy)`, `debug_ring()` (the ring's tunables), `attacker_cap()`. `slot_for(enemy)` is `debug_slot_of()`.

**What an enemy owes the director:** a truthful `wants_token()`; `release_token(self)` when its attack ends or is interrupted (the director's pruning is the backstop, not the plan); `windup_eta_s(self)` before it walks in, and `may_start_windup(self)` before every wind-up with a token (follow-up links of a chain do not ask); standing still, not winding up, while it has no go; a truthful `last_strike_eta_s()` while it attacks; handling `slot_for()` answering `Vector3.INF` for an enemy it has not seen yet.

**Tunables** (group "Enemies", subgroup "Director"): `director_min_windup_gap_s`, `director_hit_grace_s`, `director_reslot_s`, `director_reslot_move_m`, `director_spread_jitter_deg`, `director_slot_snap_tolerance_m`, `director_slot_retry_deg`, `director_revive_grace_s`, `director_token_timeout_s`, `director_newcomer_weight` (0.1) and `director_flank_gap_deg` (20); and the group's `attacker_cap`, `blind_spot_priority`, plus the posture ring (`posture_ring_min_m`, `posture_ring_max_m`) from P1-ai's subgroup. Tests: `tests/test_enemy_director.gd`, all against `tests/fixtures/stub_enemy.gd`.

### Crowd room, navigation and waves

Files: `game/level/crowd_room.gd`/`.tscn` (`CrowdRoom`), `room_kit.gd` (`RoomKit`), `nav_baker.gd` (`NavBaker`), `spawn_room_check.gd` (`SpawnRoomCheck`), `encounter_spawner.gd` (`EncounterSpawner`), `encounter_table.gd` (`EncounterTable`) with `encounters/crowd_waves.tres`, the F4 menu `game/ui/cheat_menu.gd`, and the bot `tests/bot/crowd_bot.gd` with `tests/bot/acts/room_acts.gd`. Tests: `test_crowd_room.gd`, `test_encounter_spawner.gd`, `test_cheat_spawns.gd` (fixture `tests/fixtures/spawn_probe_enemy`, a `stub_enemy` that records `enter_from()`).

**The room** (`CrowdRoom`, 30 x 12 m, street axis X; every number an export): a lane floor `x -15..15`, `z -6..6` with no margin past the walls (a margin is a navmesh island); on -Z a tall front of four blocks with three doorway recesses (2 m wide, 1.5 m deep, at x -9, 0, 9; dark back wall and a lintel, so they read as openings), windows drawn on the blocks (a turn must show against something); an alley through each end wall (3 m wide, 5 m long, dark walls); on +Z a 0.6 m curb with one side alley (gap 2.5 m, 3 x 5 m, 2.4 m dark fences) and an **invisible barrier above the curb** (a jump would clear 0.6 m and fall out of the world; a test proves it); a parked car (-5, 3.5), a crate pair and a dumpster, placed so each doorway mouth keeps 1 m beside it; a drawn-only ground and skyline beyond the curb. The player starts at the origin facing +X, so the west alley and two doorways are behind or beside him. All solid geometry hangs under the `Geometry` node, on the world layer.

**`_ready()` order**: environment, geometry, **bake**, player (`RoomKit.spawn_player`: `auto_revive`, a `NavigationObstacle3D` child with `player_avoid_radius_m`, live), `EnemyDirector` (seeded), `EncounterSpawner` (with `spawn_point_list()`), then the presentation layer if `with_presentation` (`RoomKit.build_presentation`: camera director, HUD, debug draw if none, `TuningOverlay`, VFX, audio, rumble, all direct children). Room contract for bots and tests: `player`, `player_start`, `player_yaw_deg`, `with_presentation`, `waves_enabled_on_start` (false turns the `waves_enabled` tunable off), `rng_seed`, `spawner`, `director`, `enemies()`, plus `nav_region`, `nav_bake_usec`, `spawn_point_list()`.

**Navigation** (`NavBaker.bake(geometry_root, settings) -> NavigationRegion3D`; the recipe is the godot-navigation codemap's section 0): parse only static colliders on the world layer (never an actor, the pipe, a rig mesh or paint), bake synchronously, drop roof and curb polygons (`drop_roof_polygons(navmesh, max_floor_y)`), set the mesh on the region **before** it enters the tree. Cell size 0.25 and cell height 0.1 (the project's `[navigation]`), agent radius 0.5, height 1.8, climb 0.2: room exports. The bake takes about 3 ms; the map's first sync is physics frame 2, so queries are legal by frame 3 (`NavBaker.map_ready(world)`; a query before that prints an ERROR). The floor polygons sit 0.2 m above the floor: compare flat (XZ) distances. 47 polygons, none above 0.2 m.

**The player's obstacle** (`RoomKit.PlayerObstacle`, radius `player_avoid_radius_m`, 0.3) keeps avoidance from walking enemies through him. Avoidance holds an enemy `player_avoid_radius_m + enemy_avoid_radius_m` off his origin, so that sum must stay below the shortest strike an enemy closes to (`reach_m - engage_margin_m`; a Punk's shove: 0.87 m): above it the enemy hovers just short of range, times out of ENGAGE and never attacks (a test guards it).

**Spawn points** (`spawner.spawn_points: Array[Dictionary]`, each `{position, yaw, tag, lane_point}`): a doorway recess's back (`&"doorway"`, three), an alley's far end (`&"alley"`, three: west, east, the side alley) and the strip between the car and the curb (`&"vehicle"`: seen from the lane, the enemy comes out from behind the car). `position` is on the floor and on the navmesh (inset by the agent radius plus `spawn_inset_m`, a test snaps every one within 0.3 m and finds a path to the lane); `yaw` faces the lane point; `lane_point` is where it walks first (`lane_entry_m` in from the lane's edge, past the car's end for the vehicle).

**`EncounterSpawner`** (group `encounter_spawner`; a child of the room, enemies are added to its parent, or `host`):
- `spawn_enemy(id, at, yaw, from_wave := false, lane_point := Vector3.INF) -> EnemyActor`: scene from `scene_override[id]` or `EnemyCatalog.scene(id)`; named `"<Archetype><n>"` (the enemy's `archetype`, else the id; n counted per archetype by this spawner), display name "Punk 3"; positioned before it enters the tree; then `enter_from(at, lane_point)` (`lane_point` defaults to `at`: it is already there). Null for an id without a scene or a scene that is not an `EnemyActor` (the training dummy). `static make_enemy(scene, id, serials)` is the same without placing it (the F4 menu uses it in a room with no spawner).
- `spawn_adds(id, count, near) -> Array[EnemyActor]`: the boss's call-in. The free spawn points nearest `near` (at least `spawn_min_player_distance_m` from the player, with room, no two at one point); the adds join the running wave.
- `alive_count()`: members of `"ai_enemy"` with `health > 0`. `set_waves_enabled(on)` writes the tunable `waves_enabled` (one switch for F1, F4 and bots). `static remove_enemies(tree, only_ai := false) -> int`: every AI enemy (and the dummies unless `only_ai`) leaves its groups, then is freed.
- `start_at_wave(number)`: make wave `number` (1 = the first; boss waves count) the next to start, after a full `wave_gap_s`; it drops the wave in progress (what is queued never spawns, what spawned stays as ordinary enemies); `start_at_wave(1)` starts the sequence over, a number under 1 is 1. For bots and tests that need a later wave without playing the earlier ones; the crowd bot calls `start_at_wave(1)` in every act's reset.
- `valid_point_indices(body) -> Array[int]`, `is_behind(point)`, `resolve_id(id)`, `waves_started()`, `wave_running()`, signals `wave_started(number, ids)` and `wave_cleared(number)`.

**Waves** (Tuning "Waves"; the table `EncounterTable.waves`, `wave_at(started, boss_every)`): wave 1 starts `wave_gap_s` after the room is ready, each later one `wave_gap_s` after every enemy of the one before has `health <= 0` (a KO'd body still lying there does not hold it). One enemy spawns every `wave_spawn_interval_s`; while `alive_count()` is `wave_max_alive` it waits (F4 spawns count toward it but are never refused by it). The table repeats forever; after every `boss_wave_every` regular waves comes the table's `boss_wave` (`["boss"]`) on its own. An id with no scene is replaced by the Punk (then the wave still plays before the archetypes exist). Each spawn chooses its point when it happens: points nearer than `spawn_min_player_distance_m` to the player, or with no room (`SpawnRoomCheck.has_room`: no world, body, ghost, lying ragdoll or blinking-in actor in the capsule), are skipped; a seeded roll decides "from outside the view" (`player.is_outside_view(point, rear_warning_angle_deg)`, the director's blind spot) so each wave hits `round(size x wave_behind_share)` such spawns exactly, in a random order; the point used last is not used twice in a row; if no point is valid the spawn waits one interval. **Waves hold while a menu is open** (`GameInput.menu_open`, F1 or F4): no timer runs and nothing spawns, and an attack already winding up finishes. Timers run in sim time (slow-mo and F4's 0.25x slow them).

**F4** (`CheatMenu`): a list of `EnemyCatalog.available()` and "Spawn at crosshair" (`spawn_selected()`, `spawn_enemy(id)`; `spawn_point(id)` sizes "clear" by that archetype's body); an AI enemy goes through the room's spawner when there is one (its naming and `scene_override`), else the menu makes it itself (`scene_override` seam for tests; the enemy base creates a director lazily, P1-ai); it starts in ENTER from where it stands. "Remove spawned enemies" (`remove_spawned_dummies()`, now any actor the menu spawned) and "Remove all enemies" (`remove_all_enemies()`: the dummies too; groups first, so no group scan in the same frame finds one); "Waves" (`set_waves(on)`, disabled in a room with no spawner); "Freeze AI" (`set_ai_frozen(on)`: `ai_frozen`). `spawned` is `Array[CombatActor]`.

**`crowd_bot`** (`tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn`; `tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn captures/<key> "<act filter>" 6`): the combo bot's harness in the crowd room. It reads every `tests/bot/acts/*_acts.gd` in file-name order (`static func acts(bot: Node) -> Array[Dictionary]` of `{title, cam, fn}`; one file per package). Helpers for act files: `spawn(id, rel: Vector2, face_player := true)` (x right, y ahead of the player; a stand-in that only stands still when the archetype is not merged, `ensure_scene(id)`), `clear_enemies()`, `set_waves(on)`, `freeze_ai(on)`, `log_line(text)`, and the combo bot's `_press`, `_ticks`, `_wait_until`, `_turn_by`, `_look_to`, `_hud`. Waves are off at the start of every act. Log lines, each with its physics frame: `W+`/`W-` (the HUD's rear warning, found by its `shown`/`hidden` signals), `AI f<n> <name> <old>-><new>`, `TOK+`/`TOK-`, `WU+`/`WU-`. `room_acts.gd`: "Room tour" (a full turn, then the lane, the doorway, both alley mouths, the car and the curb), "Room tour, free fly, F3", "Spawn points" (one enemy at every point, from above) and "F4 menu".

### Rear warning and the F3 AI overlay

**`RearWarning`** (`game/ui/rear_warning.gd`, `class_name RearWarning extends Control`): a full-rect, mouse-ignoring child of the HUD (`GameHud.rear_warning`), stepped by `GameHud.advance()` with `game_delta * TimeController.sim_scale()` (a pause holds its pulses, slow-mo and F4's 0.25x stretch them). It subscribes to `CombatEvents` itself (`_enter_tree`/`_exit_tree`), reads the player (group `"player"`) through its look (`PlayerCharacter.look_bearing_deg()`, never a camera) and writes nothing.
- **Trigger**, on `windup_started(attacker, move)` (so on the wind-up's first frame): an attacker not on team 0, the player alive (unless `rear_warning_hide_in_ko` is off), then `ViewGeometry.warning_corner(bearing, flat distance, reach, rear_warning_angle_deg, rear_warning_behind_deg, rear_warning_range_margin_m)` with `reach = attacker.warning_reach_m(move)` if the attacker has that method, else `move.reach_m`. `&"left"` (bearing positive, behind-left) lights the top-left corner, `&"right"` the top-right, `&"both"` (within `rear_warning_behind_deg` of directly behind) both. The side is fixed at the start; `rear_warning_follow_turn` re-runs the rule every frame instead and also tracks a wind-up that started in view, so the warning goes while the player faces the attacker and returns if they turn away.
- **End**, on the first of: `hit_landed` with that attacker (blocked too), `attack_whiffed`, `windup_ended(attacker, true)`, `grabbed(attacker, _)`, `ko`, `vanished`; and, polled in `advance()`, the attacker freed, out of WINDUP and ATTACK, or running another move (compared by `move.id`, so a `windup_move()` duplicate counts). Never on `windup_ended(attacker, false)`, the strike coming out: the warning holds until it lands. The event ends show on the frame of the event; the polled ones within one HUD frame.
- **Look**: one burst per corner (`VfxArt.texture(&"rear_warning")`, mipmapped), brightness `1 + rear_warning_brightness_step x (n - 1)` for the `n` live wind-ups on that side (a "both" one counts on both), applied to the burst's colour and alpha (`rear_warning_opacity` caps the alpha at 1); a heavy attacker (`attacker.heavy`, read duck-typed) makes the corner's burst `rear_warning_heavy_scale` times larger, at every size setting (SPEC section 8): the steady size is `min(rear_warning_base_px x rear_warning_size, edge / rear_warning_heavy_scale)`, times the heavy scale for a heavy one, where `edge` is the short side of the reserved corner (216 px at 1080p), so a heavy burst always fits it and the normal burst is held to `edge / heavy_scale` (166 px at the defaults): above a size of about 1.04 the size slider does nothing, which the plan's binding 20% x 20% corner makes inherent. It pops in `rear_warning_pulse_grow` oversized, beats `rear_warning_pulses` times of `rear_warning_pulse_s` (each dipping to `rear_warning_min_alpha` of its alpha), then holds; it beats again when `n` rises. `burst_rect()` keeps every burst inside the reserved 20% x 20% corner whatever `rear_warning_size` says; it clips steady x pulse to the corner, so a heavy burst is never smaller than a normal one's at rest or at the pop's peak (the default heavy pop, 208 x 1.25, is clipped to 216 px, against the normal pop's 200).
- **API**: `shown(side, count, heavy)` (a corner lit, or one more attacker joined it) and **`cleared(side)`** (a corner went dark; not `hidden`, the name the plan pinned, because `CanvasItem` already has a `hidden` signal that a script cannot redefine, and connecting `hidden` would connect that one); `corner_state(side) -> {active, count, heavy, alpha, brightness, size_px, scale, rect}` (`size_px` is the steady size from `_steady_size()`: the size tunable, the heavy scale for a heavy burst, inside the corner); `burst_rect(side, heavy, pulse_scale)`; `advance(delta)`; `live_count()`, `is_tracking(attacker)`. The bots print the signals as `W+`/`W-` and compare `corner_state(&"left"/&"right").active` with `ViewGeometry.warning_corner()`.
- **Audio** is the wind-up's own effort and whoosh (positional, `AudioDirector`), gated by `rear_warning_audio`; nothing else beeps.

**Boss bar**: `GameHud` shows `BossHealthBar` for a live (health above 0, not `is_gone()`) member of group `"boss"` it is not already showing, polled in `advance()`, and hides it on `vanished` of that boss or when it is freed; a boss's death keeps the bar (draining to empty) until it vanishes. `show_boss()` and `hide_boss()` still work for any actor. `EnemyHealthBars` skips group `"boss"`.

**F3 AI layer** (`CombatDebugDraw._add_ai_layer()`): drawn only while an `EnemyDirector` and `"ai_enemy"` members exist, from the director's read API and never written, in colours of its own (exports `token_color`, `slot_color`, `ring_color`, `view_color`, `behind_color`, `path_color`): a magenta ring at the feet of each token holder and a line chest to chest to the player; an orange circle at each posturing enemy's slot with a dim line to it; the posture ring (`debug_ring()`) at the player's feet; the view boundary (`rear_warning_angle_deg` off the look, both ways) and the behind sector (`rear_warning_behind_deg` either side of directly behind) as floor lines from the player; each enemy's `NavigationAgent3D` path. The AI state, slot and token text over each head is `debug_state_text()` (P1-ai).

**Voices** (`AudioDirector`): with every voice busy, `_take_voice()` takes the oldest sound that is not a live wind-up cue (a cue is protected from the start of its wind-up until `windup_ended`, either way), and only when all are cues the oldest cue, counted in `windup_voices_stolen` (the probe's R14). `voice_pitch(actor)` multiplies by the actor's `voice_pitch_mult` when it has one. `tests/test_audio_crowd.gd` measures six wind-ups plus a brawl: 36 sounds asked of 32 voices in one frame, no cue lost, so `voice_count` stays 32.

**Tests and bots**: `tests/test_rear_warning.gd` (the corners, ends, several, heavy, tunables, pulses, pause, slow-mo, KO view, follow-turn, no writes, and in the real training room real dummies behind the player and `test_warning_sweep_every_5_degrees`: 72 bearings against the rule written out by hand), `tests/test_debug_draw_ai.gd` (stub director and stub enemies), `tests/test_audio_crowd.gd`, and appended to `tests/test_ui.gd` (the burst inside the reserved corners at every size, the boss bar, no over-head bar for the boss). `tests/bot/warning_bot.gd` (training room, first person; extends `combo_bot.gd`): acts "Warn: behind-left", "behind-right", "directly behind", "edge of behind" (161 and 159 degrees), "in view", "out of range", "two on the left", "left, right and behind", "heavy", "blocked ends it", "hit out of it ends it" and "sweep" (every 15 degrees), each ending in `WARN-CHECK ok|FAIL <act> <corner> f<frame>`, with `WU+`/`WU-` and `W+`/`W-` lines; and "F3: AI layer" acts with a stub director.

### Instruments: readability probe, autopilot, warning oracle

Three plain helpers a bot or a test owns, in `tests/bot/` (test tooling: nothing under `tests/` is in the export, and none of them has a `class_name`; load them with `preload`). Each is a `Node` with an explicit API: `attach(host, player)` (adds itself under `host` when it is not in the tree yet, and finds the player by group `"player"`), a `tick()` that its own `_physics_process` calls once per frame unless `auto_tick` is off, and plain result dictionaries. They read state and `CombatEvents` and write none, except the autopilot, which drives the player's scripted `PlayerInput` like any bot. **Thresholds are acceptance criteria (Part B §B2): they live in the probe (`LIMITS`, `Oracle.MAX_*`) and in the tests, never in `TuningData`.** P1-int wires them into `crowd_bot`.

**`warning_oracle.gd`** (metric R10). At every `windup_started` of a non-player attacker it works out which corner the rule demands (`ViewGeometry.warning_corner()` from the player's `look_yaw`, the attacker's `warning_reach_m(move)` if it has one, else `move.reach_m`, and the `rear_warning_*` tunables), keeps its own model of which wind-ups are still warning (Part B §B1.3: ended by `hit_landed` with that attacker, `attack_whiffed`, `windup_ended(attacker, true)`, `grabbed(attacker, _)`, `ko`, `vanished`, or, polled, the attacker freed, out of WINDUP and ATTACK, or running another move id; never by `windup_ended(false)`; suppressed while the player's health is 0 with `rear_warning_hide_in_ko`), and every physics frame, after the simulation (`late_priority` 1000), compares it with the HUD widget: any node in the tree with `corner_state(side) -> {active, count, heavy, ...}` (found by method, so it needs no class and works the day `RearWarning` lands). Per wind-up it records the latency (frames from the start until the widget showed it) and the end lag (frames from the model's end until the widget dropped it); a burst the model does not explain is a false positive, counted once per episode. One line per wind-up, kept in `lines`, printed, and emitted as `checked(line)`: `WARN-CHECK ok|FAIL <act> <left|right|both|none> f<frame> lat=<n|-> end=<n|-> [why=not-lit|wrong-side|late|end-late]`. API: `start_act(title)`, `end_act() -> Dictionary`, `result()`, `total_result()`, `Oracle.passes(result) -> bool` (agreement 100%, no false positive, latency and end lag within 1 frame), `live_count()`. A wind-up that lived one tick or less is not required to have lit; a missing widget fails every corner wind-up and passes the negative controls.

**`autopilot.gd`** (the scripted player of the fight acts). `attach(host, player)` puts the player's input in scripted mode; it then, in this order: mashes P and K while GRABBED; dodges (B with the stick to the side away from them) when two or more wind-ups are about to land; raises the guard (B, stick centred) when one wind-up inside the guard's arc is `block_lead_frames` from striking and holds it until the attack is over; turns to a wind-up that is out of the view (the warning's corner) and quick-turns (TURN) when it is directly behind (both corners); otherwise faces the nearest enemy, walks up to it and chains P P P P then K K K. It reacts `reaction_frames` after a wind-up starts. Skill knobs are exports of the node (bot settings, not tunables); the rules it follows (the guard's arc, the view and behind angles, the dodge and quick-turn lengths) are read from `Tuning`. `result()` counts what it did (`punches`, `kicks`, `blocks`, `dodges`, `quick_turns`, `mashes`, ...), `decided(decision)` says what it chose each tick, `stop()` lets go of the guard and the stick, `enabled` switches it off. It turns the look itself (`look_yaw`), as the bots do. It faces the nearest enemy at all times, so a wind-up behind it is only a surprise when something nearer stands in front.

**`readability_probe.gd`** (metrics R1 to R15). `attach(host, player)` creates and owns an oracle; `start_act(title, tags := {})` / `end_act() -> {title, tags, frames, seconds, metrics, verdict}` per act (tag `{passive = true}` makes R12 apply); `end_act()` prints a one-screen summary (a row per metric: value, `ok` / `FAIL (hard)` / `miss` / `n/a`, threshold); `finish(dir := "")` ends the open act and writes `readability.json` (`{acts: [...], total: {...}}`, each with `metrics` under Part B's keys and `verdict` rows), into `dir`, else the probe's `out_dir` (a test's own folder), else `--probe-out=<dir>`, else the folder of `--write-movie`, else `user://`; `write_on_exit` does that when the node leaves the tree, for a bot that only calls `get_tree().quit()` (into `out_dir` when set). Also `current_metrics()`, `total()`, `report()`, `verdict(metrics, summary)`, and the statics `Probe.hard_ok(rows)` and `Probe.misses(rows)` (what `test_slice_crowd.gd` asserts: R1, R2, R3, R10 and R11 are hard).

| Row | Sampled |
| --- | --- |
| R1 `max_concurrent_attackers` | per frame: enemies (group `attacker_group`, default `"ai_enemy"`) in WINDUP, ATTACK or holding the player; frames over `attacker_cap()` counted too |
| R2 `min_windup_start_gap_frames` | gap between consecutive wind-up starts of different enemies; passes at `ceil(director_min_windup_gap_s x 60)` frames. A hold's follow-up (the headbutt or the slam, which starts as the grab connects with the player already held) is the second half of the grab's attack, not a start: it is skipped (P4-int; counted in `windups`, R1 and R3) |
| R3 `unwarned_hits` | a hit on the player whose attack (from `attack_started` step 1 to the next) had no wind-up of at least `windup_min_frames_grunt` / `_heavy`; exempt: a body impact, and any hit while its attacker holds the player |
| R4 `occluded_windup_share` | every `occlusion_every` (3) frames, for each wind-up, is its chest in the lens and hidden by another enemy's mesh that `FPView.is_drawn()` counts (`FPView.ray_hit`): the same meshes R8 casts at, so a rig with art counts through its view proxies (they stand in for the art, A4, and are hidden while it shows) and its skinned ArrayMesh is never ray-cast here; by majority of the wind-up's samples; counted from the act's first frame (not the engine's frame counter), so an act samples the same frames whatever ran before it |
| R5 `front_wedge_crowding`, R6 `ring_adherence`, R7 `capsule_overlap_frames` | per frame, over enemies in `AiState.POSTURE` (R5, R6) and standing ones (R7, capsule radius `body_radius`) |
| R8 `fp_enemy_coverage` | every `lens_every` (10) frames, `FPView.hits()` over the host's subtree, share of cells whose nearest mesh is an enemy's (7 ms a sample with six enemies; its own time is taken out of R15); counted from the act's first frame (not the engine's frame counter), so an act samples the same frames whatever ran before it |
| R9 `blind_grant_share` | a `has_token` that rose, against the previous frame's `wants_token()` waiting set (in view or not by `rear_warning_angle_deg`) |
| R10 `warning_*` | the oracle |
| R11 `max_knockdown_hit_damage_non_boss` | hits on the player with KNOCKDOWN, LAUNCH or THROWN, not blocked, attacker not in group `"boss"` (and the attack's chain total, informational) |
| R12 `passive_time_to_ko_s`, `max_damage_2s` | from the act's start; worst 2 s window of damage; judged only in an act tagged passive |
| R13 `stuck_events`, `waves_cleared_90s` | an ENTER or APPROACH enemy that moved under 0.3 m in 3 s; the live count falling to 0, within the act's first 90 s |
| R14 `windup_voices_stolen` | the `AudioDirector`'s `windup_voices_stolen` counter when it has one, else polled from its `_windup_of` and `_voices`; null when neither exists |
| R15 `frame_ms_p95`, `spawn_hitch_ms` | wall time between `_process` calls minus the probe's own cost; judged only windowed and not recording |

Wiring (P1-int, as built): see "Slice integration" below. `readability.json` goes where the probe's paragraph says (a recording's folder, `Engine.get_write_movie_path()`, since `OS.get_cmdline_args()` leaves `--write-movie` out, unless `--probe-out=<dir>` names one). The oracle ignores the HUD, and any wind-up that starts, while no act is open (the bot runs on 45 frames after an act and then resets the room), and `start_act()` empties its model, so a wind-up cut off by the end of an act, or begun in the gap before the next one, is nobody's false positive and is not judged under the next act's title. Between its READABILITY block and the next header the bot's log may still carry a `WARN-CHECK` line for such a wind-up; the log checker skips it (see `tools/check_m3_log.py` below).

### Slice integration (P1-int)

`tests/bot/crowd_bot.gd` owns the wiring: it creates one `Probe` and one `Pilot` (the autopilot, `enemy_group = ai_enemy`, off) after the room is up, and an act dictionary may carry `probe = true` and `tags` (`{passive = true}`). For a probed act the loop calls `probe.start_act(title, tags)` before it and `end_act()` right after (no waiting for the player to be idle), stops and disables the pilot, and logs `LOCK`, `CROWD` and `PILOT` lines; `probe.finish()` writes `readability.json` at the end. Helpers for act files: `god()`, `tune(property, value)` (put back at the next reset), `spawn_at_point(id, index)`, `surround(count, gap)` (Punks from `SURROUND_POINTS`: ahead, left, right, behind, behind left, behind right), `keep_alive(count)` (a new Punk every 45 frames while fewer are alive), `fight(ticks)` and `hold_back()` (the autopilot as a slow, defensive player: no walking up, no kicks, two punches a chain, a 1 s rest; with its defaults one P P P P kills a Punk and a six-Punk fight has one attacker at a time). `_reset()` empties the spawner's name counters, reseeds the director and puts back god mode, `ai_frozen`, changed tunables and the autopilot's knobs, so an act's numbers do not depend on the acts before it; `-- --seed=<n>` starts the names at 7n (each enemy's random choices are seeded by name) for repeated runs. `_quit()` stops every voice and waits 150 ms of real time before quitting: a headless run is far ahead of the audio clock and a sound still mixing at exit prints "ERROR: N resources still in use".

The lock meter (`LOCK`): the longest chain of the player's reactions (HITSTUN, STAGGER, LAUNCHED, DOWNED, GETUP; free gaps up to 12 frames merge) and the longest unbroken one, and the share of the act spent in them. The crowd meter (`CROWD`): mean enemies alive, posturing, committed (ENGAGE, WINDUP, ATTACK), backing off, waiting at strike range in ENGAGE, lingering within 1.3 m in RECOVER, and the widest empty arc between the standing enemies seen from the player (mean and worst, with four or more standing).

`tests/bot/acts/slice_acts.gd`: "Punk 1v1" (the bot blocks the first attack, punches the second out of its wind-up, then P P P P), "Three Punks", "Six Punks, observed" (god mode, a slow 360 degree turn, then still), "Six Punks, fight" (autopilot), "Warn L/R/behind" (`ai_frozen`, Punks at -135, +135 and 180 degrees at 1.5 m, then two on the left, then one at 50 degrees), "Six Punks, blind spot" (facing the north wall), "Six Punks, passive" (R12), "Director, F3" and "Director, F3, free fly" (the F3 overlay on), "Waves" (90 s, so R13's waves apply). `tests/test_slice_crowd.gd`: six real Punks, the real HUD, god mode, 900 frames: never more than `attacker_cap` in WINDUP or ATTACK, nobody inside 0.7 m of another after frame 240 (0.68 m: capsules touch at 0.7), nobody stuck walking in, at least one blind-spot grant; and the director's posture slots spread all the way round (the enemies themselves leave a mean widest empty arc of about 105 degrees, see the calls file); R1, R2, R3, R10 and R11 measured by the probe and none failing; a determinism check (a warm-up room, then two runs that must hash alike); and the autopilot fighting kept-alive Punks.

### Enemy grabs on the player

Game-boy's rule (M3 answers): an enemy's grab is telegraphed (a dodge, a jump or a heavy hit on an armored grabber gets out of the wind-up); once held the player mashes P and K to break free before the follow-up's strike; unbroken, the hit lands. The Bruiser's grab and headbutt and the boss's grab and slam (phase 3) are one reusable **grab attack**, `EnemyAttack.of(label, [grab, follow_up], weight, heavy, &"grab")`. It is proved here with the test grabber (`tests/fixtures/grab_test_enemy.gd`, scenes `grab_test_enemy.tscn` 2.05 m and `grab_test_boss.tscn` 2.3 m, the latter in group `"boss"`): an `EnemyActor` whose only attack is the grab, with the real AI or `force_attack()`.

**Moves** (`tools/gen_moves.gd` `_grab_moves()`, rows in `tests/enemy_moves/grab_table.gd`; all `unblockable`, no recoil, no magnetism, **no hitbox**: nothing fires hit VFX for the grab itself):

| Move | Frames S/A/R | Dmg | Tier | Reaction | `reach_m` | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bruiser_grab` | 32/6/24 | 0 | LIGHT | NONE | 1.15 | wind-up: arms spread wide, crouched forward, held; strike: both fists out at the chest, the body lunging over the planted feet |
| `bruiser_headbutt` | 60/3/24 | 15 | HEAVY | KNOCKDOWN | 1.0 | starts in the hold; its startup **is the mash window**; knockback (0, 2, 4) |
| `boss_grab` | 32/6/24 | 0 | LIGHT | NONE | 1.3 | as the Bruiser's at boss scale |
| `boss_slam` | 54/4/30 | 25 | HEAVY | THROWN | 1.05 | starts in the boss's hold; the wind-up lifts the player; knockback (0, 4.5, 6); HEAVY and THROWN, never FINISHER (A2.13); boss table `boss: true` |
