A grab's `reach_m` is where its strike pose's fists meet a standing player (the fists reach 1.05 m at 2.05 m, 1.18 m at 2.3 m, plus his chest 0.12 m: `tests/test_enemy_moves_grab.gd` measures it on the rig) and the connect range `enemy_grab_range_m` (1.05 at a 1.8 m rig) times the holder's height over 1.8 (1.20 m, 1.34 m); a follow-up's is the pin distance (below). The Bruiser's pair costs 0 + 15 = 15, the 15% rule's limit.

**`EnemyHold`** (`game/enemies/enemy_hold.gd`, `RefCounted`, one per holder, made by `EnemyActor._hold_arm()` from `_start_attack()`'s `kind == &"grab"` branch; its `on_move_frame()` is called from `_on_move_frame()`):
1. The grab winds up like any attack (`windup_move()`, the token and the director's go, the rear warning, the effort and whoosh cue). **At its strike frame** it connects, or whiffs. It connects when the player is within `enemy_grab_range_m` x the holder's rig scale (flat), within `enemy_grab_half_angle_deg` of the holder's facing, alive, and `CombatActor.can_be_grabbed()` (so a dodge, a jump, the player's own strike's active frames, the ground and a get-up are out). A whiff is announced by hand: `CombatEvents.attack_whiffed(holder, move)` (the move has no hitbox to whiff; the warning ends on it).
2. On a connect: `player.start_grabbed(holder)`, then the follow-up starts at once as the running move, `start_move(windup_move(follow_up, heavy), 1, &"root", true)`: the holder is in WINDUP and then ATTACK of the follow-up for the whole hold (a running move needs those states; it never enters GRABBING), the wind-up cue plays, the director counts it as attacking and keeps its token, and `windup_ended` fires only at the strike (or when the hold is cut short, interrupted).
3. At the follow-up's strike frame, when the victim is still held by it: `release_from_grab()` first (a GRABBED body takes a hit from its holder only as a jolt that keeps it held), then a hit built from the move (the player's `_throw_held()` template: `receive_hit()`, `TimeController.hitstop([holder, victim])`, `hit_landed`, and `thrown` for the slam). The headbutt's region is the head (contact at the eyes), the slam's the torso.
4. Ends: the victim mashed free (`CombatActor.break_free()` calls the holder's `_on_hold_broken()`: the hold is over, token given back, cooldown started, and the holder **staggers away** from the victim at `grab_break_push_mps`; the victim stays where it stands, invulnerable `grab_break_invuln_frames`); the holder is hit, killed or removed (`_on_hit_received()` releases the victim with no hit: no invulnerability, no stagger; a LIGHT hit that armor absorbs does not end it, a HEAVY one that staggers does); the follow-up's strike.
- `EnemyActor` hooks (a grab section at the end of `enemy_actor.gd`): `is_holding(victim)` (true exactly while it holds him and the follow-up runs: the player's per-tick safety net lets go of a holder that stopped), `lifts_victim(victim)` (the slam), `warning_reach_m(move)` and `_on_hold_broken()`.
- **The warning**: the grab's wind-up warns like any attack (`reach_m` 1.15 / 1.3, heavy: the larger burst) and ends on `grabbed(holder, victim)` or on the whiff. The follow-up's `windup_started` comes after the player is already in its hands, face to face, so `warning_reach_m()` answers a reach nothing is within (`-(rear_warning_range_margin_m + 1)`) while it holds him: the warning stays dark through the hold (the HUD and the oracle both ask `warning_reach_m` first, so they agree; probe R10 100%).

**The player held** (`player.gd`):
- `_control_grabbed()` (the `GRABBED` arm of `_tick_control()`): every press of P or K (a held button is one press) is a mash; `grab_break_presses` of them (less `grab_break_decay_per_s`) call `break_free()`; the parser's buffer is emptied every tick, so nothing waits for the release. If the holder is not valid, dead, gone or `not is_holding(self)`, he is released with no ceremony. `grab_break_progress()` (0..1) feeds the HUD. `reset_state()` forgets it all.
- `_on_grabbed(by)`: the parser chain, buffer, held attack, guard presses, quick turn and magnet target are dropped (no stale kick `_cam_offset`), and the **look turns to the holder** over `grabbed_turn_frames`, to where the pin will put him (the holder's forward axis); `_process()` drops the mouse look while GRABBED.
- `_pin_spot()` (override): on the holder's forward axis `grabbed_pin_distance_m` from its origin, plus the holder's radius over his own (1.0 m for the Bruiser, 1.05 m for the boss), at its floor level; CombatActor's pin eases him in over `grab_pin_ease_s`.
- `_held_anim()` = `held_collar` (upright, leaning back a little, hands up on the holder's wrists; no hips nudge, so a release moves nothing); while `holder.lifts_victim(self)`, `held_lift` (the hips `GrabPoses.LIFT_M` higher), blended in over `grabbed_lift_s`.
- The **held view** (the camera file is untouched: it all goes through the existing getters): `get_camera_move_offset()` adds `get_grabbed_view_pitch_deg()` x `get_grabbed_view_weight()`: the view tips up toward the holder's eyes, at most `grabbed_view_pitch_deg`, the pitch damped over `grab_view_out_s` and **kept while the weight eases out after the release** (the weight eases in over `grab_view_in_s`, out over `grab_view_out_s`, in sim ticks). Released from the slam's lift he settles over `grabbed_lift_s`, not the ordinary blend.
- Measured through the real first-person camera (`tests/test_player_grabbed.gd`; for the boss to the slam's strike frame, the tick before the release, for the Bruiser to 80% of the headbutt's startup, its dive being deliberate): nothing of the holder nearer the lens than 0.26 m through the hold (the fists on his upper arms, a hand below the lens and 0.32 m out either side, are the nearest; the slam's old folded strike came to 0.12 m), his own body covers under 1% of the view, the holder's head is in it (5-10%) over a chest and arms that cover about 63% (Bruiser) to 71% (boss): the hold is what the player looks at. A break-free (or a holder hit away) moves the lens at most 1.2 cm a tick for the Bruiser, 1.9 cm from the boss's lift, and turns the view at most 1.2 degrees a tick.

**Poses** (`game/actors/poses/grab_poses.gd`): `bruiser_hold(g)` and `boss_hold(g)` are the holders' grips (the headbutt's and the slam's `guard` pose, so a follow-up starts with no jump), `named(&"hold_collar")` is the Bruiser's grip breathing, `held_pose()` the player's, `named(&"held_collar")` and `named(&"held_lift")` his struggle loops. The arm angles were solved numerically against the rig (fists on the player's upper arms at both sizes; the numbers are art data) and every key pose passes the ragdoll joint limits (upper arms raise at most 114 degrees). The grab recipes are `bruiser_grab` / `boss_grab` (spread wind-up, reach strike), the follow-ups `bruiser_headbutt` (the head and shoulders draw back, held trembling; the whole body drops forward through the player's face) and `boss_slam` (the fists rise with the lifted player, held at the top; the strike is a heave that rears back, final review: the torso arches back, the hips lean back 0.06 m, the legs straighten, the head goes back with it and both arms go up and out as he is let go; the old strike folded forward like the headbutt, the boss's cap filling the held view 0.12 m from the lens, and told the slam apart from the headbutt only by the throw after it). From the held key to the strike the slam's head goes 0.46 m back, the headbutt's 0.31 m ahead (`test_the_slam_heaves_him_with_its_head_back_and_the_headbutt_dives`).

**HUD and audio**: `GrabPrompt` (`game/ui/grab_prompt.gd`, a child of `hud.tscn`, stepped by `GameHud.advance()`): a "MASH P / K!" comic panel below the view center (clear of the top corners the rear warning owns) with a fill bar from `grab_break_progress()` and a pop of the lettering on every press, shown only while the player is GRABBED; it reads the player and writes nothing. `AudioDirector._on_grab_escaped()`: a whoosh and a block clack at the holder's chest and the holder's grunt, `grab_break_db` louder (existing pools).

**Magnetism in a crowd** (A5.1, also `player.gd`): `_find_magnet_target()` judges the cone by heading alone (flat: pitch and height do not count, so a tall body is found at any look) and passes over a body in invulnerability (the get-up's, a blink-in's: no hit lands on it); `_stop_distance(move, target)` adds the target's radius over his own, so the lunge does not drive into a Bruiser's or the boss's capsule; `_downed_target_near()` (the stomp's condition) is true only when no standing enemy is in the cone, so a K with a Punk in front of a body on the ground is a front kick.

**Tunables** (group Enemies, subgroup "Enemy grabs"): `grab_break_presses` 8, `grab_break_invuln_frames` 20, `grab_break_decay_per_s` 0, `grab_break_push_mps` 3, `grabbed_turn_frames` 8, `grabbed_pin_distance_m` 0.9, `grabbed_view_pitch_deg` 30, `grabbed_lift_s` 0.4, `enemy_grab_range_m` 1.05, `enemy_grab_half_angle_deg` 50, `grab_break_db` 0.

**Tests and tools**: `tests/test_enemy_moves_grab.gd` (the clips, joint limits, the measured reach, the pin-distance reach, the rules), `tests/test_enemy_grab.gd` (the connect, the whiff, the dodge, jump and armor outs, the mash with exactly `grab_break_presses` and one press fewer, the unbroken headbutt and slam, the holder hit, killed or removed, the warning, the director, the real AI in the crowd room and the training room), `tests/test_player_grabbed.gd` (the look, the held view through the real camera, the release, the prompt), `tests/test_magnetism_crowd.gd`; `tests/bot/acts/grab_acts.gd` for the crowd bot (`tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn captures/grab "Grab:" 12`: "dodge out", "held, mash free", "held, headbutt lands", "slam", "from behind").

### Kicker

`Kicker` (`game/enemies/kicker/kicker.gd` + `kicker.tscn`, catalog id `kicker`) is the fast flanker of SPEC §7: an `EnemyActor` with the Punk's hooks filled for its data and one override for its flank (the strafe side). 25 HP (`kicker_health`), no armor (a light hit interrupts its wind-up), `prefers_blind_side = true` (it *may* flank: while the director has it flanking, `is_flanking()`, it has a seat of its own as near directly behind the look as it can be, no part of the others' ring; otherwise it is seated by its own arc like any enemy: see "Opportunistic flanking" under Attack arcs), a lime jacket (0.55, 0.84, 0.14) with near-black accents and `voice_pitch_mult` 1.12 (SPEC §10 reserves red; lime tells it from the dummy's orange, the player's blue and the Punk's red or teal). Everything on the walk is multiplied: walking and stepping in by `kicker_speed_mult` (1.3), the posture strafe by `kicker_circle_mult` (1.6) on top.

**Attacks** (`EnemyAttack`s, picked by `kicker_sweep_chance` 0.4 for the sweep, else the jump kick; `force_attack(0)` the kick, `(1)` the sweep). Moves in `tools/gen_moves.gd` `_kicker_moves()`, rows in `tests/enemy_moves/kicker_table.gd`, recipes in `EnemyPosesKicker`; both are held telegraphs at the grunt floor:

| Move | Frames S/A/R | Damage | Reaction | Hitbox | `reach_m` | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `kicker_jump_kick` | 22/6/18 | 8 LIGHT | FLINCH | RightFootPoint 0.2 | 2.75 | `free_lunge` 1.6: the leap is applied by the base from the last startup frame over the active frames (nothing slides through the wind-up), and **reach_m includes it** (M3-plan A5.2: the strike pose alone touches a standing body 1.2 m out, measured 2.81 m with the leap), so the rear warning fires from 3.75 m behind. Blocked recoil 20. Wind-up: a deep crouch (the eye sinks 0.36 m), torso pitched forward, arms swung back, held and sinking a little more; strike: the hips rise 0.35 m (`HIPS_POS`: only the drawn body and its hurtboxes leave the floor, the capsule stays grounded), the right leg driven out level at chest height, the left tucked, the arms thrown wide; the foot is at 1.46 m on the strike frame and 1.26 m at the end of the active frames, a hit on the TORSO. Lands in a crouch |
| `kicker_sweep` | 20/5/22 | 8 LIGHT | KNOCKDOWN | arc 1.3 m, 80 deg, height 0 to 0.5 m | 1.45 | an arc over the LEGS capsules only: a jumping player's legs are above it (a jump has to lift the legs about 0.1 m by the arc's live frames). Knockback 3.0 along the radial and 1.5 up. Blocked recoil 14. Wind-up: a side lunge to the floor (the support leg folded deep, the right leg straight out to the side, both soles level: no explicit `HIPS_POS`, which would float or sink a foot, codemap G9), the body turned away through the hips and the head on the target (the eye sinks 0.41 m); strike: the spin of `spinning_heel`, the right leg out along the floor at ankle height |

The 15% rule holds per attack (the sweep alone is 8). Neither attack chains.

**The flank.** A slot behind the player is across the posture ring from where a Kicker walks in, and the straight line to it crosses the player. The way round is the base's `_orbit_toward()` (every archetype's since the final review; `Kicker._posture_target()` and `_enter_posture()` are gone, and `test_kicker.gd` holds the base): APPROACH and RECOVER walk a point on the ring `posture_orbit_lead_deg` (40) ahead of it on the short way round, kept as an angle and radius about the player (so it goes with him when he walks), until the slot is within `posture_orbit_gap_deg` or the straight way is clear of the ring's near edge; `_enter_posture()` swallows an arrival at such a point (the same frame as the `_posture_target()` call that returned it) so there is no stutter through POSTURE at every leg, and the next tick picks the next point. A walk-in from 18 m takes about 7 s and from the ring's front to directly behind about 2.5 s, never nearer than the ring (2.5 m). What stays the Kicker's is `_pick_strafe_dir()`: while it is flanking and POSTURE has it in view, it strafes toward the nearer edge of the view (not flanking, the base's); `_circle_mult()` is `kicker_circle_mult` only while it flanks. The director grants it tokens first from the blind arc (R9) and the warning fires at its wind-up from behind, both corners within 20 degrees of straight back (tested with the real HUD in `test_kicker.gd`).

**Tunables** (group "Enemies", subgroup "Kicker"): `kicker_flank_opportunistic` (true), `kicker_flank_busy_radius_m` (4.5), `kicker_flank_on_s` (0.5), `kicker_flank_off_s` (1.0), `kicker_flank_faced_off_s` (0.0) (see "Opportunistic flanking"), `kicker_health` 25, `kicker_circle_mult` 1.6, `kicker_speed_mult` 1.3, `kicker_sweep_chance` 0.4, `kicker_attack_cooldown_s` **3.0** (S0 seeded 1.2; chosen from measurement: two Kickers round a player who stands still for 40 s make 15-17 attacks at 2.5 to 3.5 s, 21 at 1.2 s and 12 at 5 s, where two Punks at their locked 5 s make 10-11, so the Kicker presses about half again as often for about the same damage a second, from behind). (`kicker_orbit_lead_deg` is gone: the way round is `posture_orbit_lead_deg`, in the Posture subgroup.)

**Tests and tools**: `tests/test_enemy_moves_kicker.gd` (clip timing and held wind-up, joint limits, measured reach, the leap in the jump kick's reach, the head in view through both telegraphs at 1 m and 2.5 m, the crouch depth the lens sees, the sweep's arc), `tests/test_kicker.gd` (data, the slot behind, the flank, the strafe, the sweep's knockdown and the jump over it, the kick landing from the engage distance and the ring's near edge, no slide in the wind-up, the warning's reach, and a lone Kicker in the crowd room with the real HUD), `tests/bot/acts/kicker_acts.gd` for `tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn`: "Kicker flanks", "Kicker jump kick in front", "Kicker sweep in front", "Kicker sweep jumped over", the two side views, "Kicker behind warns" (the oracle at 180, 135 and -135 degrees), "Kicker and Punks", and "Kicker cadence" / "Punk cadence (reference)" (count the `WU+` lines per name): `tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn captures/p2-kicker "Kicker flanks" 6`.

### Pipe Thug and the dropped pipe

`PipeThug` (`game/enemies/thug/pipe_thug.gd`, `pipe_thug.tscn`) is an `EnemyActor` archetype (`archetype = &"thug"`): 35 HP (`thug_health`), no armor, the grunt wind-up floor, an olive-green jacket with cream accents (no red: SPEC §10) and a yellow pipe. It overrides only the archetype hooks (`_build_attacks()`, `_health_tunable()`, `_speed_tunable()`, `_cooldown_tunable()`, `_configure_rig()`) plus `_ready()` and `_on_hit_received()` (which call `super`); `enemy_actor.gd` is untouched.

**The combo.** One attack while armed, `pipe combo`: `thug_pipe_1` (the telegraph), `thug_pipe_2` and `thug_pipe_3` (links with no wind-up of their own, each only if the swing before landed or was blocked, A2.14). Generated by `_thug_moves()` in `tools/gen_moves.gd`, rows in `tests/enemy_moves/thug_table.gd`, recipes in `EnemyPosesThug`:

| Move | Frames S/A/R | Damage | Reaction | Hitbox | `reach_m` | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `thug_pipe_1` | 22/3/14 | 10 LIGHT | FLINCH | WeaponTip 0.19 | 1.23 | the forehand; the pipe cocked high behind the right shoulder and held from 80% of the startup; blocked recoil 20 |
| `thug_pipe_2` | 12/3/14 | 10 LIGHT | FLINCH | WeaponTip 0.19 | 1.38 | the backhand; cocked out wide at the left, low; recoil 14 |
| `thug_pipe_3` | 14/4/20 | 10 LIGHT | STAGGER (3.5 m/s) | WeaponTip 0.19 | 1.43 | the overhead smash; steps in 0.2 m over its active frames (`free_lunge`, in `reach_m`), so it reaches a player two flinches have pushed back; recoil 14 |

All three carry the pipe's guard drain (`guard_damage_mult` 1.5, `PIPE_GUARD_MULT`). The third staggers because the chain is 3 x 10 and no knockdown may cost more than 15 (2.10; the data test checks the chain). The strikes are the player's pipe swings' (`PlaceholderPoses._pipe_swing_1/2/3`), with the right arm of the smash lowered from a 180 to a 142 degree raise (a ragdoll joint holds no more); only the wind-ups are new. The recipes are authored on the boxer guard `g` (the rig's stance, in which the pipe rests back over the right shoulder), not the weapon guard `wg`: every swing overrides the whole right arm, so only the start and end pose differ, and starting from the idle pose avoids a pop.

**The pipe.** `_ready()` spawns `pipe.tscn` (the player's `Weapon`), colours it and thickens its drawn radius (`Weapon.radius`, visual only; the collision capsule is unchanged) and `attach_to(self)`s it to the right hand's `WeaponGrip` (`has_pipe()`). While held it is `freeze`d on the grip with no collision and `is_available()` is false, so nobody can pick it up. `armed = false` at spawn gives an unarmed Thug (tests, bots).

**The drop.** `_on_hit_received(hit)` (after `super`): a hit with reaction KNOCKDOWN, LAUNCH or THROWN, or a killing blow, calls `Weapon.drop(velocity, spin_rps)`; a flinch or stagger does not. `Weapon.drop()` (new, `game/weapons/weapon.gd`) is a no-op unless the weapon is held: it reparents to the holder's parent (the room), restores the world and pick-up physics (`_set_ground_physics()`), and sets the velocity and a spin about the vertical. The pipe leaves toward whoever hit it (the hit's direction reversed) at `thug_drop_speed_mps`: pushed away from the hit it slid two to three metres past the Thug and lay out of LB's reach (`pickup_range` 1.5 m); toward the attacker it lies 0.6 to 1.3 m in front of the player (0.6 to 1.0 before the rest damping, below). It is an ordinary pick-up (LB, `Player._find_weapon()` through group `"weapon"`) with its own full durability and wears as any pipe does. **It lies still** (final review, tells-02): a loose weapon rests by damping from its first touch of the world, not by a rotation lock or a freeze (a lock freezes a pipe that landed tilted at its tilt; a freeze places a body by script). `Weapon._integrate_forces()` sets the linear and angular damping to `weapon_rest_linear_damp` (6.0) and `weapon_rest_angular_damp` (8.0) per second, replacing the engine's 0.1, every step it touches something and is not held, flying or frozen; `_set_air_damping()` puts the engine's back when it leaves a hand (`drop()`, `throw_from()`), so the fall and the throw are as they were. The capsule stops sliding and rolling within about a second and Jolt puts it to sleep: 5 s after the drop it lies 0.87, 1.10 and 1.29 m from the player (hit from 1.0, 1.2 and 1.4 m), asleep, and has not moved ten seconds later (before, it was still spinning at 3.3 rad/s and crept another 0.32 m). Both values are weapon-wide, in "Grab and weapons"; the drop's own push values below stay. The Thug then fights unarmed (`_attacks` rebuilt with the Punk's two attacks, by move id) and never re-arms. First person: the lying pipe is below the lens at the forward pitch, so the player looks down to see it (the pipe is yellow and thick so it reads on the grey street).

**The cap.** Waves repeat and every Thug drops a pipe, so `PipeThug.cull_dropped_pipes()` clears the oldest dropped pipes (group `"thug_pipe"`, free, not held or flying) when more than `thug_pipes_on_floor_max` (4, 0 = no limit) lie in the scene. The training room's pipe and a pipe in the player's hand are never touched.

**Tunables** (Pipe Thug subgroup): `thug_health` 35, `thug_attack_cooldown_s` 8.0 (counted through the recover hold and the walk back: below about 4 s it never binds; measured on six Thugs and on a Thug among five Punks, the hard metrics held at 5 and 8 s and the combo's 30 damage against the Punk's 12 is why it is longer than the Punk's 5.0), `thug_speed_mult` 0.95, `thug_drop_speed_mps` 0.5, `thug_drop_spin_rps` 3.0, `thug_pipes_on_floor_max` 4.

**Tests and acts.** `tests/test_enemy_moves_thug.gd` (clips, joint limits, measured reach, the first-person telegraph at 1 m and 2.5 m with and without the pipe in the hand), `tests/test_pipe_thug.gd` (the Thug, the combo, the drop, the pipe at rest for three drop distances, other hit directions and a throw, the player taking it, the cap), `tests/bot/acts/thug_acts.gd` (first person: "Thug three swings", "Thug 1v1", "Thug drops the pipe", "Take the Thug's pipe", and the six-Thug measurement acts).

### Bruiser

`Bruiser` (`game/enemies/bruiser/bruiser.gd`, `bruiser.tscn`) is an `EnemyActor` archetype (`archetype = &"bruiser"`): 80 HP (`bruiser_health`), body 2.05 m tall and 0.45 m in radius (scene data), `heavy = true` (the 30-frame wind-up floor and the larger rear-warning burst), `voice_pitch_mult` 0.8, a deep purple jacket with steel-grey accents (no red: SPEC §10). It overrides the archetype hooks (`_build_attacks()`, `_health_tunable()`, `_speed_tunable()`, `_cooldown_tunable()`, `_configure_rig()`) and `_ready()`, `_apply_armor()`, `_on_armor_absorbed()`, `_on_hit_received()`, `_tick_control()`, `_pin_spot()`, `can_be_grabbed()`, `_parry_staggers()`, `_stop()`, `_animate_legs()` and `debug_state_text()` (the ones that touch the base call `super`); `enemy_actor.gd` is untouched.

**Armor.** `_ready()` sets the base's flags `armor_light`, `armor_heavy_to_stagger` and `armor_finisher_to_stagger`. A LIGHT hit lands its damage but is absorbed (`hit.armor_absorbed`, reaction NONE, no knockback): no flinch, the wind-up or strike runs on, and `HitDetector` puts only the attacker in hitstop, so a jab neither interrupts nor stretches a wind-up (and the rear warning goes on). A HEAVY hit (the hook) staggers it out of whatever it was doing, a FINISHER (the uppercut) staggers it instead of launching it. A move with `breaks_armor` (the haymaker) keeps its own reaction (a knockdown) and starts the **armor break**: `_armor_broken`, and `bruiser_armor_break_s` of it counted in sim frames from the moment the Bruiser is back on its feet (not while it is flying, down, getting up or dead: the haymaker's knockdown and get-up are about 1.75 s of the default 2.0). While broken `_apply_armor()` leaves every hit alone, so a jab flinches it; a second haymaker restarts the window. A killing hit is never softened. `armor_is_broken()` and `armor_break_left_s()` read it; F3 shows `ARMOR OFF <s>`.

**Grabs on it.** `can_be_grabbed()` is the base's rule and either STAGGER or armor broken: a hook, then LB, a knee and the throw (or the staggering a mashed-off grab leaves it in). A perfect block still staggers a Bruiser that was blocked (`_parry_staggers()` stays true, on purpose: the parry is the answer to the smash). Held, a Bruiser stands `bruiser_held_pin_extra_m` (0.3 m) further out than a 1.8 m body (`_pin_spot()`): the held pose bends a 2.05 m body over in front of the lens, and at the ordinary pin its head was 0.08 m from the lens and covered 83% of the view (a held 1.8 m dummy: 0.24 m, 33%); pushed out it is 0.35 m and 42%, and the knee and the throw still reach it.

**The brace.** An absorbed hit while the Bruiser stands with no move running plays the named clip `brace` (`EnemyPosesBruiser.named()`, 0.36 s: the chest pushes out, the chin drops). The base calls the locomotion every tick, which would end the clip at once, so `_stop()` and `_animate_legs()` leave the rig alone while `_brace_frames` counts down. During a wind-up or strike the move's own pose is never swapped for it.

**Attacks.** Two, picked by `bruiser_grab_chance` (0.4; the smash takes the rest): the overhead smash and `grab and headbutt` (`EnemyAttack` kind `&"grab"` with `bruiser_grab` and `bruiser_headbutt`, P2-grab's moves, run by `EnemyHold`: the grab connects by code or whiffs, the headbutt's startup is the mash window, unbroken it lands for 15).

| Move | Frames S/A/R | Damage | Reaction | Hitbox | `reach_m` | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bruiser_smash` | 34/4/26 | 15 HEAVY | KNOCKDOWN (0, 3, 4 m/s) | both FistPoints, 0.2 m spheres | 1.3 (measured 1.32) | blockable; a block costs the guard 60 of 100 (4 per damage) and the Bruiser never recoils (`blocked_recoil_frames` 0); no magnetism, no lunge |

Generated by `_bruiser_moves()` in `tools/gen_moves.gd`, row in `tests/enemy_moves/bruiser_table.gd`, recipe in `EnemyPosesBruiser`. The wind-up is the heavy floor or more (34 frames; `windup_mult` 0.25 floors at 30). **The recipe:** both fists raised over the head (the upper arms 140 degrees forward and 32 out, the shoulders shrugged 28 degrees so the fists reach 2.36 m, above the 2.29 m head top, inside the ragdoll's 140 degree limit), the back arched and the weight on the rear foot, the raise done by 42% of the startup (`RAISE_AT`) and held from 80% (`windup_hold_at`); then both fists drive down together in front of the chest while the body drops over the planted front foot, ending at 1.46 m high and 1.04 m ahead, x +-0.3: the player's chest and head, which a pose of the player's own angles would pass over on a body this tall. At 1.2 m (where it winds up) the raised fists are over the top of the lens (the head stays in view): what reads there is the arms leaving the picture; from the ring both fists show. The downswing passes through a wide arc (the two poses are far apart); it is three frames.

**Armor that reads** (presentation only, no game state written; A6.1). A hit with `armor_absorbed` gets: in `VfxDirector` a grey spark (`spawn_armor_spark()`: the block spark's art tinted `armor_spark_grey`, `armor_spark_m` wide, for `vfx_block_fx_s`) instead of the tier's burst, no word, no blood; in `AudioDirector` the swing's whoosh and the block clack pitched down by `armor_clank_pitch` (`armor_clank_db` on top), no thud, no grunt; in `CombatLogOverlay` the flag `ARMOR` (from `HitInfo.describe()`/`add_hit()`) and a grey line. Every other hit is unchanged, and the pooled sprite's tint is reset on reuse. The tunables live in the Bruiser subgroup and apply to the boss too.

**Tunables** (Bruiser subgroup): `bruiser_health` 80, `bruiser_armor_break_s` 2.0, `bruiser_grab_chance` 0.4, `bruiser_attack_cooldown_s` 4.0 (S0 seeded 2.2; it counts through the recover hold and the walk back, so it hardly binds below about 4: against a player who does nothing, 7 attacks in 40 s at 2.2, 5 at 3.5 and 4.0, 4-5 at 5.0; among five Punks the director's queue makes it two in 20 s whatever it is), `bruiser_speed_mult` 0.8, `bruiser_held_pin_extra_m` 0.3, and the armor look `armor_spark_m` 0.24, `armor_spark_grey` 0.55, `armor_clank_pitch` 0.6, `armor_clank_db` 0.

**Tests and acts.** `tests/test_enemy_moves_bruiser.gd` (the clip, joint limits, measured reach, the telegraph at 1.0 m, 1.2 m and from the ring, the wind-up overhead and the strike at the player's head and shoulders, the guard cost, the brace clip), `tests/test_bruiser.gd` (identity, armor in every tier, the armor break window, grabbing it, the smash and the guard, the grab mashed free and landing, the AI with a token, the warning's larger burst, the held view, and the first-person lens clearance of the smash and the headbutt landing on the player: nothing of the Bruiser nearer the lens than 0.1 m, twice the near plane; measured 0.24 m and 0.14 m), `tests/test_armor_presentation.gd` (the spark, the clank, the log), `tests/bot/acts/bruiser_acts.gd` (first person: "Bruiser shrugs off jabs", "Hook staggers the Bruiser", "Haymaker breaks the armor", "Grab the staggered Bruiser", "Bruiser smash", "Bruiser smash blocked", "Bruiser grab: mash free", "Bruiser headbutt", "Bruiser winds up behind", "Bruiser 1v1", and the measurement acts: "Bruiser cadence", "Six Bruisers, observed", "Bruiser among Punks, passive" and "fight").

### Boss

`Boss` (`game/enemies/boss/boss.gd` + `boss.tscn`, catalog id `boss`; placeholder name "Boss", its name and look wait for the art) is the level boss of SPEC §7: an `EnemyActor` with the Punk's hooks filled for its data and a handful of overrides. 300 HP (`boss_health`), `body_height` 2.3 and `body_radius` 0.5 (scene data; the rig is scaled by its height, never its node), `heavy` (the 30-frame wind-up floor, the larger rear-warning burst), `voice_pitch_mult` 0.7, **group `"boss"`** (the HUD's bottom bar follows it, `EnemyHealthBars` skips its over-head bar, the readability probe's R11 exempts it), charcoal (0.13, 0.13, 0.15) with orange (0.96, 0.5, 0.08) cap, wraps and shoes: not red (SPEC §10), told from the Punk's teal, the Kicker's lime, the Thug's olive and the Bruiser's purple.

**Armor and never grabbable.** `armor_light` and `armor_heavy_to_stagger` on, `armor_finisher_to_stagger` off (a finisher knocks it down: the punish window), so a light hit lands its damage and is absorbed (no flinch, the wind-up and its warning go on) and a heavy hit staggers it, which cuts the grab's wind-up (Game-boy's rule) and lets go of a player it holds. One addition to the base's rule (`_apply_armor()`): **while it is winding up or running a charge or a pound, a heavy hit is absorbed too**. The autopilot's third punch (a hook, HEAVY) cancelled every wind-up there was and it beat the boss in 38 s without taking a hit; now the charge and the pound are answered by a dodge, a jump, a block or a finisher, and the grab's wind-up stays cuttable. `can_be_grabbed()` is false in every state (the player's LB skips it). A second addition, **poise**, is below: for a while after it gets up the same absorption covers a heavy hit and a finisher whatever it is doing.

**Attacks** (`EnemyAttack`s with `heavy = true`; `force_attack(0)` the charge, `(1)` the pound, `(2)` the grab). Moves in `tools/gen_moves.gd` `_boss_moves()` (the grab's pair is `_grab_moves()`'s, P2-grab), rows in `tests/enemy_moves/boss_table.gd` (`boss: true`: SPEC §4 exempts the boss from the 15% rule), recipes in `EnemyPosesBoss`. All three are held telegraphs at the heavy floor, never recoil when blocked (the table's rule for the boss), and no move is FINISHER tier (A2.13).

| Move | Frames S/A/R | Damage | Tier, reaction | Hitbox | `reach_m` | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `boss_charge` | 32/24/30 | 20 | HEAVY, KNOCKDOWN (0, 3, 6) | arc 1.1 m, 35 deg either side, 0.5 to 1.9 m high | **5.3** | `free_lunge` 4.0: the base applies it from the last startup frame over the active frames (the wind-up never slides; measured 3.8 m in 24 frames) and it stops at a wall (`move_and_slide()`). **reach_m includes the dash** (A6.2): the arc alone meets a standing body 1.3 m out (measured 5.31 m with it), so a charge from behind warns out to 6.3 m. Blockable (no blocked recoil). Wind-up: a crouch pitched over the lead knee, head up and on the target, the arms drawn back and low (so they never cover the face), the right shoulder in; the eye sinks 0.48 m, held and sinking. Strike: a stride held through the dash, arms swept back (a fist in front of the chest was 0.22 m from the lens when it landed), the other stride at the end of the active frames; recovery brakes in a crouch |
| `boss_pound` | 36/4/30 | 15 | HEAVY, KNOCKDOWN (0, 3.5, 4.5) | full ring 2.5 m, 0 to 0.6 m high | 2.6 | **unblockable**; the band is the feet and shins, so a jump that lifts the legs about half a metre by the strike frame clears it (measured 0.84 m up when tested) and so does a dodge. Wind-up: rears up on straight legs and tiptoes, both arms overhead and wide (so the head stays in the view between them; the shoulder cone limits the fists to about the top of the head), held and climbing; strike (a squat, final review): the hips drop 0.33 m below the guard's on the same deep legs, the spine near vertical (the head 0.09 m ahead of the hips at the strike key and 0.12 m at its end, where the old fold put it 0.50 m ahead and the cap in the lens), both fists driven down in front of the knees (0.70 m up); the follow-through key carries the arms out wide and low (raise 10, roll 64), so a pound that misses does not bring the fists up through the lens |
| `boss_grab` then `boss_slam` | 32/6/24 then 54/4/30 | 0 then 25 | LIGHT, NONE then HEAVY, THROWN | none (by code) | 1.3 / 1.05 | P2-grab's `EnemyHold`: connects by code at the grab's strike frame, the slam's startup is the mash window, the victim is lifted (`held_lift`) and thrown. HEAVY and THROWN, never FINISHER (A6.2) |

**What it throws** (`_choose_attack()`, at ENGAGE and RECOVER). Weights are the chances `boss_charge_chance` 0.4, `boss_pound_chance` 0.3, `boss_grab_chance` 0.3, by range: the charge only while the player is at least `boss_charge_min_m` (2.5, the ring's near edge) away, since up close it has no run-up; the pound and the grab at any range (it walks in to `reach_m` less `engage_margin_m` for them: 2.55 m and 1.25 m); with `boss_crowd_count` (2) or more other live enemies within `boss_crowd_m` (3.0) of the player only the pound. Nothing weighted falls back to the base's pick. Over four seeds of 60 s against a player who stands still the mix is 35% charge, 38% pound, 27% grab.

**It wakes in place** (final review, fair-01). Every reaction used to end in APPROACH, so a boss the autopilot's chain kept in REACT walked back to its slot after every knockdown, never reached POSTURE (the only state that asks for a token) and never attacked, and `boss_attack_cooldown_s` could not bind. Now `_wakes_in_place()` is true: a reaction that ends within the posture ring's far edge puts it in POSTURE where it stands for `posture_resume_s` (3.0), with the stray rule and the slot leash off, backing out to the ring's near edge if it is inside and rolling no taunt, and the director serves it before the queue and the blind-spot roll (`has_token_priority()`); after the hold, or when it is hit again, the ordinary rules apply, and a boss knocked far away walks back to its slot as anyone does. Against the full autopilot (the boss alone, three seeds, 5400 ticks) it started 5 or 6 wind-ups and landed 2 attacks (it started 2 to 4 and landed 1), but the kill was still at 41 to 43 s and the boss in REACT over half of its life: the autopilot's chain (jab, jab, hook, uppercut) staggered and floored it every 3 s, whatever it had woken to do. That is what **poise** (next) is for; `boss_health` and `boss_attack_cooldown_s` are real knobs since the wake-in-place fix. The wake-up hold lasts as long as the poise does (`_resume_posture()`: `max(posture_resume_s, poise)`), so a poised boss does not walk back to its slot with the player at its nose.

**Poise** (F-boss-poise, after the final review's fair-01; `M3-calls/F-boss-poise.md` has the numbers and the alternatives). `is_poised()`: a heavy hit and a finisher are absorbed like a light one (`hit.armor_absorbed`, reaction NONE, no knockback: the damage lands, a grey spark, no flinch, the move runs on). It is true, for either of two reasons:
- **After it gets up**: `_tick_poise()` (before the AI's tick) sees it in STAGGER, LAUNCHED, THROWN, DOWNED or GETUP, and on the first tick it is FREE again starts `boss_poise_s` (4.0) of poise. It ends early with the `boss_poise_attacks`-th (1) attack of its own to finish (`_enter_recover()`: a wind-up cut by a hit is not one, so a cut grab keeps the poise going). A parry's recoil (HITSTUN) is not a stagger and gives none: its 36 frames are the player's reward. The poise starts at the get-up, not at the hit, so the player who staggered it still gets the finisher that follows the hook (the boss is reeling, not poised, and goes down: one knockdown per chain).
- **The reaction cap**: `boss_poise_after_reactions` (2) or more reactions (a hit that was not absorbed and staggered or floored it, counted in `_on_hit_received()`) inside the last `boss_poise_window_s` (8.0): poised until the oldest one leaves the window. So a chain that keeps landing cannot floor it more often than twice in eight seconds, whatever its timing.

What poise never absorbs: the **haymaker** (`breaks_armor`, the deliberate punish, as with the Bruiser: it knocks a poised boss down), a **killing hit**, and a hit on the **grab** (any phase of it, the hold included): a heavy hit still cuts the grab and lets go of the player (Game-boy's rule). The cut staggers it, which does not end the poise, so the finisher that follows the hook is absorbed. 0 for `boss_poise_s` and `boss_poise_after_reactions` is today's armor alone.

**Stepping back.** The shared first-person telegraph check (`tests/enemy_move_checks.gd`, a 2.3 m body) says the pound's wind-up reads from 1.4 m (its raised arms are over the top of the lens nearer than that, and the head is out of the view), the charge's and the grab's from 1.2 m, and none at 1.0 m, where a chain leaves the player (his magnetism stops him at about 1.05 m). `_tick_engage()` is overridden: with a token, the commit pause over and the next attack a charge or a pound, a player closer than `boss_read_m` (1.5) makes it walk backward at its walking speed, facing him, for at most `boss_back_off_s` (0.8) before the base's ENGAGE goes on to wind up (the bound lets a player who follows it back still be attacked; the grab is thrown from where it stands, its range being 1.3 m). Measured over twelve full fights: 34% of the pound and charge wind-ups began under 1.35 m without it and 8% with it (the median distance 1.5 m and 1.9 m), at no cost in hits landed.

**Measured** (`Boss fight`, the full autopilot, the boss alone, seeds 0 to 11, the `BOSSMETER` line; before is `boss_poise_s` 0, `boss_poise_after_reactions` 0, `boss_read_m` 0 and `boss_attack_cooldown_s` 4.0): the kill at 39 s (41 before), the boss in REACT 21% of its life (48%), 7.0 wind-ups of which 1.8 were cut, mostly grabs (5.3 and 2.2), 4.6 hits landed on the player for 76 damage (3.1 and 55; 3 to 6 a fight, 1 to 5 before), the player KO'd in 3 fights of 12 (1) and the revive winning every time. A careful player (`Boss held back fight`, jab-jab only, two seeds) kills it in 68 and 94 s (142 s before) and takes 7 to 10 hits (7); a passive one takes 13 hits a minute (8 and 9) and is KO'd about every 25 s. Each mechanism alone: poise after getting up only, 29% and 4.1 hits; the cap only, 22% and 4.7; neither, with only the cooldown at 1.5, 53% and 2.1 hits (the cooldown alone does nothing: the chain cuts the wind-ups it lets start). The cooldown matters because poise lets its wind-ups through and the fight gets shorter: with poise and the cap, 1.0 gives 7.3 wind-ups and 3.8 hits, 1.5 gives 7.0 and 4.6, 2.0 gives 5.4 and 3.0, 3.0 gives 4.7 and 2.7, and the old 4.0 with poise alone 4.5 and 2.4 (a 32 s kill). The hits per fight sit at 4 to 5 and cannot go much higher at 300 HP: each landed hit keeps the director from starting the next wind-up while the player is down and getting up, and the fight is as long as the player's damage rate lets it be (a chain deals 32 in about a second, so a boss that does not flinch is dead in well under 20 s of standing: it is the reactions, the guards and the knockdowns that make the fight 40 s long). `boss_health` 400 gives 4.8 hits (a 47 s kill) and 500 gives 6.0 (56 s, the player KO'd in half of the fights); not taken, SPEC §7 says 300.

**Phase 2.** `_tick_control()` (after the base's) polls `health <= max_health x boss_phase2_at` (0.5) while alive, so every way health can fall (a hit, an absorbed hit, a chip) is seen; once, it sets `phase` to 2, emits `CombatEvents.boss_phase_changed(self, 2)`, and from then every turn it makes is `boss_phase2_turn_mult` (1.8) faster. `_face()` is overridden: every turn the base makes (posture, engage, the wind-up's tracking) is scaled by `boss_turn_rate` over `enemy_turn_rate` (2.5 / 6.0), so the boss is slow to follow a sidestep and a late one makes its charge miss. If it is free and posturing it beckons (`taunt`, the open-handed gesture); busy (it attacks far more often than it stands there), it beckons at its next free moment (`_tick_beckon()`). It then calls in `boss_adds_count` (2) Street Punks, **one every `wave_spawn_interval_s`, the first at once** (a rig takes about 10 ms to build; two in a frame was a 40 ms spawn hitch against R15's 33, now 24 to 28): through the room's `EncounterSpawner.spawn_adds(&"punk", 1, global_position)` when there is one (the free spawn point nearest the boss, so they walk in from the doorways and alleys and join the running wave: the wave is not cleared while they live), else, or when no point is free, on the navmesh `boss_add_ring_m` (5.0) round it (`SpawnRoomCheck` for room, the first toward the player, the rest spread round). They are ordinary Punks: they count toward `alive_count()` and take tokens. `boss.adds` lists them. The call-in's countdown waits while `GameInput.menu_open` (the phase itself still changes); it is not gated on `ai_frozen`, the Debug panel's switch and the scripted acts' way of placing an attacker. A boss that dies stops calling.

**The parry.** `_parry_staggers()` is false (a perfect block would parry-lock it), and its moves' blocked recoil is 0, so a parry would do nothing. `react_to_block()` is overridden: a perfect block (not a guard break) knocks it into the base's recoil (HITSTUN, the `recoil` pose, the token released) for `boss_parry_recoil_frames` (36), a small window to hit it in. An ordinary block leaves it alone.

**Performance and look.** The boss covers 10% of the first-person view at the ring's near edge (2.5 m) and 4% at 4.0 m. Landing, the nearest boss mesh to the lens is 0.38 m for the charge (the cap's brim; `_pull_strike` snaps the pose after the hitstop as for every landed strike) and for the pound 0.41 m with the player at 0.9 m from it, 0.47 m at 1.1 m and 1.55 m at 2.2 m (it is thrown at a metre, since his magnetism brings him to about 1.05 m of it whenever he punches it; an earlier figure of 1.2 m was measured from 2.2 m only, and at 0.9 m the old fold-over put the cap in the lens at 0.000 m; the boss's own guard is 0.45 m from the lens at 0.9 m, so the torso fills the view there in any pose); the grab's view is P2-grab's. A boss alone spawns in 12 to 20 ms windowed.

**Tunables** (group "Enemies", subgroup "Boss"): `boss_health` 300, `boss_phase2_at` 0.5, `boss_turn_rate` 2.5, `boss_phase2_turn_mult` 1.8, `boss_adds_count` 2, `boss_add_ring_m` 5.0, `boss_charge_min_m` **2.5** (S0 seeded 4.0, the posture ring's far edge, where nothing posturing stands: no charge in 4 runs of 60 s), `boss_charge_chance` 0.4, `boss_pound_chance` 0.3, `boss_grab_chance` 0.3, `boss_attack_cooldown_s` **1.5** (S0 seeded 1.8, P3-boss 4.0; the cooldown counts from the attack's end through the recover hold and the walk back, and with poise letting its wind-ups through it binds above about 1.5 s: see the measurements above; against a player who stands still 4.0 was 8 to 9 hits a minute and 1.5 is 13), `boss_speed_mult` 0.85, `boss_crowd_m` 3.0, `boss_crowd_count` 2, `boss_parry_recoil_frames` 36, and the poise's: `boss_poise_s` 4.0, `boss_poise_attacks` 1, `boss_poise_after_reactions` 2, `boss_poise_window_s` 8.0, `boss_read_m` 1.5 and `boss_back_off_s` 0.8. Today's boss is `boss_poise_s` 0, `boss_poise_after_reactions` 0, `boss_read_m` 0 and `boss_attack_cooldown_s` 4.0.

**Tests and tools**: `tests/test_enemy_moves_boss.gd` (clip timing and held wind-up, joint limits, the run loop, measured reach with the dash in it, the head in view through both telegraphs at 2.5 m and 4.0 m (not 1 m: the boss's head is over the top of the lens there whatever it does), the crouch and the raised fists the lens sees, the arcs, the heavy floor, the table rows, no FINISHER, the slam HEAVY and THROWN), `tests/test_boss.gd` (data, groups, armor, poise (after a stagger and after a knockdown, its length, its end with the next attack, the haymaker, the grab's cut and the poise going on after it, a killing hit, the reaction cap and its window, no poise from a parry, both tunables at 0, the wake-up hold's length), the step back (the pound and the charge read from `boss_read_m` and not from 1 m, it steps back close up, bounded against a player who follows, 0 means where it stands, the grab is thrown from where it stands), the deferred beckon, never grabbable, the pound hit, through a guard, jumped, out of its ring, the charge's dash only in its active frames, at a wall, hitting for 20, dodged by a sidestep, an ordinary block, the parry, the choice by range, the crowd, the warning's reach, phase 2 once at 150 exactly, its turn rate, the adds with and without a spawner and when the spawner falls short, one at a time, the boss bar, the view at the ring, the lens when each strike lands, and the real crowd room with its HUD), `tests/test_boss_close_lens.gd` (the nearest boss mesh from the held pound key through the hitstop and the player's flight with him at 0.8, 0.9, 1.1 and 1.3 m, and for a pound that does not land through the follow-through key), `test_enemy_moves_boss.gd` also pins the pound's squat on the rig (hips down at least 0.30 m, the head at most 0.25 m ahead of the hips, both fists below the hips at the strike and its end), `tests/bot/acts/boss_acts.gd` for `tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn`: "Boss charge in front", "Boss charge dodged", "Boss charge behind" (180 and 135 degrees, WARN-CHECK), "Boss pound", "Boss pound jumped", "Boss grab slam", "Boss armor", "Boss phase 2 adds", "Boss bar", two side views, "Boss cadence" (count the `WU+` lines by move: `-- --act="Boss cadence" --seed=<n> --tune=boss_attack_cooldown_s=<s>`) and the three that print a `BOSSMETER` line (`_meter()`: kill time, the share of its life in REACT, its reactions, its wind-ups by move and how many were cut, the hits it landed, the grabs that connected, the player's KOs, its poise time, each wind-up's move and distance, and what the player's hits did to it, absorbed ones starred): "Boss fight" (the full autopilot, no god mode, ends when the boss falls), "Boss held back fight" (the careful autopilot) and "Boss passive" (a player who does nothing); `--tune=boss_poise_s=0 --tune=boss_poise_after_reactions=0 --tune=boss_read_m=0 --tune=boss_attack_cooldown_s=4` is the boss before the poise, so one act measures both; `tests/bot/acts/final_boss_poses_acts.gd`: "Final pound at 0.9 m", "Final pound at 1.1 m", "Final slam (side view)" and "Final pipe rest".

### Whole-fight integration (P4-int)

The last package of the milestone puts every archetype in one fight and checks the done-when end to end.

**`tests/test_m3_done_when.gd`** (the real crowd room and HUD, real enemies, seeded):
- *The warning, every archetype.* A Punk, Kicker, Bruiser, Thug and the boss each stand at their own strike range (1.0, 2.6, 1.25, 1.0 and 2.55 m: the distance at which the attack really lands) at 135 degrees left, 135 degrees right and 180 degrees from a fixed look, the AI frozen, and `force_attack()`. A sampler that runs after the frame's simulation (physics priority 1000) records which corners the HUD's `RearWarning` shows: top-left only, top-right only and both, on every frame from the wind-up's first to the contact, dark on the contact's own frame and never lit again (a chain's links have no wind-up of their own); a heavy attacker's burst is flagged heavy and larger. The corners and the distances are literals in the test. Negative controls for the Punk, Kicker and Bruiser: 50 and -50 degrees in view, and 135 and 180 degrees out of range (the reach plus the margin and half a metre): no corner on any frame.
- *The soak.* 1500 frames of Punk, Kicker, Thug, Bruiser, Punk, Kicker at the crowd bot's surround points with the readability probe on: R1, R2, R3, R10 and R11 hard, nobody stuck, never more than six alive. The player takes real damage and his health is refilled every frame (god mode zeroes `hit.damage` and would make R11 vacuous).
- *The waves.* With the waves on and the autopilot fighting, `alive_count()` never exceeds `wave_max_alive` and a second wave starts. A second case starts the spawner at wave 3 (`start_at_wave(3)`: the table's mixed waves, no boss wave, the autopilot held back, 2700 frames, god mode, the probe on): `alive_count()` never over `wave_max_alive`, at least three archetypes seen, R1, R3 and R10 held.
Both Debug toggles (`ai_frozen`, `god_mode`) are outside the preset: a test that sets them puts them back by hand.

**`tools/check_m3_log.py <bot.log>...`** parses a crowd-bot log (from `tools/capture_bot.sh` or a headless run) and exits 1 on a failure: every `WARN-CHECK` line `ok`; each wind-up the rule demands a corner for lit within one frame (`lat` 0 or 1 and the HUD's own `W+` line for that side); no `W+` for a wind-up the rule leaves dark; token holders (`TOK+`/`TOK-`, counted at the end of each physics frame, per act) never over `attacker_cap`; no `FAIL (hard)` row; no `SCRIPT ERROR`/`ERROR:`. It reads the bot's doubled event lines once, skips a wind-up that began outside its act (a `WARN-CHECK` line that follows a `READABILITY` block with no header since, or whose frame is earlier than the frame in the header of the act it is printed under: a wind-up that began in the gap before the act and was judged after its header; logs recorded before the oracle stopped doing that have such lines), and excuses a wind-up that lived one frame. `--cap N`, `--strict` (a log with no WARN-CHECK fails). Tested by `tests/test_check_m3_log.gd`.

**Acts** (`tests/bot/acts/crowd_acts.gd`, first person, probe on): "Mixed six, observed" (god mode, a slow 360 degrees, then still), "Mixed six, fight" (the held-back autopilot for 30 s, Punks topping the six up), "Waves to the boss" (waves on, `boss_wave_every` 2, the autopilot fights until the boss is in phase 2 and its two Punks are in, then 6 s: the two opening Punk waves and the boss's arrival and call-in, no mixed wave, and the boss is beaten before it attacks), "Mixed waves" (the spawner starts at wave 3 with the boss waves off, the held-back autopilot for 90 s: the table's Punk, Kicker, Thug and Bruiser waves, WAVE lines for each start and clear), "Boss wave, held back" (the spawner starts at the boss wave, `boss_wave_every` + 1; the held-back autopilot; the boss's health is set to the phase 2 line by hand if the autopilot has not taken it there in 45 s, and a `BOSS ... forced` line says so; then 30 s with its Punks in: the boss's attacks and adds in a crowd), "Rear warning, every archetype" (the same fifteen wind-ups as the test, WARN-CHECK per wind-up, heavy bursts) and "Perf, six with ragdolls" (a windowed timing sampler beside the act: frame windows, the spawn of each enemy, the worst frame after an enemy goes down; the engine monitors it prints (`process`, `physics`) hold the maximum of the last second, not the last frame; the bodies are counted directly (simulating ragdoll bodies of running, not-asleep ragdolls, and awake pipes); the frame lines and the probe's R15 are the measure; record it windowed, **not** with Movie Maker, or R15 is n/a: `godot --path . res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn -- --act="Perf, six with ragdolls"`). Crowd bot additions: `--seed=<n>` starts every archetype's numbering at 7n, `_reset` frees floor pipes (group `thug_pipe`), starts the waves over (`start_at_wave(1)`) and reseeds the spawner's random generator, `keep_alive(count, id := &"punk")`. Acts of one run are not bit-identical to the same act run alone (the autopilot's and the director's own state is not reset), so the evidence is recorded one act per process.

**The "LB" pick-up cue** (`game/ui/pickup_cue.gd`, `PickupCue`, a child of the HUD stepped by `GameHud.advance()`): a small comic panel low in the view while `PlayerCharacter.pickup_target()` is not null. That getter (read-only; `_grab_action()` is untouched) is what LB would take: an available weapon within `pickup_range`, with the player on his feet, alive, empty-handed and no enemy in the grab's reach (LB grabs first). The Thug's dropped pipe lies below the forward lens, so without the cue a player who does not look down never sees it. Tunables (group "Grab and weapons", subgroup "Pick-up cue"): `pickup_cue_enabled`, `pickup_cue_size`, `pickup_cue_opacity`, `pickup_cue_pulse_s`. `tests/test_pickup_cue.gd`.

**Rig build cost** (`HumanoidRig._named_library()`): the named `AnimationLibrary` of a (scale, stance) is built once and shared by every rig of that size and stance (it was 8 of the 9 to 15 ms each enemy cost to build; nothing alters the clips after the build). Windowed spawn hitch of "Perf, six with ragdolls": median 28.8 ms before, 25 ms after (limit 33); frame p95 13.1 ms (limit 16.7).

## Art pipeline and look (Milestone 4)

The plan is [milestones/M4-plan.md](milestones/M4-plan.md); this section is the contract as landed. Each package fills only its own heading.

### Character art seams and RigPaint (S0-contract)

The contract scaffold (package S0-contract, phase 0). It lands every seam the art packages build on and changes nothing in the M3 game: with `art == null` a rig builds today's 41 (45 with wrapped hands) primitives, every material is today's `StandardMaterial3D`, and the full M3 suite passes unedited but for the two changes under "Tests" below.

**`CharacterArt`** (`game/actors/character_art.gd`, a `Resource`; one per character in `game/actors/art/<id>_art.tres`): `id: StringName` (&"punk", &"player"), `scene: PackedScene` (the exported GLB), `palette: Texture2D` (the shared atlas), `detail_mask: Texture2D` (player only), `triangle_budget: int` (Spec §10: 15000 enemy, 30000 player).

**Actor and rig seams.**
- `CombatActor.art: CharacterArt` (null = placeholder primitives) and the virtual `_configure_art(r: HumanoidRig)`, which `_ensure_body()` calls once per body right after `_configure_rig(rig)` and before `rig.build()`; the default is `r.art = art`. `PlayerCharacter._configure_rig` never sets art (tests call it on scratch rigs).
- `HumanoidRig.art`, `paint: RigPaint` (made in `_init()`, so a color set before `build()` works), `has_art()`, `art_meshes()`, `set_art_shown(shown)`, and the groups `VIEW_PROXY_GROUP` (`&"view_proxy"`) and `ART_GROUP` (`&"rig_art"`).
- With `art` set, `_build_meshes()` still builds every primitive (names, followers, layers, materials), adds each to `VIEW_PROXY_GROUP`, then calls `SkinnedArt.attach(self, art)` and keeps the result in `art_meshes()`. `set_art_shown(true)` then hides the primitives and shows the art; `false` is the live A/B back (Tuning's `look_show_art`, wired by P1-skin). **If `attach()` returns no meshes** (the S0 stub) the primitives stay visible and `has_art()` is false, so a rig whose art failed to attach never goes invisible; `set_art_shown()` is then a no-op.
- The outline slot: `mesh.material_overlay = paint.outline_material_for(part)` on every primitive (null for now). `next_pass` stays the contact flash's (one slot per material).

**`RigPaint`** (`game/actors/rig_paint.gd`, `RefCounted`, one per rig): everything that paints the body, moved out of `HumanoidRig` verbatim. `materials: Dictionary[int, ShaderMaterial]` (toon since P1-toon) is the same object as `HumanoidRig._materials`. `build()` (was `_build_materials`), `material(paint)`, `art_material()` (null without art; with art, `ToonMaterials.art(palette, detail_mask)`), `outline_material()` and `outline_material_for(part)` (the hulls; see P1-toon's heading), `apply_colors()` (the rig's color setters call it; a no-op until `build()`), `color_of(paint)` (what tests read instead of `albedo_color`), `set_flash(amount, color)` (the whole-body parry emission; the rig ends the contact flash first), `show_flash_overlay(on, overlay)` (the contact flash's `next_pass` on every base material, the art's too), `set_damage_look(level)`, `base_materials()`, `warm_up(parent)` (was `_warm_flash`: windowed only, once per run) and the static `end_warm_up()` (the first contact flash drops the warm-up box). `DARK_COLOR`, `PANTS_DARKEN` and `FLASH_SHADER` moved with it. The rig keeps the contact flash's aim and anchor (`flash_at()`, `follow_flash()`, `_flash_overlay`, `_flash_on`) and delegates the material loops.

**`SkinnedArt`** (`game/actors/skinned_art.gd`): `static attach(rig, art) -> Array[MeshInstance3D]` and `static check(art) -> PackedStringArray`. The S0 stubs return `[]` and `["not implemented"]`; P1-skin implements them.

**`ToonMaterials`** (`game/fx/toon_materials.gd`, static): S0 landed the factories as stubs keeping M3's recipes; P1-toon replaced every body with the toon materials (see its heading below). Call sites: `Weapon._ready()` takes the pipe's material from `prop()` and its `material_overlay` from `outline_for_prop()`; `RoomKit.flat_material()` and `RoomKit.grid_material()` delegate to `environment()` and `grid()` and are typed `-> Material` (`RoomKit.GRID_SHADER` moved to `ToonMaterials.GRID_SHADER`). P1-toon and P1-post replace the bodies, not the call sites.

**Tuning.** A "Look" group sits between "Audio" and "Debug" (M4-plan A9), one subgroup per owner with one documented seed each: Toon `look_mid_level` (0.72), Outline `look_outline_px` (3.75) and `look_hull_on_placeholders` (true, A7; declared only), Edges `look_edges_enabled`, Grade `look_grade_enabled`, FP feel `fp_knockdown_hide_torso` (false, today's behavior). There is no Art subgroup: `look_show_art` (true) is in "Debug", which presets skip by name, so the A/B is never saved. Every `vfx_*` name stays in "VFX" (A9).

**The joint table.** `tools/pipeline/dump_joints.gd` writes `tools/pipeline/joints.json` from `PlaceholderBody`: `bones` (23, parents first: name, parent, `rest_local`, `rest_global`), `points` (the 14 rig points), `soles` (heel, ball, toe tip per side), `fist_boxes`, `hurtboxes`, `fp_hidden_bones`/`fp_hidden_parts`, and `parts_enemy`/`parts_player` (every primitive). Rig space, metres at 1.8 m, +Y up, faces -Z (own right is +X), every rest rotation identity; numbers rounded to a micrometre. It needs no autoload: `godot --headless -s tools/pipeline/dump_joints.gd --quit-after 5`. The Python tools read the file and never write it; `test_the_joint_table_file_matches_placeholder_body` fails when `PlaceholderBody` changes without it.

**Tests.** `tests/test_m4_contracts.gd` pins the signatures, the `art == null` build (41/45 meshes, no overlays, no proxies), the proxy/A-B logic, the hook order, RigPaint's materials, colors, flash and overlay, the stub materials, the Look group and the joint table. Two existing files changed: the damage-look material tests (`tests/test_vfx.gd` 688-835, six tests) moved verbatim into `tests/test_damage_look.gd` (P1-toon's), and `test_the_audio_tuning_group_sits_before_debug` in `tests/test_audio.gd` now expects "Look" between Audio and Debug (it said Debug directly after Audio).

### Look acts and the bench (L0-bench)

Tooling only: no game file. The Punk and the player's limbs are recorded from the same scripted acts before and after each look change, and the machine's frame time is compared across commits.

**The acts** (`tests/bot/acts/look_acts.gd`, run by the crowd bot, titles start "Look:"; one entry of `acts()` and one function each). Every act hides the bot's caption (it comes back at the act's end), freezes the AI, sets god mode and seeds the VFX generator (`VfxDirector.rng.seed = 7`). All motion is scripted: the player's own presses, `force_attack()`, or the Punk's rig played by hand (`_unplug()` takes an actor off the physics tick, `_pose()` places it and advances its rig, because on the AI's tick `_hold_still()` plays the idle over any `play_locomotion()` every tick).

| Slug | Title | Shows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| c1 | `Look: Punk face at 1 m` | Punk 1 m ahead, view +8 degrees, 3 s idle |
| c2 | `Look: Punk body at 1 m` | the same at the default -6 degrees |
| c3 | `Look: Punk strafes at 1 m` | a 150 degree arc at 1.3-1.0-1.3 m, the view following, then a step in and out |
| c4 | `Look: Punk attacks at 1 m` | its two-hit punch, then its shove, landing on the player (0.95 m) |
| c5 | `Look: Punk reacts at 1 m` | the player's jab, P P P P (hook stagger, uppercut launch), the get-up, health 100/65/30% held 1 s each, a jab that finishes it (KO, blink-off) |
| c6 | `Look: distances` | the Punk at 1, 2.5, 5 and 10 m, view level |
| c7 | `Look: lineup` | Punk, Kicker, Thug, Bruiser on a 2.2 m arc |
| c8 | `Look: own limbs` | view -45 degrees: jab, cross, hook, front kick, guard, walking; then -20 (the chain and the guard, where his arms are in the view) and -65 (walking, where the lead leg and the feet come in). Subject: the player; rectangles of his hands and legs |
| c9 | `Look: street tour` | `room_acts`' tour with six frozen enemies in the street |
| c10 | `Look: turntable` | free-fly camera once round the Punk at rest, in the guard, lying (mesh QA, not first person); the hero's own body is hidden for it, the camera is placed before the settle and each quarter is marked after the tick that draws it, so q0 and q360 film the Punk's front (final-v1-07) |
| c11 | `Look: hold the Punk` | grab, three knees (the third lets go), grab, throw |
| c13 | `Look: Punk, impact frame off` | c5 with `impact_frame_enabled` off |

C12 (extremes) is F-motion's; SL-int adds `Look: Perf, six art Punks`. A title must not be inside another title (the capture filter is a substring match; `test_look_acts` checks it).

**The log lines** (`tests/bot/look_rect.gd`, through the bot's `log_line()`):
- `LOOK-MARK <slug> f<frame> <label>`: a frame worth a still. `start` and `end` delimit the act and get none.
- `LOOK-RECT <slug> f<frame> <head|hands|body> x y w h`: 1600x900 pixels, the bounds of spheres round the rig's drawn points (`get_visual_point_transform()`: the ragdoll's pose while one runs) through the current camera, grown 12 px and clipped to the frame; nothing is printed for a region that is off the screen. The player's own body (c8) has no head and leaves out the bones hidden from his lens.
- `f<frame>` is the video frame (from 0) of the state the line describes: `Engine.get_process_frames() - 1`, read as a bot resumes from a `physics_frame` await (before any node ticks, so it is what the last rendered frame drew). It is not the physics tick of the bot's other `f<n>` lines. `LookRect.mark(bot, slug, label, subject)` also prints the subject's rectangles; call it right after a bot await, never after a further `await`.

**`tools/capture_bot.sh`** forwards `CAPTURE_ARGS` (split on spaces) to the bot after `--act=`: `CAPTURE_ARGS="--tune=impact_frame_enabled=0" tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn captures/x "Look: Punk reacts" 6`. Movie Maker records 1600x900 whatever `--resolution` says (project.godot's window override).

**`tools/look_sheets.sh <avi> <out dir> [--log f] [--offset n] [--slug s]...`** reads `bot.log` beside the video and writes, per act, `<slug>/overview_NN.png` (6 fps, 5x4 tiles of 480 px a page), `<slug>/stills/<slug>_f<frame>_<label>.png` (full resolution at every mark) and `<slug>/crops/<slug>_f<frame>_<region>.png` (every `LOOK-RECT` cut out and doubled), plus `index.txt`. The bot's doubled log lines are read once. Frames are cut exactly (`-ss` a quarter frame early; `ffmpeg -nostdin`). Record one act per process (`capture_bot.sh ... "<title>"`): acts of one run are not bit-identical to the same act alone.

**`tools/perf_compare.sh [--act "<title>"]... [--runs N] [--vsync on|off] [--force] [--keep] [--out DIR] <ref> [<ref>...]`** refuses above a 1-minute load average of `PERF_MAX_LOAD` (2.0) unless `--force`; checks each ref out to `.worktrees/perf-<sha>` in the main checkout (removed on exit), copies in `tests/bot/perf_bot.gd` and `.tscn`, imports, and runs each act windowed with vsync off (`--disable-vsync`; `--vsync on` leaves it on), N times per ref (default 3), the refs in turn (a, b, a, b, ...). `perf_bot.gd` (the crowd bot with a recorder over each act it runs) prints `PERF-BENCH {json}`: frames, mean, p95, worst, spawn hitch (worst frame within 0.5 s of any enemy appearing), first spawn, first hit, first finisher (-1 when it did not happen), spawns, hits, stalls (frames of 0.5 s or more), vsync. The report gives each metric as the mean over the runs with min..max, a delta column (last ref minus first) and the A16 soft bars for the last ref (p95 <= 16.7, spawn hitch <= 33, first hit and first spawn <= the first ref + 2 ms). Logs, `results.tsv` and `report.txt` land in `captures/perf_compare/<time>/` (never committed). **Vsync caveat (measured on this M1 in an unattended session):** with vsync off the window stalls for 1.0 s every few seconds (about 15 of 1700 frames; a Metal drawable that is not released), with or without a 120 fps cap; with vsync on there are none (one frame of 1.0 s at the start of a process). The report counts stalls and warns when a run has more than two: then use `--vsync on` (frame times follow the display's refresh) or put the window in front. Needs the machine to itself.

**Tests** (`tests/test_look_acts.gd`): the act list and unique titles, the crowd bot loads the acts by name, `look_rect.gd`'s projection, c1 run headless in the real crowd room (marks, rectangles inside 1600x900, at least 120 ticks, caption hidden during and back after, AI frozen, god mode), the head and hands inside the body rectangle, one `PERF-BENCH` line from the perf bot, and `look_sheets.sh` on a synthetic video whose brightness is its frame number (the still of frame 50 is frame 50).

**The "before" pack:** every act recorded once per process on the M3 look (the game's code of `afdb39e`; this package changes none) into `captures/m4_before/<slug>/` of the main checkout (`bot.avi`, `bot.log`, `sheets/`), never committed; 0 `SCRIPT ERROR`/`ERROR` lines in every log. Frames, stills and crops: c1 290/4/12, c2 290/4/12, c3 392/8/24, c4 298/19/36, c5 795/26/63, c6 440/4/12, c7 290/4/12, c8 696/35/53, c9 1966/12/0, c10 1634/15/45, c11 358/15/39, c13 795/26/63. Later packages re-record the same acts (`capture_bot.sh` and `look_sheets.sh` with the same titles) and compare frame by frame: the marks and labels are the same.

### Tripo and the asset tools (P1-tools)

All four steps of M4-plan.md A2 are in: `tripo.py`, `blender_dump_rig.py`, the mannequin renders and the smoke Punk (sources, T1-T3 and their dumps, 55 credits). None of it touches the game.

**`tools/pipeline/tripo.py`** is the one door for Tripo spending (Part A A10). Stdlib only, run as `python3 -I tools/pipeline/tripo.py <command>`, API v3 at `https://openapi.tripo3d.ai/v3` (v2 shuts down on 2026-10-31).
- *Commands:* `balance`, `ledger [--live]`, `task <id>` (free); `upload <image>`; the task commands `image-to-model`, `multiview`, `rig-check`, `rig`, `texture`; `wait <task_id>`; `--self-test`. A task command takes inputs as `task:<id>`, `file:<token>`, `url:https://...` or a local image (uploaded first; png, jpg or webp, at most 20 MB, at least 256 px). Options go in as `--options file.json` and `--opt key=value`; unknown keys, wrong types, values outside an enum and combinations the API rejects (`fast` without texture v3.5, `generate_parts` with texture, v3 options on v2.5, `unity` on rig v1.0) are refused before any call. `rig` defaults to model `v3.0-20260909`, `rig_type biped`, `spec unity`, `out_format glb` (the API's own default is v1.0). A run that creates a task needs `--label` (the brief's row, e.g. `T1`) and `--spend <credits>`, which must equal the price table's expected cost for that exact request (`rig-check` is `--spend 0`): a missing or different number, `nan` included, is refused before anything is uploaded or created, in the CLI and again in `Tripo.run`, so a label alone can never spend.
- *`--dry-run`* prints the request body, the expected cost and the exact `--spend` to pass, makes no network call (`--live` adds one free balance read) and writes nothing.
- *The price table* is `tools/pipeline/tripo_prices.json`: every credit the script charges and the policy (milestone `M4`, start balance 1500, floor 700, stop 750). A call with no price is refused, and prices the docs do not state (texture v3.5, uploads) are flagged `UNVERIFIED` in the output until the first real `credits_consumed` settles them. There is no flag that loosens the policy.
- *The guard,* before every task: the live balance is read and the task refused if `balance - frozen - expected < 700`, or if `max(ledger spend, start balance - balance) + expected > 750`. The second term makes spend outside the ledger (another worktree, the web app) count.
- *A run* creates the task, polls it, downloads every known output at once (the URLs live 5 minutes) with no Authorization header, https only, into a NEW or EMPTY folder inside the repo (default `assets/source/_cache/tripo/<task_id>/`; files `<task8>_gen.glb`, `_rig.glb`, `_tex.glb`, `_preview.webp`, plus a `task.json`), then appends to the ledger. The POST that creates a task is never retried (a retry could double-spend); only HTTP 429, where nothing was created, is. A task that times out stays `created` in the ledger and counts at its expected cost until `wait <task_id>` finishes it.
- *Keys:* `TRIPO_API_KEY` comes from the environment or `.env` (a linked worktree falls back to the main checkout's `.env`). It is sent only in the Authorization header; every message and ledger line passes through `redact()`, which also masks `GEMINI_API_KEY`.
- *`--self-test`* (offline, a mock service) covers the price table, option validation, the dry run, the floor and the stop (including an unfinished task and untracked spend), the whole lifecycle with its files and ledger lines, failure and timeout and resume, a server that echoes the key in an error, and a grep of the ledger, the mock run's files and every new or changed file for the key values, read in memory.

**`assets/source/tripo_ledger.jsonl`** is append-only JSON lines (M4's T1-T3 are in it: 55 credits). Every line has `v`, `event`, `ts` (UTC), `milestone`. Events: `created` (`task_id`, `call`, `endpoint`, `label`, `options` = the request body, `expected_credits`, `balance_before`, `frozen_before`), `finished` (those fields plus `status`, `credits_consumed` as a float from the task itself, `credits_source`, `balance_delta`, `balance_after`, `frozen_after`, `duration_s`, `result` = the small scalar outputs such as `riggable` and `rig_type`, `files` with repo-relative path, role, bytes and sha256, `error`) and `upload` (`path`, `sha256`, `file_token`). Spend = the sum of `credits_consumed` over `finished` lines plus `expected_credits` of every `created` line not yet finished; `tripo.py ledger --live` reconciles it with the live balance. No URL is ever written.

**`tools/pipeline/blender_dump_rig.py`** dumps a rigged `.glb`, `.gltf`, `.fbx` or `.blend` to `bones.json`: `blender --background --factory-startup --python-exit-code 1 --python tools/pipeline/blender_dump_rig.py -- <in> <out/bones.json>` (never the Blender MCP; one Blender process). It only reads, and its output is deterministic (rounded, ordered, no paths or times). All numbers are in Godot space (x right, y up, the rig faces -z; Blender world `(x, y, z)` becomes `(x, z, -y)`) after every object's transform, so they compare with `joints.json`.
- *Content:* every bone's `name`, `parent`, world `head` and `tail`, `roll_rad`, `length`, `deform`, `weighted_verts` (glTF stores only joint heads, so the importer invents tails: trust heads, parents and names); per mesh the vertex and triangle counts, bbox, per-group `count`, `weight_sum` and `centroid`, `max_influences`, the influence histogram, the `unweighted` count, `over_four`, `not_normalized`, groups with no bone; materials with base colour and image, images with size, packed flag and `dominant_colors` (each channel rounded to 16 levels); a `summary` (triangles, bbox, `height_m`, `feet_y`, `bones_without_vertices`, and `neutral_bone {present, weighted_verts}`: Blender's glTF exporter and Godot's importer add that bone when any vertex has no weight, and the asset contract forbids it).
- *Facing:* `facing.yaw_deg_to_minus_z` is the rotation about +Y that turns the model to face -z. The toe direction is the primary (the long axis of each foot's footprint, signed toward the end that reaches further from the shin above it); the head confirms it (a face reaches further in front of the torso's axis than the skull behind it). `confidence` is `high`, `weak`, `conflict`, `toe_only` or `none`; the thresholds are in the output's `params`.
- *Sides and names:* `sides.consistent` is true when the bones named Left sit on the character's own left (a mirrored label, the codemap's `left` ambiguity, shows as false); `names` lists which of the project's 23 bone names are missing and how many bones match Godot's 56-bone `SkeletonProfileHumanoid`.
- *`--self-test`* builds a small armature-and-mesh character headless (an 18-bone body of subdivided boxes with a nose, one shared vertex, one five-influence vertex, three unweighted vertices, a packed texture), exports it to GLB facing -z, +x and +z (the last with Left and Right swapped), dumps each and the original `.blend`, and checks bones, parents, heads to 0.1 mm, triangles, vertices, weights, neutral_bone, facing, sides and determinism.

**`tools/pipeline/blender_mannequin.py`** renders the joint table as a chunky grey mannequin: `blender --background --factory-startup --python-exit-code 1 --python tools/pipeline/blender_mannequin.py -- assets/source/mannequin/mannequin.json assets/source/mannequin`. Every number (the pose, the hand and nose shapes, the torso loft, the voxel melt, the colours, the camera, the views) is in `assets/source/mannequin/mannequin.json`; the geometry is `tools/pipeline/joints.json`'s enemy parts (read-only), so the mannequin fills the rig's own volumes.
- *Pose:* a 45 degree A-pose, each upper arm swung about the rig's Z axis to 45 degrees below horizontal, the forearm and hand straight along it, the legs parallel at x = +-0.11. The torso (pelvis to upper chest) is one smooth superellipse loft through the boxes' widths and depths, the hands are open flattened capsules with a thumb (A13), and a small nose shows the front in the side view; the parts are melted by a voxel remesh and smoothed.
- *Output:* `mannequin_front.png`, `mannequin_left.png`, `mannequin_back.png` (1536x2048, 3:4 at 2K, orthographic, 952.56 pixels per metre, flat #d9d9d9 ground, Workbench studio lighting) and `mannequin_report.json`: per view the pixel position of every bone head, the silhouette's bounding box, a hash of the decoded pixels and a check that the ground is exactly #d9d9d9. A rerun gives the same report. The front view puts the character's own right on the image's left; the left view shows its own left side, facing the image's left.

**`tools/pipeline/blender_views.py`** renders any `.glb`, `.gltf` or `.blend` from four sides as one textured contact sheet (`... -- <in> <out.png> [tile_px]`; the tiles name where the camera stands in glTF space: +Z, +X, -Z, -X). It is how a generated model's facing and pose are seen before a step touches it.

**The smoke Punk's sources** are in `assets/source/characters/punk/smoke/`, with their provenance and every measured fact in its `source.json` (the files, hashes, T1-T3 ids and credits, and `findings`). A Gemini flat front view of concept A on the mannequin (`smoke_jobs.json`, `punk_smoke_front.jpg`), the T1 generation GLB, the T3 rigged GLB, `bones.json` (the rig's dump) and `bones_gen.json`, and `views_gen.png`, `views_rig.png`. What the chain must know of Tripo's output, as measured:
- *Facing:* +X in glTF space (not the standard +Z and not the rig's -Z); `facing_yaw_deg` 89.33 turns it to -Z. The dump's confidence reads `weak` here only because the Punk's cap is worn backwards.
- *Side label:* `Left_*` is the character's own left (no swap).
- *Scale:* 1.000 m tall, not real-world (generation GLB centred at y 0, rig GLB with the feet at y 0); arm span 0.784 m. Uniform 1.8 to the rig.
- *Bones:* 67, `Hips` is the root (no `Root`), names `Left_UpperArm` style (strip the underscore after Left and Right for the project's names); extras are `Neck_Twist_A`, five fingers of four bones per hand, `*_ToesEnd` (weighted) and `Left_Eye`, `Right_Eye` (no weight). 19,510 triangles, one mesh, every vertex weighted, at most four influences, no `neutral_bone`.
- *Pose:* upper arms 38 degrees and forearms 41 degrees from vertical, the legs splay about 10 degrees; the joint heads sit on a 1/512 m grid, so rebuild the armature from the table and use them as landmarks (`source.json` has each one beside the table's rest joint).
- *Cost:* texture v3.5 on generation is 30.00 credits, the standard tier.

### The Blender chain and the asset contract (P1-chain)

One command takes a source model to the shipped GLB: `python3 -I tools/pipeline/run.py --asset <id>` (or `--config <pipeline.json>`), steps in `tools/pipeline/`, each one a headless `blender --background --factory-startup --python <step>.py -- <pipeline.json> [mode]` (never the Blender MCP; one process at a time). `run.py` is standard-library Python 3.9; numpy lives only in the Blender steps. Options: `--from STEP`, `--only STEP`, `--no-stills`, `--no-import`, `--set a.b=<json>` (the effective config is kept in `work/`), `--check` (rebuild into `assets/source/_cache/check_<id>/` and compare the report hash), `--list`. A step name that is not one of the eight (`--from smoke`: `smoke` is the asset's source folder, not a step) prints the usage line and exits 2 before anything is written. The reproducibility command is `--check`, not a step: `run.py --asset punk --check` (the Tripo weights) and the same with `--set rig.weights="auto" --set paths.report=assets/source/characters/punk/report_auto --set paths.export_name=punk_auto` (the auto weights) each rebuild into `assets/source/_cache/check_punk/` and compare the report hash with the committed `report/` or `report_auto/`. Details and the numbers measured on the probe and the smoke Punk are in [milestones/M4-calls/P1-chain.md](milestones/M4-calls/P1-chain.md); the gate's fixes are in [milestones/M4-calls/SG-chain.md](milestones/M4-calls/SG-chain.md).

**The asset contract (what SkinnedArt can rely on in every `assets/export/characters/<id>/*.glb` and `tests/fixtures/art/*.glb`; `tests/test_art_contract.gd` checks all of it).**
- *Scene:* `Armature` > `Skeleton3D` > `MeshInstance3D` `Body` (the Punk) or `Body` + `Body__fp_hidden` (a player body with the first-person split), meshes at identity, one surface each.
