Exactly the 23 `BONE_NAMES`, every Skin bind named, no `neutral_bone`. Bone order is Blender's: index the Skin's bind list, not the skeleton.
- *The rest is the A-pose and it is chained (Part A A3).* Every exported joint is the table's (`tools/pipeline/joints.json`) except the arm chains, which are one straight limb along the mesh's measured direction `d` (a unit vector, hanging = `(0, -1, 0)` in Godot space): `p_LowerArm = p_UpperArm + |table|*d`, `p_Hand = p_LowerArm + |table|*d`, `p_UpperArm` on the table, within 1 mm (they are within 0.001 mm). The Skin's inverse binds and the skeleton's rest agree. `pipe_common.chain_joints()` is the rule. A variant `<id>_rest.glb` is the same body baked arms-down (d = hanging, so `R = identity` in the bind formula); the two routes share one code path.
- *Space:* metres at 1.8 m, +Y up, faces -Z, soles within 5 mm of y = 0 over heel z +0.085 ... toe z -0.25. At most 4 normalised influences per vertex, every vertex weighted, nothing on Root.
- *Attributes:* UV1 `palette` (every face's three vertices on one swatch centre of the atlas), UV2 `detail` only when the config asks (the player's ink mask), COLOR `hull_normal` = the position-averaged smooth normal `n * 0.5 + 0.5` (area-weighted, across both parts, never from the caps), so inverted-hull outlines do not crack. **It is in the GLB's Y-up axes, like NORMAL**: Blender's glTF exporter turns POSITION and NORMAL from Z-up but passes a colour attribute through, so `blender_export.write_hull_normals` turns the normal itself, `(x, y, z) -> (x, z, -y)`, before the encode (it was baked in Blender's axes until the slice gate: the ink vanished on every top and bottom silhouette). `test_art_contract` checks per mesh that the decoded COLOR agrees with NORMAL (mean dot 0.92-0.96, bar 0.85) and beats the permuted reads. No materials, no images in the GLB.
- *Budget:* the Punk at most 15,000 triangles, the player 30,000 (probe bodies stand in for the player).
- *Fists:* the hands are replaced by Blender-made fists filling the table's fist box (area-weighted centroid within 15 mm of the box at `FistPoint`, extents within 20%), weighted 100% Hand, their cuff blended into `LowerArm`.
- *The first-person split (when `parts.split_fp.enabled`):* `Body__fp_hidden` = Chest and above, both Shoulders and the shoulder half of each upper arm, cut by planes (a horizontal one at the Chest joint, one across each upper arm), so every loop is clean; `Body` has no open loop left (every cut is closed by an inward rounded cap; the wrist holes inside the fist cuffs too). Both parts keep all 23 groups and the same Skin.
- *`.import`:* `meshes/generate_lods=false`, `meshes/create_shadow_meshes=true`, `skins/use_named_skins=true`, `animation/import=false` (set by `run.py`, committed).
- *The binds SkinnedArt builds* (A3, verified in `tools/pipeline/pose_lib.py`, the numpy mirror used for the stills): `R_n` = shortest arc from the rig's rest direction (bone to its first child, from the table) to the GLB's `p_child - p_n`, leaves take their parent's, Root and Hips identity; `bind'_n = Scale(s) * (R_n, p_n)^-1`, `p_n` = the Skin bind's inverse origin. The stills in `report/stills/` are skinned with exactly that.

**The palette atlas** (`assets/export/palette/palette_atlas.png`, built by `tools/pipeline/gen_palette.py` from `assets/source/palette/*.json`, standard library, byte-reproducible; the lead reruns it after merges): 256x16 RGBA8, 8 px swatches, slot `s` at column `s % 32`, row `s // 32` (0-15 shared, 16-31 punk, 32-47 player, 48-63 reserved). RGB is the sRGB colour; the alpha is the swatch's damage class: **1.0 takes the clothes' marks** (`damage_tex`: scuffs, then blood), **0.8 takes the skin's marks** (`damage_skin_tex`: bruises, then blood; `"skin": true` in the palette JSON, which needs `marks`; shared slots 6-9), **0.5 takes none** (today's DARK). The shader reads `step(0.75, a)` for "takes marks" and `1 - step(0.9, a)` for skin. A **glossy** swatch (`"gloss": true`, never with `skin`) is one 8-bit step lower in its class, 254, 203 or 127 (`GLOSS_ALPHA_STEP` in `gen_palette.py`): the thresholds read it as the same class, and `toon.gdshader`'s `swatch_gloss(alpha)` reads "the class's alpha minus exactly one step" as the per-swatch gloss flag (`ToonMaterials.swatch_glossy(alpha)` mirrors it; any other alpha is matte). The Punk's lens (slot 19) is the only glossy swatch; the vest, jeans, cap, laces, tank, gloves, skin, wraps and boots are matte (`test_toon` reads the atlas, the JSON and the four Punk GLBs' faces). The swatch centre UV is `((col + 0.5)/32, (row + 0.5)/2)`. Unused slots are magenta at alpha 0.5. Nearest filter, no mipmaps, no VRAM compression (the `.import` says so). `python3 -I tools/pipeline/gen_palette.py --check` fails on a stale atlas.

**The steps** (state is carried in `work/NN_*.blend`; every step writes `work/<step>.report.json`; `run.py` aggregates them into `report/report.json`):
1. `inspect` (`blender_inspect.py`): imports with `blender_dump_rig.py`'s facing and side checks, flattens every mesh to world space, joins, welds the importer's split vertices, folds the rig's weights onto the 23 names (`Left_UpperArm` style names and fingers, jaw, eyes folded into their nearest mapped ancestor) as `tw_<Bone>` groups, turns the model to face +Y (Blender) and verifies the turn, stores the rig's joint heads as landmarks.
2. `fit` (`blender_fit.py`): similarity (scale, place) from the landmarks, soles to z = 0, the arm swing measured per side, optional `fit.regularize` of the torso landmarks, then the proportion warp: each bone maps its source segment onto its target segment, rotating and stretching along the bone only, blended by weight; reports stretch, rotation and the nearest-vertex landmark residual.
3. `rig` (`blender_rig.py`): the 23-bone armature on the chained table and the weights (`tripo` = the source's, `auto` = Blender's heat map), fill, limit 4, normalise.
4. `parts_replace` (`blender_parts.py replace`): the fists.
5. `palette` (`blender_palette.py`): swatch labels, border-protected decimation to the budget, palette UVs, optional detail UV.
6. `parts_split` (`blender_parts.py split`): the first-person split and caps.
7. `export` (`blender_export.py`): hull normals, GLBs (the A-pose default and the arms-down bake), each read back and checked.
8. `stills` (`blender_stills.py`): Workbench stills and strain numbers at the rig's own poses, per bind route, against the GLB's rest and the skinned rest pose, with the worst edges.

**`pipeline.json`** (per asset, `assets/source/characters/<id>/pipeline.json`; the probe's is `tests/fixtures/art/probe_pipeline.json`): `paths` (`work`, `report`, `export_dir`, `export_name`, optional `blend_dir` and `report_json`), `source` (`mesh`, `rig` or "same", `facing_yaw_deg` override, `sides` auto/keep/swap), `target.head_top_m`, `fit` (`landmarks` rig/config/table, `bone_map`, `swing`, `regularize`, `warp_weights`, `residual_limit_m`, `sole_percentile`), `rig` (`weights`, `max_influences`, `smooth_iterations`, `variants` apose/rest), `parts.fists`, `parts.split_fp`, `palette` (`slots`, `max_swatches`, `min_share`, `majority_passes`, border relax, `budget_tris`, `detail_uv`), `stills`. No numbers live in the Blender scripts but the algorithms' own constants. A new character: copy `punk/pipeline.json`, set its source, palette slots and budget, add its palette JSON, run.

**The probe body** (`tests/fixtures/art/probe_body.glb`, `probe_body_rest.glb`, report and config beside them; `python3 -I tools/pipeline/make_probe_body.py` rebuilds them): a synthetic Tripo-like delivery (a voxel-remeshed capsule body with a painted texture and an auto-weighted Unity-named rig with extras, off the table in scale, facing, swing and proportions) put through the whole chain, deterministic (the same report hash on a rerun), with `Body` + `Body__fp_hidden`, UV2 and COLOR. P1-skin and F-player-int use it before a real GLB exists.

**Tests.** `tests/test_art_contract.gd` (14 tests, about 1,300 assertions): the contract above on the fixtures and every character GLB, the palette atlas against its JSON, every exported file named in ASSETS.md, the probe fixtures against their committed report (their sha256 and a landmark residual under 1 cm). Part A A1, A2, A3 (swatches and red band), A5, A13.

### Skinned art on the rig, view proxies, ragdoll write-back (P1-skin)

`SkinnedArt` (`game/actors/skinned_art.gd`) turns a `CharacterArt` into skinned meshes on the rig's own skeleton. `HumanoidRig._build_meshes()` calls `attach()` after the primitives, which stay built as the view proxies (M4-plan A4). Only a GLB's meshes, its vertex weights and its joint positions are used; its own `Skeleton3D` never goes under the rig and its inverse binds are read for their joint positions only.

**`attach(rig, art) -> Array[MeshInstance3D]`.** Nothing (and no warning) when the art has no scene. Otherwise it checks the art (below) and, on any problem, adds nothing and pushes one warning, so a rig whose art breaks the contract keeps its primitives visible. Each GLB mesh becomes a child of `rig.skeleton` named `Art<Mesh>` (`ArtBody`, `ArtBody__fp_hidden`) with an identity transform, `skeleton = ^".."`, the rig's `Skin`, `material_override = rig.paint.art_material()` (so `base_materials()` holds it and the flash passes reach it), `material_overlay = rig.paint.outline_material()`, `cast_shadow` on, group `HumanoidRig.ART_GROUP`. A GLB mesh named `*__fp_hidden` carries the meta `SkinnedArt.FP_HIDDEN_META`; `HumanoidRig._apply_layers()` (run again after the attach, and by the `first_person_body` setter) puts it on `VIS_FP_HIDDEN` on a first-person body and on the default layer otherwise. The art meshes are in `HumanoidRig._art_meshes` (`art_meshes()`), never in `_head_meshes`, which `set_art_shown()` swaps with the art.

**The bind route (A3), one code path.** The GLB's rest is the table (arms hanging) or an A-pose whose arm chains are swung. For each bone `n`, `R_n` is the shortest arc from the table's direction (the bone to its first child in `PlaceholderBody`, from `pose_globals(<typed empty dict>, ZERO, 1.0)`) to the GLB's direction `p_child - p_n`; leaves take their parent's `R`, Root and Hips take identity. The Skin keeps the GLB's bind order (the mesh's bone indices point into it) and holds `bind'_n = Scale(s) * Transform3D(Basis(R_n), p_n).affine_inverse()`, `s = rig.rig_scale()` (1.0, 1.139, 1.278 for the Punk, Bruiser, boss), `p_n = bind_n.affine_inverse().origin`. A mesh authored at the table has `R = identity` and the binds are translations. One Skin per mesh and rig scale, shared by the rigs of the same art (`SkinnedArt.skin_of()`); one cache entry per art (`entry_of()`, `clear_cache()`, weakly keyed). `swings()` and `chain_residuals()` are the pure functions of the route.

**`check(art) -> PackedStringArray`** (empty = ok; cached with the entry): no art or scene; no palette atlas; no `MeshInstance3D`, an `ArrayMesh` without a skin; a bind named `neutral_bone` (never aliased), outside the 23 `BONE_NAMES`, doubled or missing; a joint more than 1 mm (`JOINT_TOLERANCE_M`) from the table chained through the swing (`|p_c - (p_n + R_n * (table_c - table_n))|`, the rule A3 asks for in place of "joints on the table"; a direction opposite the table's is refused); no UV1; 8 influences; a vertex with no weight, a weights sum off 1 by more than 1%, a weight on Root or past the binds; more triangles than `art.triangle_budget` (a budget of 0 fails any mesh). The landmark-warp residual of the mesh is P1-chain's report, not this check's.

**CUSTOM0/CUSTOM1.** Each surface is rebuilt once with the authored vertex position in `ARRAY_CUSTOM0` and the normal in `ARRAY_CUSTOM1` (RGB_FLOAT, `ARRAY_FORMAT_CUSTOM0/1_SHIFT`), for P1-toon's damage look: the marks' coordinates are mesh data, so they hold still on a moving body.

**Ragdoll write-back (A5).** `Ragdoll._process_modification_with_delta` writes the share-adjusted, eased local rotation of every bone, and the Hips and Root positions, back into the skeleton when `rig.has_art()`. The skin then draws exactly what the followers show (the blended head and arms of a stagger, the eased launch); the skeleton restores its own pose after the update, so nothing leaks into the next tick's FK. A primitive rig runs the M3 path bit for bit.

**`look_show_art`.** `HumanoidRig` reads `Tuning.data.look_show_art` when the art attaches and listens to `Tuning.changed` (the property, or `&""` after an apply) to call `set_art_shown()`, so the Debug-group A/B is live on every rig with art. `set_shown(false)` (the KO blink) hides the art with the rig. `FirstPersonCamera._head_clear_of` skips `ART_GROUP` meshes (their bounding box is the bind pose's) and keeps reading the hidden primitives, which follow the body.

**Measuring the first-person view (A4).** The proxies stay: `tests/fp_view.gd` gets `is_drawn(mesh)` (false for `ART_GROUP`; a `VIEW_PROXY_GROUP` mesh counts while its bone follower is visible, hidden itself or not; any other mesh by `is_visible_in_tree()`), used by `hits()`, `nearest_mesh()` and `outside_view()`; `mesh_distance()` is INF for a mesh that is no primitive; `depths()` returns each cell's depth beside `hits()`. `tests/skinned_view.gd` measures the art itself:
- `skin_vertices(mi, rig, globals = [])` and `skin_normals()`: CPU linear-blend skinning with the rig's Skin (one native `Transform3D * PackedVector3Array` per bind, the influences accumulated); `faces()` the plain triangle soup; `bone_vertices()`.
- **The pose.** Every call below skins `SkinnedView.drawn_pose(rig)` unless it is handed `globals`: the ragdoll's visual pose per bone (`rig.get_ragdoll().visual_pose(i)`, which an art rig writes back into its skeleton) while `rig.is_ragdolling()`, the FK `rig.animation_pose(i)` otherwise. `fk_pose(rig)` is the FK pose whatever a ragdoll shows (`hurtbox_fit()` skins in it, the hurtboxes being FK). `hits()`, its triangle soups and their pose key, and so `compare_with_proxies()` and `compare_with_exact_proxies()` (the proxies' bone followers show the ragdoll's pose too), measure the body that is on screen in a stagger or a launch.
- `hits(root, cam, cols = 48) -> Array[Dictionary]`: the 48x27 rays against a native `TriangleMesh` per art mesh, `{mesh, bone (the dominant bone of the hit face), depth}` or `{}` per cell; cached per physics frame, pose, skin and camera. The triangles whose three vertices sit on one bind go in a group per bind, skinned by one native transform each, the blended ones vertex by vertex; groups wholly outside the view are left out; rays that miss the mesh's box are not cast. `TriangleMesh.intersect_ray()` reports a normal that always faces the ray, so front and back faces are told from the soup's winding (`_is_front()`).
- `compare_with_proxies()` (against `FPView.hits()`), `compare_with_exact_proxies()` (against the proxies' own meshes ray-cast the same way) and `framing_problems(cmp, label, nearest_limit = 0.02)`: row A9 (at most 3% of cells differ, share of the view within 4 points, the art nowhere 2 cm nearer; the nearest figure is the second-largest gap, so one cell on a silhouette edge is no bulge).
- `strain(mi, rig, globals, squash_below, with_triangles, rest) -> Dictionary` and `worst_lines()`: every edge's posed length over its rest length (the authored mesh at the rig's scale), the p0.1 and p99.9 over all edges (edges on one bind are exactly 1 and not measured; edges shorter than 0.1 mm are not either), min, max, NaN, the lowest vertex, and with `with_triangles` the triangles under 15% of their rest area (`squashed`) and the flipped ones (`flipped`). A triangle is folded when its winding's geometric normal opposes the mean of its corners' skinned vertex normals (`folded_mask()`); it is **flipped** when it is folded in the pose and was not at the skinned rest (`rest_pose(rig)`, or `rest`), so the mesh's own folded triangles are a baseline, never charged to a pose (`rest_folded`; `folded_triangles(mi)` counts the authored mesh's, about 159 on `punk.glb`, and `folded_triangles(mi, rig, globals)` those at a pose). A triangle folded at the rest and right in the pose is `flipped_healed`: no defect, not in `flipped`. `tests/test_art_punk.gd` measures the same flips and the squash against the skinned rest (P1-chain C13), and prints the baseline.
- `hurtbox_fit(rig)` (row A6) and `surface_gap(mi, rig, point)` (row A11's anchor distance, brute force).
A drawn-art measurement costs about 12-14 ms for a 15k-triangle mesh: it runs in `test_skinned_art`, `test_art_player` and one windowed evidence act, never in a long headless act or the perf act.

**Fixtures.** `tests/fixtures/skinned_probe_art.gd` builds a `CharacterArt` in code: the placeholder's own primitives baked into one mesh (`Body`, and `Body__fp_hidden` for the player), skinned to 23 named binds in reverse skeleton order with a bind roll of their own, authored at the table (`REST`) or with the arms swung 45 degrees (`A_POSE`), weighted rigid or with a gentle smooth blend. The art is then the primitive body: a skinned vertex equals the same primitive's vertex at any pose, and the first-person framing differs from the proxies only by tessellation (and FPView's own looseness, below). P1-chain's `tests/fixtures/art/probe_body.glb` (A-pose) and `probe_body_rest.glb` (arms-down bake) are the third and fourth fixtures. `tests/fixtures/skin_punk.tscn` is a Street Punk wearing the probe, for the acts.

**Tests (`tests/test_skinned_art.gd`, 41).** Attach and layers; the bind formula at 1.8, 2.05 and 2.3 m for both authoring poses (and the control that translation-only binds leave an A-pose arm 0.2 m off); exact skinning against the primitives at the rest and at idle, jab, flinch, knockdown and block; `check()` for each violation (a neutral bone, an unknown or missing bind, a joint 5 mm off the chain, no weight, weight on Root, an unnormalized sum, over budget, no palette, no UV, 8 influences); CUSTOM0/1; the A/B and the KO blink; `FPView`; the ragdoll gap (the skeleton within 1 mm and 0.001 rad of `Ragdoll._visual` in a stagger, a launch out of a stagger and a full ragdoll; a primitive rig's skeleton untouched; the followers equal with and without art); the first-person framing at the player's 13 poses and the Punk's 1 m poses; the 15 ms cost of an exact sample; the contact flash anchor; `_head_clear_of`; the hurtbox fit; the strain run (A19) over the 46 animations, the Punk's three moves and six peak flexes on three fixtures, under 25 s, printing the worst ten, and its controls (a wide blend, a sunken body); the real GLBs' contract, binds, soles and a reported strain run.

### Toon shader, hull, flash and damage on toon (P1-toon)

Every lit surface of the game is one toon shader, and every character and prop has an ink hull. The calls and the measurements are in [milestones/M4-calls/P1-toon.md](milestones/M4-calls/P1-toon.md); the targets are [milestones/M4-look-bible.md](milestones/M4-look-bible.md).

**Shaders** (`game/fx/`):
- `toon_light.gdshaderinc`: the one `light()`, included by `toon.gdshader` and by `game/level/grid.gdshader` (the floor), both with `render_mode ambient_light_disabled`, so the ramp owns the shadow side and the sky never lifts it. Light is `dot(NORMAL, LIGHT) * ATTENUATION` (the shadow map's term is in it). Below `band_1` a surface is in shadow (`shadow_level` of the lit value, its hue shifted `shadow_tint_amount` toward `shadow_tint` with its luminance kept, so never black), below `band_2` in the mid band (`mid_level`), above it lit (the albedo as authored: the light's color is `LIGHT_COLOR / PI` capped at 1). Band edges are `band_soft` pixels wide at 1080p (`fwidth` of the light, scaled by the viewport height). One hard highlight (`spec_cut`, x`spec_gloss` on glossy materials; on the skinned art it is **per swatch**, the final review's final-v1-04: `toon.gdshader` defines `TOON_SWATCH_SPEC` before the include and sets two varyings in `fragment()`, `swatch_spec` (the share of the highlight the surface takes: 1 for a flat-painted material, whose own `spec_gloss` decides; on the art 1 on a glossy swatch of the atlas and 0 on a matte one, so denim, cotton, cap and skin never show it) and `swatch_glint` (1 on a glossy swatch); `light()` multiplies the sun's highlight by `swatch_spec`, and on a glossy swatch adds the **glint**: a second hard highlight from the camera-fixed `fill_dir` (`dot(NORMAL, normalize(fill_dir + VIEW))` above `glint_cut`, scaled by `glint_strength` and the glossy factor), because a visor that faces the lens never meets the sun's half vector, so without it the lens would never glint; **the glint is gated** (re-score rs-01: the cap is an angle, 28 degrees against the fill's half vector's 26 off the view axis, so a head tipped back into the cone clipped the whole lens to white): `toon.gdshader` sets a third varying, `glint_gate`, to 1 and, for an art with a detail mask, to `texture(detail_tex, UV2).g` cut at 0.5 with a one-pixel edge, and `light()` multiplies the glint by it, so the glint can only light the two authored streaks per eye the Punk's mask carries in G on the lens's texels, never the whole lens; a flat-painted surface or an art without a mask keeps its whole glint; a shader that does not define the macro, the floor's grid, keeps the one highlight), a stepped rim where the light reaches (`rim_*`, characters only), optional screen-space halftone dots in the shadow band (`halftone_*`, off).
- `toon.gdshader`: `albedo` (a flat paint, or the tint of the atlas), `albedo_tex` + `use_albedo_tex` (the palette atlas, `filter_nearest`; the alpha is the damage class: 1.0 clothes marks, 0.8 skin marks, 0.5 none), `detail_tex` + `use_detail_tex` + `detail_strength` (a mask on UV2, R = ink: `color = mix(color, ink, mask)`, never shading; any art may have one, and the Punk and the hero both ship with one) and `detail_sharp` + `detail_cut` (the final review's J14 fix: with `detail_sharp` on, the mask is cut, `smoothstep(cut - e, cut + e, mask)` with `e` half its own `fwidth()`, instead of mixed in by its bilinear value, so a line one texel wide stays a pixel-wide-edged line when it is magnified to 3 to 9 pixels at 0.2 to 0.4 m; the linear mix is the default, `ToonMaterials.art(atlas, detail, sharp_detail)` turns the cut on for an art whose resource asks for it in its metadata (`detail_sharp`: `player_art.tres` and, since the wave-2 punk-detail fix, `punk_art.tres`; `PlayerCharacter.worn_art()`'s copy keeps it and `RigPaint.art_material()` reads it); `look_detail_cut` (Look > Toon, 0.15; 0 turns it off) is the cut, and 0.35 to 0.5 would break the wraps' strands into dashes; `ToonMaterials.detail_ink()` mirrors the math for the tests), `ink`, `flash_color` x `flash_amount` (the parry flash, added as EMISSION after the lighting), `damage_tex` + `damage_skin_tex` + `damage_scale` + `marks` + `use_rest_custom` (a triplanar of the damage texture over `CUSTOM0/1`, the rest position and normal, for the art, or the part's own vertex space for a primitive; on the art a skin swatch (alpha 0.8) takes `damage_skin_tex` where the clothes take `damage_tex`, and a primitive never reads the skin one; `damage_skin_tex` is `hint_default_transparent`, so an unfilled material shows a clean skin), and `edge_tag` (written as ROUGHNESS: 0 on a surface with its own hull, 1 elsewhere; the edge pass skips pixels below 0.5). Every float is a `hint_range` uniform fed from Tuning.
- `toon_outline.gdshader` (`unshaded, cull_front`): the inverted hull. The vertex is pushed along a direction by `width_px * 2 * clamp(d, near_m, far_m) / (1080 * PROJECTION_MATRIX[1][1])` metres (the same pixels at 1080p on any window size or field of view; thinner beyond `far_m`), times `fp_scale` on the player's own body, faded to 0 within `lens_fade_m` of the lens and full from twice that. The direction (uniform `mode`, a function of position alone, so no cracks): 0 the vertex normal (spheres, capsules), 1 `sign(VERTEX)` per axis (boxes), 2 radial in xz plus `sign(y)` (cylinders), 3 `normalize(VERTEX)` (a dome's rim), 4 the art's smooth normal baked in COLOR (`n * 0.5 + 0.5`, the GLB's Y-up axes, rest pose), turned by the shortest arc from the rest normal (`CUSTOM1`) to the skinned normal so it follows the bent body. The width is 0 in an orthographic pass, because a hull on `material_overlay` also draws into the directional light's shadow map and would fatten the shadow (measured +50% at 40 px). The game has no orthographic camera.

**`ToonMaterials`** (`game/fx/toon_materials.gd`, static) makes every material: `character(color, takes_marks := false, glossy := false)` (a primitive paint; `glossy` means it takes the hard highlight, times `look_spec_gloss`), `art(atlas, detail := null, sharp_detail := false)` (the glossy kind: its `spec_gloss` is `look_spec_gloss`, which only a glossy swatch of the atlas takes), `prop(color)` (the pipe: glossy, no rim), `environment(color, hulled := false)` (matte, two bands; `hulled` puts the box hull on the material's `next_pass`), `grid()`, `outline()` (the art's hull), `outline_for_part(shape, first_person := false)` and `outline_for_prop()` (a primitive's or the pipe's hull for its `material_overlay`, one shared material per mode and first-person flag, null when `look_hull_on_placeholders` is off), `warm_up(parent, skinned := null)`. Each is a ShaderMaterial of the one shader, tracked weakly; `Tuning.changed` (a `look_*` name or a whole-set change) gives every live one the Look values again: `apply_look()`. The room's and floor's `mid_level` is 1 (two bands) and their `spec_strength` 0; a primitive paint that is not glossy takes `spec_strength` 0 too (the art's rule: no hard highlight on a matte surface), so only the dark paint (shades, belt, soles, hair, gloves), the pipe and the art's glossy swatches take the highlight; the rim is the characters' and the art's alone; halftone follows `look_halftone_env` / `look_halftone_chars`; the hulls take `look_outline_*`. Mirrors for the tests: `outline_width_m(dist, px, proj11, viewport_h, near, far, lens_fade, fp_scale)`, `band_shade(...)`, `swatch_glossy(alpha)`, `detail_ink(mask, gradient, cut, sharp)`, `hull_direction(mode, vertex, normal)`. `ToonMaterials.INK` is `HudStyle.INK`: one ink for the hull, the detail lines and the HUD.

**Where the hull goes.** On a character's primitive it is the mesh's `material_overlay` (`RigPaint.outline_material_for(part)`, by shape; the player's own body gets the `look_outline_fp_scale` variant); on the art, `RigPaint.outline_material()`. `next_pass` stays the contact flash's (every base material's, as before), so the hull and the flash never share a slot. A hidden mesh draws no hull (`set_shown(false)`, a KO's blink-off, hides both), and the first-person-hidden meshes keep their hulls on the hidden layer the lens never draws. A prop box in the room (crate, dumpster, car) takes `environment(color, true)`, which the room calls; the pipe takes `prop()` and `outline_for_prop()` in `Weapon._ready()`. Whether a placeholder has a hull is read when it is built.

**RigPaint on toon.** `materials` is a `Dictionary[int, ShaderMaterial]` (also `HumanoidRig._materials`): one toon material per `PlaceholderBody.Paint`, per rig, the damage texture slot filled from the build (clear), DARK glossy and mark-free. `color_of(paint)` reads the `albedo` uniform. `set_flash(amount, color)` sets `flash_color`/`flash_amount` on every base material; `show_flash_overlay()` is unchanged (`next_pass`); `art_material()` is `ToonMaterials.art(palette, detail_mask)`, one per rig, in `base_materials()`. `set_damage_look(level)` swaps `damage_tex`, `damage_skin_tex` (the art's) and `damage_scale` only (`DamageLook.apply()` on the primitives; `DamageLook.apply_art()` on the art: `damage_tex` = the clothes' tile, scuffs then blood, and `damage_skin_tex` = the skin paint's tile, bruised then bloodied, the tiles the primitives' skin wears; `prepare()` fills both slots clear on the primitives), never the shader. `RigPaint.FLASH_SHADER` is the one preload of `hit_flash.gdshader`.

**Tuning** ("Look", subgroups Toon and Outline; `docs/milestones/M4-look-bible.md` has the targets): Toon `look_mid_level` 0.72, `look_band_1` 0.25, `look_band_2` 0.62, `look_band_soft` 1.2 (px), `look_shadow_level` 0.42, `look_shadow_tint` (0.36, 0.32, 0.55), `look_shadow_tint_amount` 0.45, `look_spec_cut` 0.99, `look_spec_strength` 0.5, `look_spec_gloss` 3, `look_glint_cut` 0.81 (the lens glint's cut on the camera-fixed light's half vector, within 36 degrees; lower is a bigger glint; since the streak gate it decides only WHEN the lens glints, so it was lowered from 0.88 to show the streaks in a front view) and `look_glint_strength` 1 (0 turns the glint off), `look_rim_cut` 0.7, `look_rim_power` 3, `look_rim_strength` 0.35, `look_halftone_env` / `look_halftone_chars` off, `look_halftone_cell_px` 6, `look_halftone_strength` 0.5. Outline `look_outline_px` 3.75, `look_outline_near_m` 0.3, `look_outline_far_m` 12, `look_outline_fp_scale` 0.8, `look_outline_lens_fade_m` 0.1, `look_hull_on_placeholders` true.

**Warm-up.** `ToonMaterials.warm_up()` (windowed, once per run, called from `RigPaint.warm_up()`'s gate): a toon box under every hull mode, and `skinned` (an art instance) with the art's material and hull, on a holder that is hidden for the call's first two frames and then stands in the camera's view (`WARM_DISTANCE_M` ahead, `WARM_DROP_M` below, scaled to the camera's near distance: a speck, since the draw builds the pipelines and the picture is not wanted) for two frames, and is freed. The camera is read on the second frame, not the first: `process_frame` fires before any node's `_process`, so on a room's first frame the `FirstPersonCamera` is still at the world origin with no turn, and a holder placed from it lands under the floor behind the view, where the renderer culls it and builds nothing (slice-g1-03). SL-int passes the Punk's art instance at the room's load.

**Tests.** `tests/test_toon.gd` (shaders and pinned uniforms, the width and band mirrors, the hull directions over the rig's real meshes, the Look values pushed to every kind, the registry, flash and damage on toon, `set_shown`, the first-person layers, the UV2 detail mask and the art fixture), `tests/test_damage_look.gd` (the six moved tests on the uniforms, and the skin tiles on the art), and the material-type fallout in `test_hit_flash.gd`, `test_humanoid_rig.gd` and `test_m4_contracts.gd`, each change logged old to new in the calls file. `tests/fixtures/toon_art_probe.gd` is a procedural two-bone art with every channel (palette UV, UV2, COLOR, CUSTOM0/1); `tests/bot/acts/toon_acts.gd` has the "Look: toon ..." acts for the crowd bot (the Punk and dummies at 1 and 3 m, hit and damage, the player's limbs, the art fixture).


### Edge pass, grade, rooms, look probe (P1-post)

The look's post pass, the grade and the rooms' shared build. Presentation only: it reads Tuning and draws, and writes no game state.

**`LookPost`** (`game/fx/look_post.gd`, `Node3D`, named "LookPost"; `RoomKit.build_presentation()` adds one to every room that builds a presentation, the training room's too): the edge quad, the grade and the AA mode, live from `Tuning.changed` (`look_*`, or a reset). `edge_quad()`, `edge_material()`, `grade_environment()`, `grade_texture()` for tests; static `aa_settings(mode)`, `depth_only_shader()`, `find_environment(room)`, `node_value(i, n)`, `identity_lut_bytes(n)`, `mix_lut_bytes(identity, graded, strength)`. Build the room's environment first (`RoomKit.build_environment()` names its `WorldEnvironment`): the grade goes on that Environment, found among the room's children.

**The edge quad** (`game/fx/edge_pass.gdshader`): a `QuadMesh` 2x2, `extra_cull_margin` 16384, no shadow, `unshaded, depth_test_disabled, depth_draw_never, cull_disabled, skip_vertex_transform, fog_disabled`, `render_priority` -127 (the contact flash is -128, comic sprites 0 and up). It outputs `ALBEDO = ink`, `ALPHA = line` and never reads the screen texture (a screen copy is taken before the transparent pass and would erase the flash). `look_edges_enabled` off hides the quad, so the renderer makes neither buffer. HUD, speed lines and the impact frame are canvas layers above the 3D picture: untouched (measured: 0.0% of the HP plate's pixels change with the pass).
- *Depth lines* are drawn on the NEARER side of a depth step: the second difference of 1/z along x and y, relative to the pixel's own 1/z, above `look_edge_depth_threshold` (smoothstep from half). A plane has none however steep, so no noise on the floor at a grazing angle, and a silhouette gets one line `look_edge_px` wide.
- *Crease lines* come from the normal-roughness buffer: the forward differences (+x, +y) of the view-space normal, `1 - dot` above `look_edge_normal_threshold`; never to the sky, never across a roughness class, and weighted down where the depth is discontinuous (a silhouette is the depth line's).
- *Width* is whole pixels: `max(1, round(look_edge_px * VIEWPORT_SIZE.y / 1080))`; the fade is `1 - smoothstep(look_edge_fade_start_m, look_edge_fade_end_m, distance)`. The buffers are read with `texelFetch` at integer offsets.
- *The character mask* (A8). A toon material writes `ROUGHNESS` 0 on a character and 1 elsewhere (P1-toon's `edge_tag`) and the buffer carries it (probed: the tag reaches `hint_normal_roughness_texture`, a `StandardMaterial3D` of roughness 0.9 reads 0.896). The sky reads 0 there as well, so the pass finds it from the depth (raw depth 0) and counts it as 1, and so it does a pixel whose normal is empty (the MSAA resolve keeps the clear value on some silhouette pixels). A pixel is masked when it, or any of 8 taps on a ring of `look_edge_mask_px` (1080p pixels, scaled), reads roughness below 0.5: no depth line there, and no crease just outside a character; creases inside a character (collars, brims, soles) draw. Measured on scratch spheres (E2): a hull (an unshaded `cull_front` overlay writes roughness 1; the lit one writes 0) is the same 4.5 px at 1080p with the mask on or off, because a near-side line falls on the hull itself and not beside it (the first probe's symmetric lines did double); what the mask keeps is the line off a tagged character that has no hull (0 px with it, 1.5 px without) and the creases out of the ring round a character. It looks at its ring only for a pixel that has a line to lose. `look_edge_character_mask` (Debug) off is A8's fallback 2.
- *The contract with P1-toon*: a surface that wears a hull (characters, A7's placeholder props) writes `ROUGHNESS` 0 and one that does not writes 1; the hull is an unshaded `cull_front` overlay or a lit one writing `ROUGHNESS 0, ALBEDO 0, EMISSION ink`; `ink` is a `source_color` uniform (a literal `vec3(0.07, 0.06, 0.09)` in a shader is linear and draws grey). Measured in the crowd room (4x MSAA, filmic, the grade on): an edge line draws as (27, 24, 31) of 255, a fogged unshaded hull as about (34, 29, 33), the HUD's ink is (18, 15, 23).
- *Debug* (`look_edge_debug_view`, Debug group): 1 roughness (white world, black character or sky), 2 normals, 3 distance, 4 the character mask, 5 only the depth lines, 6 only the creases. `look_edge_depth_only` (Debug) swaps in the shader with `EDGE_DEPTH_ONLY` defined (`#define` prepended to the source): no normal buffer, so no creases and no mask. These are the A16 fallbacks.

**The grade.** `tools/gen_lut.gd` writes `assets/export/look/grade_lut.png` (1024x32 RGB8, 32 slices of 32x32: slice = blue, x = red, y = green) from `assets/source/look/grade.json` ("street_dusk"): a luma-weighted tint (cool in the shadows, warm in the highlights, multiplicative, black and white unchanged) and a desaturation of low-chroma colors, both by the color's HSV saturation, so a saturated color (the enemies, the blood) takes 35% of the tint and none of the desaturation: the palette stays within dE76 4 (the bible's 6; a test holds every saturated enemy and player paint and the blood colors to the recipe's `keep_delta_e`). The LUT is applied after tonemapping, in sRGB (measured: the picture's colors match the CPU recipe to dE76 under 1), and the renderer samples the 3D texture at the picture's color itself, so node i holds the grade of (i + 0.5) / 32 and the first and last nodes hold black and white (`node_value()`, the same in the tool and in `LookPost`). `LookPost` builds an `ImageTexture3D` and sets `Environment.adjustment_color_correction` and `adjustment_enabled`; `look_grade_strength` mixes it with the identity, so the texture is rebuilt when the strength changes (not for any other tunable); `look_grade_enabled` off sets `adjustment_enabled` false. No glow (SPEC: bloom only on VFX).

**Cost** (noisy, the M1 shared with other builders, six frozen primitive Punks in the crowd room, the edge pass alone: no hulls or toon shader yet; M4-calls/P1-post.md has the table): the edge pass is about 3.1 ms of a 10.8 ms frame at 4x MSAA and about 2.0 ms with FXAA, most of it the depth and normal-roughness buffers; the mask is about 0.3 ms, depth-only about 0.7 ms less, FXAA instead of MSAA 2.8 ms less with the edges on, the grade about 0.25 ms. The lead's quiet-machine run (A16) is the real one.

**AA mode.** `look_aa_mode` 0 = 4x MSAA (the project's), 1 = FXAA (MSAA off), 2 = off, applied to the room's viewport; `project.godot` is untouched, and the viewport gets its own mode back when the room leaves (unless something else set one since). Post lines are not anti-aliased by MSAA; FXAA smooths them and also fades them (measured on the primitive Punk's silhouette at 1 m).

**Both rooms.** `test_room.gd` builds its sky, sun, boxes, materials and presentation through `RoomKit` (`build_environment`, `add_box`, `add_visual_box`, `flat_material`, `grid_material`, `build_presentation`), so one look serves both. It keeps its own actor spawn (its player has no avoidance obstacle, as in M1). `RoomKit.box_mesh/add_box/add_visual_box` take an optional `overlay` for a prop's hull (`material_overlay`); nothing passes one yet. The crowd room's car is a muted blue-grey `Color(0.34, 0.38, 0.45)` (A13: red only for blood, the warning and finisher accents); `tests/test_look_post.gd` walks both rooms' meshes and every enemy, dummy and player paint and asserts none is in the red band (hue 345-15 degrees, saturation above 0.5), and that the car's saturation is under the bible's 0.30.

**Tunables** (Look > Edges, Grade; `tuning_data.gd`): `look_edge_px` 1.25, `look_edge_depth_threshold` 0.08, `look_edge_normal_threshold` 0.35, `look_edge_fade_start_m` 25, `look_edge_fade_end_m` 60, `look_edge_mask_px` 6, `look_aa_mode` 0, `look_grade_strength` 1. Debug (never in presets): `look_edge_debug_view`, `look_edge_character_mask`, `look_edge_depth_only`.

**The probe** (`tests/bot/look_probe.gd`, static functions on `Image`s): `grab(viewport)` (null headless), `dark_runs`, `line_width_px` (the median width at 1080p of the lines the pass added), `luma_clusters` and `shadow_ratio` (L2/L3, for the jacket), `white_share` (L5), `changed_share`, `mean_delta_e`, `record(key, values)` merging into `look.json` beside the recording (or `--probe-out=<dir>`). `tests/bot/acts/look_post_acts.gd`: "Look: edge pass and grade" (e1: on, edges off, grade off, both off, FXAA, AA off, depth-only; metrics in `look.json` and as `LOOK-METRIC` log lines) and "Look: edge mask probe" (e2: scratch spheres, tag 0, with an unshaded hull, a lit hull and none, the mask on and off and the debug views; the width of the dark run at each sphere's body by case). SL-int reruns e2 after P1-toon lands to see the real hulls under the mask. Record one act per process (`tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn captures/e1 "Look: edge pass and grade" 6`).

**Known artefacts, for F-look.** (1) Thin geometry draws dashed creases: the crowd room's windows are boxes 1.5 cm deep, their top and bottom faces are slivers under a pixel tall, and the lines follow whichever pixels the MSAA resolve gives them the sliver's normal. A deeper window or a higher `look_edge_normal_threshold` would calm them. (2) 1-pixel lines step on curves (an MSAA-resolved silhouette): FXAA smooths them and fades them; the hulls are the characters' line. (3) The depth lines have no per-material width: the line is one width everywhere.

### Comic VFX, final pass (P1-fx)

The M2 comic set (SVG drawn by `tools/gen_fx.gd`, rasterized to 512 px PNGs, ten words, SPEC §10) redrawn to the look bible, with two director changes (variants, splats). The calls are in [milestones/M4-calls/P1-fx.md](milestones/M4-calls/P1-fx.md).

**One ink, weighted to the hull.** Every stroke is `HudStyle.INK` (`#120f17`, also the toon hull's and the HUD borders'): the blood's dark-red outlines are ink now. A stroke's weight is set from the effect's size on screen: `INK_TABLE` in the generator holds, per effect, the width in px at 1080p it is drawn at seen from 1.2 m (from the director's sizes and the view-share cap at fov 90) and the width in px of its main ink line there. Main effects (the three bursts, the lettering, the guard break) are inked at 7.5 px, twice the hull's `look_outline_px` (3.75); the rear warning at the HUD's 6 px border; sparks and particles lighter. A stroke is baked into its PNG, so the weight is an art parameter (edit `INK_TABLE`, rerun the generator), not a runtime tunable. The effects hold their size in metres, so on screen the line is 7.5 px from 1.0 to 1.2 m (the view-share cap holds the sprites' size there), about 5.8 px at 1.6 m and 3.7 px at 2.5 m. Each SVG's comment records its main line (`Ink line N units`).

**Ben-Day dots** are explicit `<circle>`s on a 45-degree lattice (`_dots()`): on every burst's outer ring, growing toward the tips, and on the lower half of every lettering face. No random numbers, so a lattice is the same on every run; dot colours are a shade darker than the colour they sit on (red only on the finisher burst's red).

**Lettering** is drawn to one cap height: every word's letters are stretched or squeezed across until the picture (letters, ink and shadow) is exactly four cap heights wide, so every word's PNG has the same cap-height share (0.25 of its width) and the director's width sizing (`vfx_word_*_m`) now sets one letter height for every word of a tier. Each letter has an ink outline grown from its shape (no stroke inside its counters), an inner highlight along its top and left (`HIGHLIGHT_*`), and the first letter keeps its glint. The ten words and 512 px are unchanged.

**Burst variants.** `VfxArt.BURST_VARIANTS` lists two textures per tier (`burst_<tier>` and `burst_<tier>_2`); `VfxDirector.pick_burst_variant(tier)` picks one, never the one that tier showed last (`last_burst_variant`), from the director's seeded generator; `Tuning.vfx_burst_variants` off always shows the first. The sprite's `kind` stays `VfxArt.BURSTS[tier]`, only its texture differs. `VfxArt.burst_texture(tier, variant)` and `burst_variant_count(tier)` are the accessors.

**Splats** (`Motion.LYING`) are unshaded and a little dark (`vfx_splat_shade`, 0.85): a lit sprite grades smoothly where the toon floor steps. They share the thrown droplets' material, so the warm-up now draws two variants (a popping sprite with no depth test, and a plain one) instead of three.

**Reproducibility.** `godot --headless -s tools/gen_fx.gd` redraws and rasterizes every effect; `-- --out=<dir>` writes `<dir>/source` and `<dir>/export` instead of the project's folders (no `.import` patching). Every shape comes from a seeded generator, so a rerun leaves every SVG and PNG unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/test_fx_m4.gd`: ink px at 1.2 m against `look_outline_px` for every burst variant and every word (1.6-2.4 times), the rear warning against the HUD border, one ink in every SVG stroke, red only in blood, the warning and the finisher accents (pixel check), the variants never repeating (and off), one cap height, splats unshaded, the warm-up covering every `[shaded, no_depth_test]` an effect draws, every PNG logged and imported lossless, and the generator run in a second process reproducing every committed SVG and PNG byte for byte. `tests/test_vfx.gd` keeps the M2 behavior; its warm-up assertions count two variants. Tunables in "VFX": `vfx_burst_variants` (Bursts), `vfx_splat_shade` (Blood, spit and teeth).

### The art Punk in the game (SL-int)

Every Street Punk wears the smoke Punk's art, and the crowd room and the training room ink their props. The calls, the numbers for the slice gate and what the first-person sheets show are in [milestones/M4-calls/SL-int.md](milestones/M4-calls/SL-int.md).

**The art.** `game/enemies/punk/street_punk.tscn` sets `art = game/actors/art/punk_art.tres` (`CharacterArt`: `punk.glb`, the shared palette atlas, `triangle_budget` 15000), so `CombatActor._ensure_body()` gives the rig its art through `_configure_art()` and `SkinnedArt.check()` passes (an art that failed would attach nothing and warn once, leaving the primitives). The primitives stay built as the hidden view proxies: every first-person test and the readability probe still measure them (A4). `StreetPunk._configure_art(r)` takes the scene's art, or, for the slice gate's A/B, one of three others picked by the Debug tunable `look_punk_art_variant` (an `@export_range(0, 3, 1)` int, never in a preset, read when a body is built): 0 `punk.glb` (Tripo's weights, the A-pose route, the default), 1 `punk_rest_art.tres` (the arms-down bake), 2 `punk_auto_art.tres` (heat-map weights), 3 `punk_auto_rest_art.tres`; `StreetPunk.ART_VARIANTS` holds the three paths, loaded only when asked for. F-punk replaces `punk.glb` in place; the resources and the switch stay.

**The rooms.**
- `RoomKit.prop_material(color) -> Material` is `ToonMaterials.environment(color, true)`: a prop box (the crowd room's crates, dumpster and car, the training room's three crates) wears the box hull as its material's `next_pass` and is tagged `edge_tag` 0 for the edge pass; walls, windows, paint and the backdrop keep `flat_material()` (tag 1, no hull). `look_hull_on_placeholders` off gives no hull and tag 1 (the A16 fallback). `RoomKit.add_box()`'s `overlay` argument is unused: use one route, not both.
- `RoomKit.warm_up_art(room)` (end of `build_presentation()`, windowed only): a throwaway `HumanoidRig` wearing `WARM_ART` (`punk_art.tres`) is built, `ToonMaterials.warm_up(room, rig.skeleton)` draws a copy of its skeleton, and the rig is freed. `ToonMaterials.warm_up()` has two once-per-run flags now (the boxes under each hull mode; the skinned variant, whichever call brings it, since the first rig of a room is the player's and has no art), and its holder stands `WARM_DISTANCE_M` ahead of and `WARM_DROP_M` below the viewport's camera, read on the room's second frame (after the camera's first update), in the lens for two frames. The look act `W1` ("Look: warm-up in the lens", `tests/bot/acts/look_acts.gd`) checks it windowed: it clears the two once-per-run flags and loads the crowd room and then the training room cold into a `SubViewport` of its own (from a physics frame, so the load is before the frame's process step as the game's start is), and logs `LOOK-WARM <room>: OK ...` when the holder's skinned `ArtBody` AABB and its five hull boxes are in the camera's frustum on both frames the holder is drawn. Headless it logs `LOOK-WARM skipped`.

**The acts** (`tests/bot/acts/look_acts.gd`): every act prints `LOOK-ART variant <n> <glb>` at its start. `Look: Perf, six art Punks` (slug p1, the probe on): six Punks from the six surround points, `keep_alive(6, &"punk")` and the autopilot for 30 s, through `CrowdActs._sample_frames()` and `_report_perf()`; run it with `tools/perf_compare.sh --act "Look: Perf, six art Punks"` (windowed, on a quiet machine; the unvsynced window stalls for a second every few seconds on this M1). `Look: warm-up in the lens` (slug w1, after p1; no marks or rectangles beyond start and end): the windowed warm-up check of `RoomKit.warm_up_art` above. The A/B: `CAPTURE_ARGS="--tune=look_punk_art_variant=2" tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn <dir> "Look: Punk body at 1 m"`. `look_acts.gd` must not name `StreetPunk` (a static reference makes every recording end with leaked-resource `ERROR` lines at exit).

**Tests** (`tests/test_art_punk.gd`, 15): a StreetPunk wears the art with its proxies hidden and built, the A/B switch (off, and each variant's GLB), the tunable's group, every GLB passing `check()` and standing on the floor, A6 (the hurtbox fit by each vertex's dominant bone, and `SkinnedView.hurtbox_fit()`'s axis points), A9 (the 1 m poses: idle at +8 and -6 degrees, jab, cross, shove), A10 (the marks), A11 (the flash anchor), A19 (the strain against the SKINNED REST pose over every `ANIM_IDS` id and Punk move at five phases and the six peak flexes, 13 s for the four GLBs, the worst ten printed), the rooms' prop hulls (crowd and training, and with the placeholder hulls off) and the headless warm-up; the flip count against the skinned rest (a mesh posed at its own rest has no flips, the authored mesh's folded triangles printed as `ART-PUNK A7 ... folded triangles`). Every number is printed as `ART-PUNK ...`; a bar is asserted only where all four GLBs pass it, and the rest are the slice gate's (the Punk's arms are bulkier than the proxies' in a strike: A9's cell share and nearest figure; the flash anchor at the torso and legs: 4 to 5 cm; the strain: no GLB passes A19, the shoulder and the foot seam are the worst).

### Motion (F-motion)

The placeholder clips made to look alive on the same frame data. The calls, the numbers and what the first-person sheets show are in [milestones/M4-calls/F-motion.md](milestones/M4-calls/F-motion.md). **No move clip changes** (M4-plan A20): the polish may touch only `PlaceholderAnims.POLISHED_IDS` (the idle, the four walks, `run_forward`, `taunt`, `brace`, the five flinches, the two get-ups, the two downed loops); every other named clip and every move is built exactly as in Milestone 3.

**`PlaceholderAnims`** (`game/actors/placeholder_anims.gd`). `build_named(id, scale, stance, life = null)` and `build_library(scale, stance, life = null)` take a `PlaceholderAnims.Life`: the thirteen Look > Motion tunables and `move_speed` as one value (`Life.from_tuning()`, the default, reads `Tuning.data`; `Life.off()` is the M3 clips). A library is built once per size and stance (`HumanoidRig._named_library()`, the cache key still `"<stance>@<scale x 10000>"`) with the amounts the tunables have then. `make(keys, length, loop, scale, smooth = false)`: `smooth` gives every track `INTERPOLATION_CUBIC` (the polished clips); a move is never smooth, and its hash is the proof. A clip on the list that is not authored with more life below is eased by `MotionClips.ease()`.

**`MotionClips`** (`game/actors/poses/motion_clips.gd`), the keys of the polished clips:
- `idle(g, stance, life, scale)`: one breath long (`1 / look_motion_breath_hz`, 4 s), every component a whole number of cycles, so the seam is the first key and the first key is the stance exactly. Breathing (the chest rocks, the upper chest and neck give way so the head's and torso's volumes stay within about 1 mm per degree, the shoulders rise), a bounce (the hips dip `look_motion_bounce_m` at `look_motion_bounce_hz`), a weight shift (the pelvis rolls once a breath, the chest rolls against it), the guard's arms swaying after the bounce, a head drift. The legs are solved by `MotionLegs` so each ankle stays where the stance has it: the soles do not move. No hurtbox key point moves 1 cm peak to peak (8.7 mm; the player's, scaled by `look_motion_player_calm`, 3.1 mm), on every body size (the amplitudes are divided by the rig's scale).
- `walk(id, g, scale, life)`: the boxer's walks (the relaxed stance keeps M3's keys, eased). The cycle is `stride / (move_speed x STANCE_SHARE)` (`stride` = `look_motion_stride_m`, or `STRAFE_SWEEP_SHARE` of it for a side step) long: `HumanoidRig.play_locomotion()` plays a walk at `v / move_speed`, so the body covers `move_speed` meters per second of clip time whatever `v` is, and a foot planted for `STANCE_SHARE` of the cycle must sweep exactly that far to keep still in the world. The planted foot rolls about a contact point that does not move (toes up at the strike, flat, heel up from `HEEL_OFF` of the stance, the toes flat on the floor), the swinging foot rises before it travels and settles before it lands; the pelvis turns and tips and the chest turns against it (`look_motion_hip_roll_deg`, `look_motion_counter_swing_deg`), the hips sway toward the planted foot and ride `WALK_DROP_M` below the guard's height so the legs reach (lowered further, per key, where one would not). `walk_back` is the forward cycle run backward. The art's planted soles slide at most 1.5 cm a step at the game's speeds (M3: 0.9 m forward, 2.5 m a side step). The cycle is read from `move_speed` when the library is built.
- `flinch(g, f, length, life)`: a key every frame plus 0.06 s and 0.1 s; per bone an envelope (an ease-out snap to the hit pose at 0.06 s, a hold to 0.1 s, an ease in and out back): the body's bones are M3's at the key times, the shoulders and arms lead by `look_motion_overlap_s`, the neck and head lag by it and the head overshoots by `look_motion_overshoot`. Length and end pose are M3's.
- `ease(keys, length, loop, drive_in)`: the other polished clips keep their authored key poses at their times and are resampled on a 1/30 s grid (at most 24 steps) with a Fritsch-Carlson monotone cubic per bone and axis: no overshoot, a bone that reverses at a key stops there; a clip that plays once starts and ends at rest unless `drive_in` (the get-ups leave their pose at the speed of their first segment).

**`MotionLegs`** (`game/actors/poses/motion_legs.gd`): two-bone leg IK for the placeholder skeleton, pure math at the reference height in rig space like `PlaceholderBody.pose_globals()` (whose FK it inverts, to 0.001 mm): `plant(pose, hips, side, ankle, foot, toes_deg, knee_hint) -> shortfall` writes the side's UpperLeg, LowerLeg, Foot and Toes angles that put the ankle at `ankle` with the foot's world basis `foot` (a pitch and a yaw: `foot_basis()`); `ankle_for_heel()` and `ankle_for_ball()` give the ankle for a heel or ball contact point; `hips_transform()` and `hip_joint()` for the hips' pose. The knee follows `knee_hint` (a pose's own knee) or points forward and a little out.

**Tunables** ("Look" > "Motion", after "FP feel"; every one an `@export_range`): `look_motion_breath_deg` 1.5, `look_motion_breath_hz` 0.25, `look_motion_bounce_m` 0.005, `look_motion_bounce_hz` 1.5, `look_motion_shift_deg` 1.0, `look_motion_guard_sway_deg` 3.0, `look_motion_head_deg` 1.4, `look_motion_player_calm` 0.35, `look_motion_stride_m` 0.62, `look_motion_hip_roll_deg` 4.0, `look_motion_counter_swing_deg` 5.0, `look_motion_overlap_s` 0.025, `look_motion_overshoot` 0.15; and in "Debug" (presets skip it) `look_motion_polish` (true; off builds the M3 clips). Three existing tests (`test_ragdoll`'s launch from behind and the uppercut in a corner, `test_ko_view`'s revive) have no margin on the pose a ragdoll starts from: they pass with these defaults, other values can flip them (M4-calls/F-motion.md C10).

**Acts** (`tests/bot/acts/look_motion_acts.gd`, titles start "Look:"): `Look: extremes` (slug c12: the strain test's flexes, a deep knee bend, the hold's knee and the four lying poses, each held and turned once round at 1 m), and the motion acts at 1 m `Look: motion idle` (m1), `Look: motion walks` (m2), `Look: motion flinches` (m3), `Look: motion get-ups` (m4). The Punk is off the physics tick and its rig is played by hand at the game's speeds; `CAPTURE_ARGS="--tune=look_motion_polish=0"` records the M3 clips of the same act.

**Tests** (`tests/test_motion_m4.gd`, 29): the move clips against the hash table (`tests/fixtures/motion/move_clip_hashes.json`, made by `clip_hash.gd` before the polish: every move file, both stances, two sizes; and every named clip as M3 built it); with the polish off every named clip is its M3 hash and with it on only the list changes; a rig plays every move to the same rig points and hurtboxes at its first, middle and last active frame with the polish on and off; the cache key's shape; the idle (starts in the stance, breathes once in 4 s and bounces inside it, no hurtbox 1 cm on any size, the soles hold, the player's share); the tunables and the Debug switch; the walks (their length, the art's soles at most 2 cm and never under -15 mm, every body size, a slower `move_speed`, and through `play_locomotion()`); no pop at a loop seam; the eased clips have the M3 pose at every authored key and do not overshoot; the flinches' length, end pose, overlap and ease-out; the deformation of every polished clip printed beside M3's (A19) and bounded; the acts' titles and the extremes' poses. `tests/fixtures/motion/motion_probe.gd` (`clip_globals`, the art's sole vertices, `walk_slide`, `primitive_slide`, `hurt_extent`, `strain`) measures a clip on its own, without an AnimationPlayer; `tests/fixtures/motion/gen_clip_hashes.tscn` rewrites the table (`godot --headless res://tests/fixtures/motion/gen_clip_hashes.tscn --quit-after 5`; run it only to add a clip: the table is the proof that a clip did not move, and a rerun on this commit reproduces it byte for byte).

### Showcase, evidence acts and rig upkeep (P3-early)

The part of P3-int that needs neither the concept pick nor the real Punk: it plays whatever Punk the catalog has, so F-punk's replacement in place changes nothing here. The calls, the numbers and what the first-person sheets show are in [milestones/M4-calls/P3-early.md](milestones/M4-calls/P3-early.md).

**The long take** (`game/debug/showcase_script.gd`, `ShowcaseScript`, data only). `STEPS` is 22 steps, 40.00 s: idle, a turntable (a full turn in 10 s), backing off and walking in at `posture_circle_speed_mps`, taunt, `punk_jab` (the AI's wind-up copy) and `punk_cross`, takes a jab (the contact flash on `EyePoint`, bruised), stagger, knockdown, lying (bloodied), get-up, walking in again, a turn away, a face-down fall, lying face down, the face-down get-up, a turn back, KO. A step is a Dictionary: `kind` (`stand` with `turn_deg`, `walk` with `move_m` and `pace`, `clip` with `anim`, `move` with a move id), and optional `slide_m` (a body a hit sends somewhere: metres along its facing, quadratic ease-out; a lying body is a metre long each way of its hips, so the knockdowns carry it until it lies 1 m or more from the lens), `hit_point`, `damage` (a `DamageLook.Level`), `marks`, `view_pitch_deg` (the act's view only). `seconds_of(step, rig)` and `durations(rig)` give what a step lasts on a rig's clips (a clip its own length plus `hold`, a move `total_frames() / 60`, a walk its distance over the Tuning pace); `steps()` hands out a copy.

**`ShowcaseRunner`** (`game/debug/showcase_runner.gd`, a `Node` child of the Punk it plays). `setup(actor, anchor, loop)`, `advance(dt)` (the body is placed first, then the rig advanced, as its owner does each tick; a step that ends hands the rest of the tick to the next), `restart()`, `current_step()`, `take_seconds()`, `seconds_of_step()`, `is_finished()`, `index`, `elapsed`, `takes`; signals `step_started(index, name)`, `step_marked(name, label)`, `take_finished`. `auto_advance` (default on) steps it from its `_physics_process` by the simulation's delta (`TimeController.sim_scale()`: F4's 0.25x slows the take); an act turns it off and steps it itself. Each step is `rig.play()`, `play_locomotion()` or `play_move()` on the Punk's own rig; the contact flash is `rig.flash_at()` with the hit's tunables, shown for exactly `hit_flash_frames` rendered frames; a loop starts again whole (clean, shown, idle, on its spot). It writes the rig and the actor's transform and nothing else. `static unplug(punk)` takes an EnemyActor off its own tick and out of `enemy`, `ai_enemy` and `boss` (not `actor`), off the navigation avoidance, with its hurtboxes off.

**F4 "Showcase Punk at 1 m"** (`CheatMenu.set_showcase(on)`, `is_showcase_on()`, `showcase_point()`, `showcase_distance_m` (an `@export_range` on the menu, 1.0), `showcase_toggle`, `showcase`). On: a Street Punk of the catalog `showcase_distance_m` ahead of the player's feet along his look (the nearest clear spot when something is there), facing him, `unplug()`ged, with a runner looping the take; Freeze AI goes on (the crowd stands). Off, or the menu leaving the tree (F6): the Punk leaves `actor` and is freed, and Freeze AI goes back to what it was unless the player changed it meanwhile. It needs no spawner and no navmesh, so it works in both rooms (tested in each: the enemy, AI enemy, boss and actor counts, the director's token holders and queue, the spawner's alive count, Freeze AI, waves, god mode and the room's child count are as before). Nothing can hit it (hurtboxes off): it is a show.

**The FP feel toggle of feel question 4** (grab-03, the log over the knee). `CombatLogOverlay.TOGGLE_KEY` (L) shows and hides the log (its `visible`, the state `GameHud.set_combat_log_visible()` and the bots use; it keeps recording while hidden), listed in the help overlay's debug keys (`HelpOverlay.KEY_ROWS`). `Tuning.combat_log_hide_while_holding` (Look > FP feel, false = today) holds the log back for as long as the player holds an enemy (`PlayerCharacter.holding_enemy()` and `is_holding()`: a grab, and a knee that has not let go): `set_held_back()`, `is_held_back()`, `is_showing()`; it draws nothing and records on, and `visible` is untouched, so the key and the hold are two things. `fp_knockdown_hide_torso` is wired by F-player-int (see its heading).

**Rig upkeep** (`HumanoidRig`, F-motion's two requests). *Live libraries:* the named libraries are rebuilt when a value they are built from changes: `_on_motion_tuning_changed()` (one static connection to `Tuning.changed`) drops the cache and raises `_library_revision` when the `PlaceholderAnims.Life` values (the Motion subgroup, `move_speed`, Debug's `look_motion_polish`) differ from those the cache was built from (compared by value, so a whole-set change that leaves them as they were rebuilds nothing); a rig whose own count is behind takes the cache's library in `advance()` (`_refresh_library()`), the clip that is playing going on from the same share of its length, faded in from the pose on screen over `locomotion_blend_s`. The cache key keeps its shape. *Idle phase:* a rig whose actor is in the group `ai_enemy` when it is built starts and restarts its looped idle at `fposmod(hash(actor.name) x 0.618..., 1)` of the clip (a Fibonacci hash: `Punk1` to `Punk6` land evenly spread, the same name the same phase in every run, no random numbers); every other rig (the player's, a training dummy's, a scratch rig's) starts at the first key.

**Acts** (`tests/bot/acts/look_acts.gd`): `Look: Punk at 1 m, the long take` (slug `c14`, 40 s first person: the take played by a runner stepped once a tick, a still `TAKE_MARK_TICKS` into each step, in the middle of a long one and (a hit) one tick in for the contact flash, the turntable's quarters, the view down for the lying bodies; prints `LOOK-TAKE`); `Look: F4 Showcase Punk` (slug `c15`: the overlay opened, the toggle on, closed, the player once round the Punk at 1 m, opened, off; `LOOK-SHOWCASE before/on/off` lines with Freeze AI and the enemy counts, two street Punks standing by); `Look: readability with the art drawn` (slug `l5`, windowed work: "Six Punks, fight" then "Mixed six, fight" through `tests/bot/look_l5.gd`, prints `LOOK-L5 <fight> R8 ...` and `R4 ...`). **`L5`** (`tests/bot/look_l5.gd`, one per fight, `tick()` once a physics tick on the probe's cadence): R8 both ways (`FPView.hits()` as the probe measures it; and the same grid with the hidden proxies of art rigs taken out and the nearer of what is left and of `SkinnedView.hits()` taken in each cell, every rig in its drawn pose, a ragdolling one in the ragdoll's) and R4 both ways (another enemy's meshes as `FPView` counts them; and as drawn, a native triangle ray through `SkinnedView._soup()`/`_ray()`); `summary()` and `lines()`. The first figures are in the calls file: R8 p50 within 0.5 points and p95 within 2.8, R4 0 of 14 and 0 of 17 both ways.

**Tests.** `tests/test_rig_upkeep.gd` (16: each Motion tunable, `move_speed` and the Debug switch reach the rigs; a change that leaves every value alone rebuilds nothing; the idle and the walk go on from the same share without a pop; a move that plays is left alone; the crowd's idle phases are apart and the same every run, scratch rigs, dummies and the player keep the first key, a body that leaves the crowd keeps its phase, the idle returns at the rig's phase after a walk), `tests/test_fp_feel_log.gd` (10: the default, the L key, the help row, a real hold in the test room), `tests/test_showcase.gd` (16: the script's beats, the take's length and clips, the runner's order, loop, turn, walk, slides, determinism, flash frames and damage looks, the time scale, and the cheat in both rooms), `tests/test_look_take.gd` (3: the long take and the F4 act headless in the real crowd room), `tests/test_look_l5.gd` (10: the sampler on small scenes with known answers), `tests/test_look_acts.gd` (the new slugs and titles), and in `tests/test_art_punk.gd` the write-back guard of gate gap 10 (a `SkeletonModifier3D` after the Ragdoll on a real StreetPunk through jab, stagger, launch, get-up and KO: the skeleton equals the followers to 0.00000 m on every update, and a probe before the Ragdoll sees the raw simulator 0.01 m or more off).

### Tuning the look (F-look)

The look tuned against the smoke Punk at 1, 3 and 8 m in both rooms, in both lights. Defaults that moved from the numbers in P1-toon's and P1-post's headings above: `look_band_1` 0.25 to 0.22, `look_band_2` 0.62 to 0.52, `look_rim_cut` 0.7 to 0.4, `look_rim_power` 3 to 2 (and, after the final review, `look_fill_strength` 0.8 to 0.4 and the new `look_detail_cut` 0.15); the sun and the fill are new tunables, and the tonemapper is linear (it was filmic). The calls, the numbers (L1-L3) and the sheets are in [milestones/M4-calls/F-look.md](milestones/M4-calls/F-look.md); the targets are [milestones/M4-look-bible.md](milestones/M4-look-bible.md). Presentation only.

**The light.** One sun and a camera-fixed fill. The sun is Tuning (Look > Toon: `look_sun_elevation_deg` 45, `look_sun_yaw_deg` -50, `look_sun_energy` 1.2, `look_sun_shadow_blur` 0.25): `RoomKit.apply_sun(sun)` puts the room's `Sun` there when the environment is built and `LookPost` again on every `look_*` change, in both rooms. The default yaw lights the player's default view (+X) from behind his shoulder, so a Punk facing him is front-lit; turned half a circle (the contre-jour case, about half of all enemies whatever the azimuth) the same Punk has no sun on its front. `look_band_1` 0.22 and `look_band_2` 0.52 sit under cos(elevation) (0.707), so a vertical surface facing the sun can be lit (it could not at 52 degrees with a break at 0.62: the slice gate's gap 2). The fill (`fill_strength`, `fill_dir` in `toon_light.gdshaderinc`; Tuning `look_fill_strength` 0.4 (0.8 until the final review), `look_fill_yaw_deg` -35, `look_fill_pitch_deg` 40; `ToonMaterials.fill_direction(yaw, pitch)` mirrors the vector) makes a character's light the stronger of the sun's `dot(N, L) * ATTENUATION` and `fill_strength * dot(N, fill_dir)`, `fill_dir` a unit vector in view space (a light fixed to the camera, from the upper left). It runs on the directional light's pass only and only on the character and art materials (`ToonMaterials.apply_look()` sets it 0 on the room, the floor and the pipe), so a character that faces away from the sun has a mid area and a violet shadow (and the sun's lit patches where it grazes it) instead of one flat shadow, and the sun still lights a front-lit one and casts the shadows on the floor. **The fill alone never reaches the lit band** (the final review, J5 and L2: at 0.8 the fill lit about 40% of the normals facing the lens by itself, overwrote the sun's mid and violet shadow bands wherever the sun did not reach, and a front-lit Punk's vest was one flat teal; with the fill off the same vest has three bands, so the cause was the fill, not the vest's front panel): `look_fill_strength` stays under `look_band_2` (the lit band is the sun's, so a front-lit character keeps a sun-lit, a fill-mid and a violet shadow band) and above `look_band_1` / 0.63 (a lens-facing front is a mid band, not the flat shadow: gap 2), the useful range 0.35 to 0.45. At 0.4 the cone of normals within 45 degrees of the lens axis is 49% shadow and 51% mid by the fill alone (`test_the_fill_alone_never_reaches_the_lit_band...`); the probe reads 2 jacket bands front-lit and back-lit in both rooms, shadow/lit 0.80 and 0.76. The rim is gated by the combined light (`smoothstep(0, 0.3, lit)`), so it sits on the fill's side of a back-lit character; `look_rim_cut` 0.4 and `look_rim_power` 2 make it 7 to 10 px at 1 m.

**The picture.** `look_tonemap` (Look > Grade; 0 linear, the default; 1 filmic, M3's; 2 Reinhard; 3 ACES; `LookPost.tonemap_mode(value)`) sets the Environment's tonemapper live, and `RoomKit.build_environment()` the first one. Linear shows a flat colour as authored: the skin's mid band is 0 to 1 degree from its swatch, the teal's 0 (filmic moved them 10 to 40), and the ink reads as ink. The hull (`toon_outline.gdshader`) is `fog_disabled`: on screen it is (16-17, 14-15, 22-25) at 1 to 10 m and the edge pass's lines (16, 14, 22), against `HudStyle.INK` (18, 15, 23); a fogged hull was 7 to 13 levels lighter and a grey ring at 10 m. Edge lines are 1.5 px at 1080p.

**The street.** Every street surface is inside the bible's saturation (at most 0.30) and value (0.25 to 0.6): the crates, dumpster, windows, doorway voids and road paint of the crowd room and the training room's crates and paint were retuned (`test_every_street_surface_is_inside_the_bibles_saturation_and_value`). The crowd room's window boxes are `window_depth_m` 0.12 deep and stand `window_proud_m` 0.09 from the wall (new exports; shallow boxes drew dashed crease lines). The grade, the AA mode and every other tunable are unchanged.

**The damage tiles** (`tools/gen_fx.gd`, `_damage_tile()` only): no wash over the skin or the clothes; the skin takes comic bruises with an ink edge, the clothes pale dust smears, grease smears, ink tears and scratches. The four damage PNGs and SVGs are the only effect files that changed.

**Instruments.** `tests/bot/acts/look_tune_acts.gd` has the acts "Look: tune ..." T1-T8 (bands at 1 m front-lit and back-lit, 3 and 8 m, the hull's ring at 1, 2.5, 5 and 10 m, damage, the jab's flash, the lineup and the street in both lights, the player's own hull); the crowd bot loads them by name and `tests/bot/look_tune_bot.tscn` (the combo bot, with `spawn()`, `tune()`, `god()`, `freeze_ai()`, `clear_enemies()` and `log_line()`) runs the same ones in the training room. "Front-lit" and "back-lit" are the sun at its default yaw and turned half a circle. A metric frame is taken with the character on, with it hidden and with the rim off; `look_probe.gd` gained `hue_gap`, `bands` (a swatch family's brightness bands with each band's colour), `line_width_scan`, `ring_width_px` and `outer_ring_px` (the width of the ink ring a frame has over the same frame with `look_outline_px` 0, sub-pixel, blind to the background), `dump`. L2 reads the jacket's three bands from the rim-off frame (the rim is a fourth level in a back-lit shot). A recording's frames are the window's 1280x720 render upscaled to 1600x900; the probe reports at 1080p whatever the frame's size. **Tests:** `tests/test_look_tune.gd`, and the fill, the unfogged hull, the sun, the tonemap, the street's colours and the probe's functions in `test_toon.gd` and `test_look_post.gd`.

### The player wears the art (F-player-int)

The player is a `CombatActor` with `art` like the Punk. His GLB (`assets/export/characters/player/player.glb`, Hero C, F-player-art's) is at that path, so the shipped game wears it with no edit to any file. Without the file the game draws his 45 primitives, and behind a Debug switch he wears the probe body. The calls, the numbers and what the first-person sheets show are in [milestones/M4-calls/F-player-int.md](milestones/M4-calls/F-player-int.md).

**What he wears.** `game/player/player.tscn` carries `game/actors/art/player_art.tres` (`id` `&"player"`, the shared atlas, 30,000 triangles, no `scene`) as `art`. The resource names the hero's files in metadata (`scene_path`, `detail_mask_path`) and asks for sharp ink (`detail_sharp`, see the toon shader), because a resource whose `ext_resource` file is missing fails to load with an error. `PlayerCharacter._configure_art(r)` (called by `CombatActor._ensure_body()` before `build()`) sets `r.art = PlayerCharacter.worn_art(art, Tuning.data.look_player_art_probe)`: the probe's `CharacterArt` (`game/actors/art/player_probe_art.tres`, `tests/fixtures/art/probe_body.glb`) when the switch is on; the scene's art as it is when it carries a scene; a copy of it holding the GLB (and `player_detail.png` when that exists) when `ResourceLoader.exists(scene_path)`, one copy per art so `SkinnedArt`'s cache holds; else **null**, which is the M3 build (the 45 primitives, no proxies, no art meshes, no warning, no error). The switch, `look_player_art_probe` (Debug group, false, presets skip it), is read when the body is built and applies to every `PlayerCharacter`, a `.new()` in a test too. A `PlayerCharacter.new()` has `art == null`, so only the scene's player ever wears the hero.

**The split and the layers.** The art is the Punk's pipeline output with the first-person split: `Body` on the default layer, `Body__fp_hidden` (chest and above, both shoulders, the shoulder half of each upper arm) on `VIS_FP_HIDDEN`, the layer of `PlaceholderBody.FP_HIDDEN_BONES` and `FP_HIDDEN_PARTS` (`HumanoidRig._apply_layers()` does it from the mesh's `fp_hidden` meta, set by `SkinnedArt.attach()`). The first-person lens never draws layer 2 (so never the inside of the head or chest); the KO view lifts it once the lens is clear and shows the whole body; the upper mesh still casts the shadows the player sees; the side view and free fly (default mask) draw all of it. `FirstPersonCamera._head_clear_of()` skips art meshes and reads the hidden primitives, which follow the pose.

**The hull.** The art's meshes wear the first-person hull: `ToonMaterials.outline(true)`, the same shader at `look_outline_fp_scale` (0.8) with the lens fade, tracked, so every `look_*` change reaches it. P3-int moved this out of the player into `ToonMaterials.outline(first_person)` and `RigPaint` (see "Closing the milestone (P3-int)").

**Feel question 3: `fp_knockdown_hide_torso`** (Look > FP feel, false = M3). A player knocked down and not killed lies on the camera and the lens, in his eyes, sees the inside of his own belly as one slab (the Kicker's sweep: 74% of the view on the primitives, over half for 45 of 121 frames; 83% at the get-up in the crowd room; 37% on the probe). With the switch on, `FirstPersonCamera._update_torso()` (each `update_camera()`) moves the torso meshes to `VIS_FP_HIDDEN` while the lens is the current camera, the player is alive, in `LAUNCHED`, `DOWNED` or `GETUP`, and the lens is in his eyes (`_ko_view` 0: the KO view shows the body whole, and a KO's get-up keeps it), and gives them the layers they had when any of that stops. The torso is the primitives on the `Hips` and `Spine` bones (`TORSO_BONES`: Pelvis, Belt, Belly, BellyTop) and, on art, the mesh named `*__fp_torso` (the hero's `Body__fp_torso`: the belly and belt; the whole visible body for art that has none, the probe). The meshes still cast shadows; layers are re-applied each update while it holds. With it on, the primitives' slab is 15% of the view at most (0 frames over a third, was 76) and the probe's 0% (was 37%, 6 frames over a third). Off, no layer is ever touched.

**The lens rules on the skinned art** (slice-gate gap 7), through `SkinnedView` (the drawn pose), in `tests/test_art_player.gd`: in a hold (the room's grab, three knees, a regrab, the throw) the nearest forearm or hand vertex to the lens is 0.320 m on the probe (0.3 m) and the own body at most 25.1% of the view (a third); in the KO view the nearest vertex of the drawn body with the head layer lifted is 0.344 m (`ko_view_clearance_m`, 0.25 m), the own body 17.3% at most and the head layer pops 0.0% on the lift and on the hide; held by an enemy (`test_player_grabbed`'s twin) the own body is 0% and the nearest vertex 0.261 m (0.25 m).

**Acts** (`tests/bot/acts/look_player_acts.gd`, titles start "Look:"): `Look: my arms and legs` (slug `pa1`: idle level, looking 45 and 65 down, at 20 down the chain jab, cross, hook, uppercut, the front kick, the guard, the spin kick, the haymaker's charge and swing, a held Punk and a knee, then his own KO and the lens's return) and `Look: my knockdown` (slug `pa2`: the Kicker's sweep, the fall, three stills lying, the get-up). They print `LOOK-PLAYER art <probe|hero|primitives> <triangles>` (and, for the second, `LOOK-PLAYER fp_knockdown_hide_torso <bool>`) and `look_rect.gd`'s marks and rectangles. The body is built when the room loads, so record with the switches as arguments: `CAPTURE_ARGS="--tune=look_player_art_probe=1" tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn captures/pa "Look: my arms and legs" 6`, and `--tune=fp_knockdown_hide_torso=1`.

**Tests** (`tests/test_art_player.gd`, 15, on the probe body and on the hero's GLB): the missing GLB is a silent no-op; `worn_art()`; the probe switch and its Debug group; the two meshes, their layers, the camera's cull mask; the hull's fp scale, lens fade and live Look values; A9 over 15 first-person poses against FPView's proxies and the proxies' own meshes (the plan's bars on the hero, a ratchet at what the mannequin does on the probe, with the figures printed `GATE`) and a teeth test; the visible mesh watertight and the seam closed; the hold and the KO view on the drawn art; the knockdown slab off and on, on the primitives and on the probe; the switch leaving other cameras, standing and the KO view alone; both acts headless. `tests/test_player_grabbed.gd` gains the held-view twin. With the probe as the default the whole suite passes 1502 of 1503 (the one failure is the assertion that the default is off): the seven fallout files (`test_grab_view`, `test_ko_view`, `test_player_grabbed`, `test_launch_from_hold`, `test_health_bars`, `test_humanoid_rig`, `test_hit_flash`) need no edit.

### The flash in first person and the warm-up (P3-flash)

Feel questions 1 and 2 of M4-plan Part C section 8 (grab-05 and hit-01: a close flash fills a fifth of the view; r3look-05: the haymaker's and the uppercut's barely show), each behind a toggle that defaults to today, and F-motion's warm-up request. The calls, the numbers and what the first-person sheets show are in [milestones/M4-calls/P3-flash.md](milestones/M4-calls/P3-flash.md). Presentation only: `CombatActor._flash_at()` and the contact point are untouched, and the simulation reads no camera.

**The cap** (`Tuning.hit_flash_fp_max_share`, Look > FP feel, 0 = off). `HumanoidRig.flash_at()` hands the overlay the share as the uniform `flash_max_share`; `game/fx/hit_flash.gdshader` does the rest from the camera that draws the pass.
