With the cap on, the shader measures a fragment's distance from the flash's point in view space with the offset across the line of sight to the point stretched by 1 / k and the offset along it as it is (`cap_distance()`), and flashes what is inside the radius by the usual falloff. k (`cap_scale()`) is how much the flash's reach across that line, at the fragment's offset along it (`sqrt(radius^2 - along^2)`), has to shrink on screen to cover at most the share of the view on a surface at the fragment's depth: `limit * depth / (reach * proj11)` with `limit = sqrt(4 * aspect * share / pi)` half-heights of the view, `proj11` the camera's `abs(PROJECTION_MATRIX[1][1])` (negative in a viewport's flipped target) and `aspect` the viewport's. k is 1 when the flash already fits, and then the distance is the plain one: **a flash that fits, and the cap off, are today's flash.** The flash keeps its shape and its place, and a contact hidden behind the surface the lens sees (a knee under a cap's brim) still flashes what is in front of it. The share is the disc's: what a flash changes on a body is a third to a half of it at close range. The disc is exact on the view axis and at most `1 / cos(angle)` wider across the line of sight to a point off it. Mirrors for the tests: `HumanoidRig.flash_cap_scale(reach, depth, proj11, aspect, max_share)`, `flash_cap_distance(fragment, point, radius, proj11, aspect, max_share)` (view space, z negative ahead) and `flash_view_share(radius, depth, proj11, aspect)`.

**The finisher's radius** (`Tuning.hit_flash_finisher_radius_mult`, Look > FP feel, 1 = off). `HumanoidRig` connects once (`_watch_finishers()`, on the first flash) to `CombatEvents.hit_landed`, which follows `receive_hit()` in the same tick; a clean finisher (tier FINISHER, not blocked, so not a guard break either) multiplies the struck rig's aimed overlay radius once (`_widen_flash()`), hidden for the impact frame or drawn, with the impact frame on or off. With the cap on the overlay's share grows by the multiple squared (at most 1), so a finisher's flash is that many times an ordinary one wherever the cap bites. The point, core, strength and duration are as they were.

**The warm-up** (`RoomKit.warm_up_libraries()`, `WARM_LIBRARY_ENEMIES`). `warm_up_art()` (windowed only) builds the boxer named animation library of the Bruiser's and the boss's body sizes (`EnemyCatalog.body_size(id).y / PlaceholderBody.REF_HEIGHT`, `HumanoidRig._named_library()`) before the first of them spawns: that first spawn was 30 ms, now 3. Callable headless (the same builds, no draw); `warm_up_art()` itself still does nothing there.

**Acts** (`tests/bot/acts/flash_fp_acts.gd`, titles "Look: flash share, ..."): the jab and the flurry, the hold's knees, the haymaker and the P P P P chain's finisher, each played once per setting of the two tunables (a pass each, the first always today's) in first person with the art Punk. For each flash they print `FLASH-FP ...` (the depth, the radius, the cap, the disc's share, `white`, `pure` and `any`): the frame it is drawn in against the same frame redrawn with its strength at 0 (`RenderingServer.force_draw()`, no tick between, no extra recorded frame), and `FLASH-FP-SUM` per pass. **Tests:** `tests/test_flash_fp.gd` (23).

### The Punk through the full pipeline (F-punk)

Game-boy's pick, Street Punk A, replaces the smoke Punk in place: the same paths (`assets/export/characters/punk/punk.glb`, `punk_art.tres`, `street_punk.tscn`), the same asset contract, a new source and a longer chain. The calls and numbers are in [milestones/M4-calls/F-punk.md](milestones/M4-calls/F-punk.md).

**Sources** (`assets/source/characters/punk/`, every file in ASSETS.md "› Punk"):
- `views/`: four flat Gemini views of the concept on the mannequin (`views_jobs.json`; front, back, left, right), checked by `tools/pipeline/views_check.py` (Blender headless: same size, subject height within 3% of the mannequin's, feet within 32 px, centred; `views_check.json` and `views_report.json`). The prompts ask for flat albedo with no outline of any colour and keep the garments' fine lines in their own colour, so the lines reach the texture and the detail mask.
- `tripo/`: T4 (multiview from front, left and back: the model; Tripo's `left` is the character's own left, no swap), T8 (rig v3.0 `unity` on T4: the mesh and the rig the chain reads), `bones.json`; the rejected T5 stays in the git-ignored `_cache`.
- `parts/fist/`: two Gemini fist views (the back of the left hand, the thumb side) and T9 (multiview, detailed geometry): a glove fist hanging from the wrist (a RIGHT-hand fist, measured: F-fist-turn, below), cuff and yellow strap above, bare fingers (the chain keeps the knuckle block and throws the generated cuff and strap away: the collar, below).

**What the chain does for it** (`pipeline.json`; every number is config, the scripts hold only algorithms; all opt-in keys leave the probe body and the player's runs as they were: the probe fixtures are byte-identical and `run.py --config tests/fixtures/art/probe_pipeline.json --check` is YES):
- `blender_fit.py`: `fit.snap` (a smooth displacement field, Wendland C2 of radius `radius_m` on the 22 source joints, carries each warped joint onto its target: the landmark residual by the chain's definition is 0, the blend's own 1.76 cm kept as `landmark_residual_blend_m`); `fit.girth_clamp` (the vertices a bone owns pulled radially toward a bone's axis, or a sphere's centre, beyond `limit_m` with a `soft_m` roll-off: thigh 0.078, shin 0.068, head 0.158); `fit.bulge` (a swell, or with `direction: "back"` a push of the rear half, around a joint's height: the knee's rear push keeps the hurtbox axis of a bent knee inside the art).
- `blender_rig.py`: `rig.clamps` (named weight moves above a height: the waist moves the thigh bones' weight above 0.955 m to Hips), `rig.torso_blend` (a height blend of the trunk bones, off: it did not help).
- `blender_parts.py replace` with `parts.fists.source = "generated"` and `pipe_fist.py`: the generated fist is imported (untrusted data), turned to face -Z, welded and triangulated; its block (below the strap, `block_top_frac`) is fitted to the table's fist box, the model is the RIGHT fist as it came (`generated.source_side`) and the LEFT is its mirror; the forearm is pulled toward its axis near the wrist (`wrist_radius_m`, `wrist_taper_m`), with `generated.collar` (F-punk-fix; the cuff and strap of the generation are a torn double shell with overlapping flaps) everything above the block is thrown away and `pipe_fist.build_collar` lofts a clean WRIST CUFF (F-cuff: the concept's dark band, about as wide as the glove's back, not the flange F-punk-fix left) from the 149-vertex loop the cut at the block's top leaves (the glove's neck, 3.6 cm radius): a long gentle funnel (`rings`: a height above the block's top and how far it has blended from the loop's shape to the forearm's outline) that reaches the outline plus `margin_m` (4 mm) 5.7 cm above the block's top, a band that follows the forearm from there to `length_m` (7.1 cm above the block's top, the same on both sides), a slight flare and a rolled rim (`rim`, `lip`: the outer arc to 7 mm from the outline, over the top, an inner arc closing inside the arm), its faces tagged `pipe_collar`. The outline is the forearm's own cross-section (`pipe_fist.arm_section`: the arm's triangles, which `blender_parts.py` hands over, sliced at each ring's height, binned by angle, dilated one bin, smoothed to `outline_harmonics` 4 and lifted so it is nowhere inside the slice; no ring more than `ring_step_m` 4 mm from the next), and the collar's clearance over the skin is reported as `min_clearance_m` (2.4 mm left, 5.1 mm right) and fails the run under `min_clearance_m` 1 mm in `pipeline.json`. The arm's lower end, the Tripo glove's own torn, flared cuff remains (radius 7-8.8 cm against a forearm of 4.8-5.4), is trimmed away above `arm_trim_above_block_m` (6.15 cm, 5.55 cm above the hand joint; `blender_parts.py`, which refuses a plane below the level where the funnel reaches the outline) so the cuff hugs the forearm proper. Weights are 100% Hand in the block, at most 0.85 above it, ramping to LowerArm at the rim. Without `collar` the older path runs (the cuff stretched over the cut, flared to the wrist's radius plus `cuff_margin_m`, rings clamped by `ring_clamp_m`, the strap's two planes kept as `pipe_fist_planes`). The fist's texture is packed with the body's into one 2048x4096 atlas (v remapped), so the next step labels both and the mask is baked from both.
- `blender_palette.py`: `palette.regions` (faces of one slot inside a box take another: the lens, slot 19, the cap's yellow with marks off (F-cuff's A/B at 1, 2.5, 5 and 10 m; it was the shared cream from F-punk-fix); the shades' dark frame, `frame` and `frame_low`, the faces of the teal and the jeans, or the skin, around and under the lens become the dark grey; `boots`, the skin cells below 0.2 m become cream; `bisect_z` cuts the mesh at the box's planes first so its edges are straight); the fists (face attribute `pipe_fist`) are decimated apart from the body (`fist_budget_tris` 1,350 each, borders thinned to one protected vertex per `fist_border_cell_m`, `reserve_tris` kept for the cuts); the collar faces (`pipe_collar`) are labelled the glove's slot (`glove_slot`) and a strap band, when the config still makes one, is painted `strap_paint_slot` (2, the glove's dark grey: no yellow on the gloves); with `bisect_strap` the fists are cut along the strap's planes again on the decimated mesh (the older clean ring); `palette.unfold` relaxes the folded triangles (`pipe_cleanup.py`: 159 in the smoke, 17 now); `palette.detail_bake` (`pipe_detail.py`), whose `zones` give the head's skin its own ink thresholds and whose bake leaves the collar faces out of the UV2 unwrap unless `collar_in_unwrap` (the Punk's config sets it since rs-02: the wrist cuff takes no texture ink but the glove's authored lines need texels there), and whose `glint` writes the lens's streaks into G, `clean` cleans the strokes and `glove_lines` adds the glove's lines (see "The detail mask").
- `blender_export.py` and `pipe_normals.py` (F-punk-fix): `export.smooth_normals` (`iterations` 12 since re-score rs-04, 6 before: the face's bands were polygon-edged patches, and the reason for stopping at 6, a cap highlight, went with the per-swatch gloss; `crease_deg` 50, `falloff` 2) gives each variant, on its final positions, shading normals blended with the neighbours' by agreement, so the toon bands (cut on the normal alone) lose the lumps of the generated mesh; only `NORMAL` changes, never a position (the hull's `COLOR` normal, A6 and the skinning numbers are untouched). `gen_palette.py` and `run.py` are unchanged except that `run.py` gives a `*_detail.png` mipmaps in its `.import`. The heat-map GLBs are rebuilt with `--set 'rig.weights="auto"' --set 'palette.detail_bake=null'` plus the `paths.work`, `paths.blend_dir`, `paths.report` and `paths.export_name` overrides (`--check` is YES for both builds).
- Review tools: `blender_closeup.py` (a Workbench still of a work file in palette colours: the head, a hand end-on) and a zoom in `blender_views.py`.

**The palette** (`palette/punk.json`): punk_teal (16, the vest), punk_yellow (17, the cap and the laces), punk_jeans (18, #8aa4a8) and punk_lens (19, #e8bd2e, the cap's yellow on its own slot, marks off, so it takes no damage marks: the concept's gold shades; it was the cap's yellow, then the shared cream from F-punk-fix to F-cuff, whose A/B kept the yellow because the dark frame above and below separates the lens from the cap at 1 and 2.5 m), with the shared dark grey (the tank top, the gloves and their collars, the shades' frame; value 0.30), skin and cream. Seven slots, six colours: `test_art_contract` counts the colours (4-6) and the slots (at most 7) and asserts the separation through the frame (the first face above and below the lens in each column of its middle is the dark grey).

**The detail mask** (A18, a departure from SPEC section 10 for enemies, to be logged in README's M4 section): `assets/export/characters/punk/punk_detail.png`, 1024x1024, R = ink lines only (plus the glove's authored lines, below), G = the lens's glint gate (below), B = 0, baked on the final mesh's UV2 (smart-projected, packed) from the dense Tripo texture's black top-hat (what is darker than both its sides over a few pixels: no shading, no colour edges, nothing within 6 px of an island border, nothing on the lens or on the glove collars; the head's skin has its own higher thresholds, `detail_bake.zones` `face_skin` 0.04 / 0.10 against 0.02 / 0.07 and a lower pair for the nose box, so only the mouth mark and the nose line stay: 1.62% of its surface has ink over one half as `test_art_contract` samples it (1.56% at F-punk-fix, 1.98% after F-cuff's re-bake, 2.62% before the zone; the pipeline's own texel share is 1.34%); a zone with `no_ink` takes none: the cap and its strap slot, the shades' frame and the laces and the boots' ankles, whose texture is noise or a colour border that is the shape already; the soles keep their lines at 0.06 / 0.14; final-v1-03, -05, -06 in `M4-calls/F-punk.md`'s "Final review fixes (wave 2)"). The cap's strap slot is authored like the lens: `palette.regions` `strap_base` and `strap_top` (a dark-grey arch of two boxes with `bisect_z` and `bisect_x`, so its borders are edge loops). `game/actors/art/punk_art.tres` carries the mask as `detail_mask` and asks for the sharp cut with `metadata/detail_sharp` (like the hero's: the shader cuts the mask at `look_detail_cut` with a pixel-wide edge, so a one-texel line stays a crisp line at 1 m instead of a grey smear); the heat-map and arms-down variants' `.tres` carry no mask.

*Re-score additions (rs-01 to rs-03, `M4-calls/fix-rescore-punk-art-chain.md`).* **G, the glint gate** (`detail_bake.glint`, `pipe_detail.glint_field`): concept A's two diagonal white streaks per eye (a wide band and a thin one, leaning to the viewer's right going up, `x_m`/`width_m` read at the lens's mid height) written as a signed-distance ramp (0.5 on a band's edge, `ramp_m` 4 mm, so the shader's cut at 0.5 is sharp from a 2.6 mm texel) on the lens's texels only (the faces of `glint.slots`, 33% of them), with a 2-texel fill outside the island for the bilinear sample; every other texel's G is 0; `tests/test_toon_glint.gd` reads the shader's text, where G is non-zero, and the cap evaluated over the lens's own texels at head pitches. **The stroke cleanup** (`detail_bake.clean`, `pipe_detail.clean_strokes`): at 1k (SPEC section 10, A18: no 2k) the strokes, the texels over the shader's cut, have the gaps along a stroke bridged (`bridge_gaps`: up to `bridge_px` texels, only where both ends run along the gap's direction, so two parallel strands are not joined, which a closing does and made blots), every piece shorter than `min_extent_texels` or smaller than `min_texels` dropped, and the cleaned mask blurred a little (`smooth_sigma` 0.5, `smooth_gain` 1.05) so the cut draws a smooth contour, not the texel staircase; the pieces over the cut went from 1,137 to about 430, those of 6 texels or fewer from 25% of the ink to 7%, the median from 2 to 10 texels (`test_art_contract`). **The glove's lines** (`detail_bake.glove_lines`, `pipe_detail.glove_lines_field`): the generated glove has no line in its texture to extract, so its lines are authored in the hand's frame (the hand bone's axis, the outward side the back of the hand faces) as a distance ramp whose 0.15 contour lies `width_m` / 2 from the line: a seam ring where the knuckles begin, the outline of the dorsal plate with three knuckle ticks, and three rings round the wrist cuff (which the unwrap now gives texels, `collar_in_unwrap`, the cuff being what a strike's contact shows); they are merged over the cleaned strokes.

**Numbers** (the smoke in brackets): 14,300 triangles (14,999; body 11,600, fists 2 x 1,350, the collars' cuts none; 14,984 before the fix round), 66 folded triangles in the A-pose GLB and 107 in the arms-down one (159; 17 and 40 before the fix round: the count is against the shipped NORMAL, so the smoothing raises it with no geometry change), joints 0.0007 mm off the chained table, A6 torso 100% / legs 95.3% / head 99.9% (99.4 / 89.1 / 95.0) with no axis point outside the art, A9 lens share at most 2.5 pp in every pose but two, and 4.71-4.78 (jab) and 4.94-5.09 pp (cross) at +8 degrees on the four GLBs (the bar is 4: the lead's call is that the cuff follows the concept, not the proxies' fist box, so those two poses are ratcheted at 5.1 and 5.4 and the bar is printed as a GATE; the flange held all poses at 3.3-3.8), a held victim 0.254 m from the lens (the smoke's cap came to 0.20), the strike at 0.95 m 0.230 m, the fist extents 0.099 x 0.115 x 0.118 m against the box's 0.100 x 0.116 x 0.118, the cuff 5.8 cm at its widest from the arm's axis (8.6 before), needle 22.6. Not met: A9's 3% cell bar on the strikes (6.4%, reported), A11's 2 cm flash anchor at the torso (6.0 cm, as before) and the 2 cm visible-stretch bar (8.4 cm: the vest's waist flank in the cross's torso twist; ratchet at 10 cm).

**Which way the fists face (F-fist-turn, `M4-calls/F-fist-turn.md`).** M4-plan A5 says each fist hangs knuckles along the hand bone, palm to the midline, thumb forward (-Z). M3's box fists were symmetric and A5's box tests (centroid, extents) cannot see a hand's handedness, so the first F-punk build shipped both generated fists with the palm OUTWARD: the generated model is a right-hand fist and was taken for a left one (the header of `pipe_fist.py` said so, on the Gemini view's word). Raised in the boxer's guard (forearms nearly upright, the hand bone not rolled) the curled fingers then faced out and toward the lens and the thumbs sat on the outside: Game-boy's playtest note. The fix is in the piece, not the pose: `parts.fists.generated.source_side` is `"Right"` (the model as it came is the right fist, the left its mirror in x, winding and UVs reversed), the four Punk GLBs are rebuilt, and no clip changed (A20's hash table and every FistPoint are untouched). The glove's authored lines (`detail_bake.glove_lines`: the dorsal plate's outline, the knuckle ticks) were already defined on the outward side of each hand, the real back of the hand now, so the bake lands them on the plate. What pins it: `test_art_contract.test_the_punks_fists_hang_palm_to_the_midline_and_thumb_forward` (the bare-skin faces of each fist sit toward the midline by 2.0 cm and toward the front by 1.5 cm in the hand's table frame, all four GLBs; red on the old GLBs at -2.0 cm), `test_motion_m4`'s guard test (the boxer's idle, walks, brace and head flinch: the A5 frame through the hand bone gives palm in 0.92 to 0.98, away from the viewer 0.10 to 0.31, thumb back 0.94 to 0.99, knuckles up 0.92 to 0.99) and the jab and cross test (knuckles forward 0.79 and 0.81, palm in 0.98), and `test_art_punk`'s drawn-mesh twin (skin the art in the guard: the curled fingers 2.8 cm toward the face and 1.8 cm away from the viewer, the thumb pad 3.0 cm toward the face; the old GLBs read -0.3 cm). The hero's fists meet A5 as they are (`player_parts.py` takes its piece for a left fist and it is one: the bare skin 2.3 cm toward the midline of the fist's centre, the thumb across the front of the fingers as on the corrected Punk's) and were not touched.

**Tests** (`tests/test_art_punk.gd`, 21): A6 asserted at 95% for torso, legs and head; the visible-stretch test with a ray-tested view; the hold (grab, three knees, throw) and strike lens rules. `tests/test_art_contract.gd`: the colour count and, from the fix round, four tests on the shipped GLBs: no yellow or lens face on the hands and a cuff with no needle, long edge or spike; the lens a marks-off slot in a dark frame above and below it, apart from the cap through the frame (F-cuff; a colour distance before), and the cuff a band no wider than the forearm above it by a centimetre and within 7 cm of the arm's axis (F-cuff); neighbouring shading normals that agree (11.3% of edges over 25 degrees, mean 15.1); the head's skin with its lines and without the speckle. Each is red on the pre-fix GLB. Not met, as before: A9's 3% cell bar on the strikes (6.4%, reported), A11's torso anchor and the visible-stretch bar (ratchets).

### The hero's art (F-player-art, fixed by F-player-fix)

The player's own GLB, `assets/export/characters/player/player.glb`, and its mask `player_detail.png`: concept C (Game-boy's pick) as a full generated body with the first-person split, generated wrapped fists and a 1k ink mask. `game/actors/art/player_art.tres` names both in metadata, so the player wears them with no code edit (F-player-int's heading). The calls, the numbers and what the first-person sheets show are in [milestones/M4-calls/F-player-art.md](milestones/M4-calls/F-player-art.md) (its "Fix round" is F-player-fix's: the legs turned right way out, the arm's dome, the fists, the torso mesh, the hero's A9 ratchet). Tripo credits: T11 30, T12 25, T13 50 (105; the fix round spent none); Gemini images: 9.

**Sources** (`assets/source/characters/player/`): `views/` (three Gemini views of the hero on the mannequin, the right one the left mirrored, `views_jobs.json`, `views_report.json` from `player_views_check.py`, `refs/` crops of the concept sheet), `tripo/` (T11's generation, T12's rig, the dumps), `fist/` (four Gemini views of one clenched, wrapped, gloved fist with the wrist, `fist_jobs.json`, and T13's generation), `pipeline.json`, `report/`, `work/player.blend`. The palette is `assets/source/palette/player.json` (slots 32-35: navy, slate, hair, charcoal; skin tan and cream are shared): six swatches.

**The chain** is the shared one with three player steps: `python3 -I tools/pipeline/player_run.py --asset player` (options as `run.py`'s; `--check` reproduces the report hash from the sources). `player_run.py` imports `run.py`, replaces `blender_parts.py` by `player_parts.py` and adds the steps `detail` and `normals` after `parts_split`; every other step, the report and the Godot import are the shared ones, and the player's `.import` for the mask (lossless, mipmaps) is patched after it. The shared palette step runs with `palette.unfold` (the folded triangles of the generated mesh, as the Punk's) and four majority passes.
- `player_parts.py replace`: the hands are cut off at a plane across each forearm (`parts.fists.cut_a_m`, stray fragments of the open hand removed), the limbs' girth is clamped toward the proxies' radii (`parts.fists.girth`), and T13's piece replaces the hands: turned into the table's fist frame (the drawn piece is a left fist: a 180 degree turn about X; mirrored for the right), mapped onto the arm axis by `parts.fists.map` (the strap, the wrist plane, the knuckles), scaled across by `xz_scale_m` so the fist block fills the table's box, the strap and wrist column flared so the forearm's ring ends inside the strap, weighted LowerArm to Hand over `ramp_a_m`. The piece is centred on the arm by the slice of its height from `centre_band_height_g` (0.15) under the wrist plane (`map.y_g[1]`) up to it, its strap's radius is measured under the strap's bottom plane plus `strap_margin_g` (0.05), and the ring the cut leaves and the body's ledge are found within `ring_window_m` (0.02) and `ledge_window_m` (0.012) of their planes (final review: these four were literals in the script; the defaults in the script and the values in `pipeline.json` are today's, so `player.glb` is byte-identical). **The labels and the decimation** (all `parts.fists.*`): each dense face (19,273 triangles) is labelled Lab-nearest among charcoal, skin and cream from the piece's texture, two majority passes, then `label_smooth` (80 passes of blending each label's 0/1 field with its neighbours' mean, argmax: a frayed border becomes a curve); the piece is decimated to `target_tris` 4,000 with the colour borders pinned one vertex per `border_cell_m` (4 mm: the decimator joins them with straight edges instead of a fan of slivers) and the bare fingers (`fine_slots`, `fine_weight`) and the vertices near a border (`border_zone_m`) held back in proportion, so the triangles the lens looks at stay small; each decimated face then takes the commonest label of the dense faces at its centre and `relabel_reach` of the way to its corners, smoothed twice (`label_smooth_decimated`). The dense, textured pieces go to `work/04a_fists_dense.blend` for the mask.
- `player_parts.py split`: three meshes. **`Body__fp_hidden`** = Chest and above, both shoulders, the shoulder half of each upper arm, cut by the Chest plane through the shoulder zone and the arms too (no hidden vertex below the Chest joint). **`Body__fp_torso`** (`parts.split_fp.torso`) = the faces between a plane at the hip joint (`low_bone`'s joint, y 0.925, cut through every face that is not an arm's) and the Chest plane whose dominant bone is not an arm chain's: the belly, the belt and the pelvis' top, the mesh `fp_knockdown_hide_torso` hides. **`Body`** keeps the legs, shoes, forearms, fists and the upper arms' visible half. Hairline cracks are welded, slits collapsed, defects cut out and patched in every part, **and no step recalculates normals over the body: `mark_trusted()` marks every face of the generated mesh and `orient_untrusted()` gives the faces the split makes (hole patches, caps) the winding of the trusted faces they grew from** (the first version's global `recalc_face_normals` turned the hero's legs, feet and part of the hips inside out). Every open loop of `Body` and of the torso is capped by `add_caps()` from `parts.split_fp.caps[<mesh>][<bone>]` over `caps[<bone>]` over `caps.default` (`dir` in/out, `depth_m`, `rings`, `ring_verts`, `wall_normals`): the upper arms end in a **convex 4 cm dome** (8 rings, a ring of 32 over the cut's 75 joined by a strip: M3's Elbow capsule's end, tangent to the arm, so the lens sees a rounded form with smooth shading instead of a bowl with a fan of hull lines), the belly's top in a 5 cm dome (M3's `BellyTop`), the legs' top in a 4 cm dome and the torso's bottom in a flat fan (inside the pelvis); each cap wears the loop's commonest swatch, its UV2 is the empty texel, its faces carry `pipe_cap` (the hull's smooth normal ignores them). Triangulated.
- `player_detail.py`: re-unwraps UV2 of all three meshes together with islands scaled by bone (hidden x0.3, forearms and hands x1.8, feet and shins x1.5, thighs x1.2), then bakes `player_detail.png` (1024, R = ink, G and B repeat it): a texel is ink on a swatch border (analytic distance to a triangle edge whose neighbour wears another swatch), in a groove of the dense geometry (the vertex cavity from 0.12 to 0.28: the wraps' strands, the glove's stitching, the cloth's folds) or on a thin dark ridge of the dense texture (neither on the body's bare skin: `crease.skip_slots` and `stroke.skip_slots`; the fist piece's fingers keep theirs). Texel size: 1.7 mm at the 10th percentile, 2.3 median, 10.5 on the hidden part. Cap faces sit at UV2 (0, 0), which is empty. **The mask is cut, not mixed** (the final review's J14 fix: the toon shader thresholds the hero's mask at `look_detail_cut`, so every partial value and blot of the mask would show as hard ink): the fist piece's glove (`detail.fist_skip_slots`, the charcoal swatch) takes no stroke or crease ink, since the generated texture's mottling on a dark glove was a black patch the size of the fingers once cut; the stroke and crease terms are hardened (`detail.harden`, a smoothstep from 0.05 to 0.2, so a faint ridge is a line and no value is a smear) and cleaned (`detail.clean`: a morphological opening of 2 texels takes the blots, 1 texel grown round them, and any piece under 6 texels is dirt, not a line; the analytic borders are never cleaned); the step's report holds `ink_mean`, `ink_texels` and what was cleaned (about 5,400 stroke texels, 659 blot texels and 905 specks removed; mean ink 0.039).
- `player_normals.py` (`normals.smooth`): custom shading normals on every mesh: Blender's vertex normals, except that a ring a cap marked `wall_normals` touches takes the wall's, and the upper arms' (`bones`) are relaxed over the mesh edges (`passes`, `lam`, weighted by the bone's weight, no normal turned more than `max_turn_deg`): the generated surface's muscle bumps no longer break the toon bands.
- Tools: `player_views_check.py` (size, subject height, feet row, red of the generated views), `player_clay.py` (studio-lit clay sheets of any GLB: a model's shape without its colours).

**The GLB**: three meshes `Body`, `Body__fp_hidden`, `Body__fp_torso` (`pipe_blender.body_objects()` returns them in that order and the contract asserts exactly those), 23 named binds on the table chained through the measured arm swing (41.9 and 42.7 degrees; joints within 0.001 mm), faces -Z, soles at y 0.00015, 29,320 triangles (19,868, 6,172 and 3,280; the budget is 30,000), 92 folded triangles, UV1 palette, UV2 detail, COLOR hull normal in glTF axes (0.95-0.98 agreement per mesh), six swatches, the fists' Hand-weighted vertices within 1.02 x 1.01 x 1.09 of the table's fist box (centroid 13.8 mm of the 15). Only the A-pose route is shipped (no `_rest` variant). Strain against the skinned rest pose is the F-player-art round's (guard p99.9 2.2, elbow 1.7, arm raise 4.5, knee 2.1, spine twist 3.0: A19's bar 1.6 is met by none, as for the Punk; not re-run on the three-mesh GLB).

**First person** (F-player-int's tests on it, `tests/test_art_player.gd`, 17): the hold keeps the nearest forearm or hand 0.321 m from the lens and the own body at most 25.5% of the view; the KO view's nearest vertex is 0.331 m and the own body 18.1%; the knockdown slab with `fp_knockdown_hide_torso` off is 43% of the view at most (73 frames over a third), and on 19% (0 frames over a third): only the belly and belt go, `FirstPersonCamera._find_torso_meshes()` taking the art mesh named `*__fp_torso` (the whole visible body when there is none). Row A9 at the plan's bars against FPView's proxies is NOT met (the plan's bars are printed `GATE`; strikes 1.8-2.6 cm from the proxies' own meshes, the block covering 8.4 points less, the haymaker and the front kick 7.5 and 4.4 cm nearer than the proxies' own meshes): the hero is held to a ratchet at those figures (`HERO_*`: cells 9%, share 9 points, 9 cm and 8 cm nearer, coverage 40%), as the probe is at its own. The near upper arm in the straight punches fills no more of the view than M3's Elbow capsule (asserted: at most 3 points over it; a quarter of the view in a jab at 20 down, as in M3). `test_art_contract` asserts the split (the hidden part starts at the Chest joint, the torso lies between the hip and the Chest joint plus its dome, both closed), the dome at the arm's cut and that no leg, foot or thigh triangle faces its bone (it fails the F-player-art GLB with 49-96%). The hull on his guard (T8, 1080p; the target is 3.0 px +-1, x0.8 of 3.75) reads 4.15 / 3.87 / 4.35 px (left, right, top) on this GLB with the soft detail-mask mix and 3.85 / 3.74 / 4.05 with the sharp ink of the final review (the soft mask's smear beside the silhouette counted as ring; top is 0.05 px over the bar); the '3.6 px' this paragraph first gave was an ad hoc measure, not the act's.

**A second hero** is another folder of this shape, `pipeline.json`, palette JSON, `<id>.glb` and `<id>_detail.png`, and its `CharacterArt` resource; `PlayerCharacter.worn_art()` still names one hero (calls file, the last paragraph of F-player-art's section 5).

### The player's straight punches turn the fist (F-punch-turn)

Game-boy's own request after playing M4 (2026-11-02; M4 call 28): "When the player throws a straight punch, the fist should turn 90 degrees so that the curled fingers angle down toward the ground (rather than inward toward each other)." A departure from A20's frozen active-frame poses, not from the SPEC. The calls, the numbers and the first-person stills are in [milestones/M4-calls/F-punch-turn.md](milestones/M4-calls/F-punch-turn.md).
- **The poses.** `PlaceholderPoses._jab_1(g, turn_fist)` (the left jab) and `_jab_2(g, turn_fist)` (the right cross) roll the striking fist about the forearm's own long axis (`_turn_fist()`, `_rolled()`): the wind-up key is turned 22.5 degrees (`PUNCH_TURN_WINDUP_DEG`), the strike key 90 (`PUNCH_TURN_DEG`), the left hand one way and the right the other. The roll is split between the lower arm (15 degrees at the wind-up, 27 at the strike: `PUNCH_TURN_WINDUP_LOWER_DEG`, `PUNCH_TURN_LOWER_DEG`) and the hand (the rest), so the hero's skinned wrist keeps 0.885 of its width (the whole roll in the hand: 0.78) and the elbow bends about one axis from wind-up to contact. The fist point moves 7.1 mm at most at the keys (the 5 mm it sits off the axis) and 17 mm at the frames between them. The clip keeps the keys M3's had (the Euler triple of a roll that follows a bend past 90 degrees would make `PlaceholderAnims._subdivide()` lerp it).
- **Whose.** `_recipe()` passes `turn_fist = true` for `jab_1` and `jab_2`, the player's moves; `EnemyPosesPunk._cross`, `EnemyPosesPunk._jab` and `_dummy_jab` call the same functions with the default `false`, so `punk_cross`, `punk_jab` and `dummy_jab` are the M3 clips (A20), and the hash table's eight `jab_1` and `jab_2` entries are the only ones that moved (reason in its `notes`).
- **How far down.** At contact the palm, and with it the curled fingers (the hero's bare fingers sit on the palm side, `tests/test_punch_turn.gd` measures it from the mesh), is 33.7 degrees from straight down in the jab and 36.0 in the cross, 2-3 degrees from the lowest the forearm allows: the forearm rises 34 and 36 degrees at contact, and a straight wrist cannot be nearer. Bending the wrist to level the fist would move the fist point more than the centimetre the hits allow.
- **Tests.** `tests/test_punch_turn.gd` (11): the M3 poses as the twin that fails the rules, the palm at the strike, the wind-up and the turn over the punch, the fist point at the keys and at every frame, no extra keys, the clip's end, the Punk's and the dummy's strikes unturned, the hero's forearm and wrist width, the whole-mesh strain no worse than M3's.

### Closing the milestone (P3-int)

What the last package of the fix round landed: the player's first-person hull through `ToonMaterials`, the strain of the shipped GLBs, the done-when file that shows every measured row's state, and the final look probe. The calls, the numbers and what the stills show are in [milestones/M4-calls/P3-int.md](milestones/M4-calls/P3-int.md). With the part of F-player-int's "The hull" paragraph that this replaces:

**The first-person hull is `ToonMaterials.outline(first_person := false)`.** `RigPaint.outline_material()` returns `ToonMaterials.outline(_rig.first_person_body)`, which `SkinnedArt.attach()` puts on each art mesh's `material_overlay` when the art is attached (`first_person_body` is set in `_configure_rig()` before `build()`), exactly as `outline_for_part(shape, first_person)` does for the primitives: one shared material per flag, `fp_scale` = `look_outline_fp_scale` and the lens fade on the player's, full width on an enemy's. `PlayerCharacter._wear_first_person_hull()` is gone, and nothing outside `ToonMaterials` calls `_hull()`. The F-player-int heading's paragraph "The hull" describes the method this deleted. No look change: T8's widths at the guard (3.82, 3.46, 3.85, 3.94 px at 1080p, on F-player-art's GLB; F-player-fix's GLB reads 4.15, 3.87, 4.35, 4.5) and its stills are identical before and after.

**`tests/art_strain.gd`** (a helper, not a test) is A19 against the SKINNED REST for any rig wearing art: every `PlaceholderAnims.ANIM_IDS` id and the moves given at five phases, then the six peak flexes, the worst ten, the problems by kind, the flexes over the triangle bar; a two-mesh art reports the worst of its meshes. `ArtStrain.SHIPPED` names the Punk's and the hero's GLB with the ratchets they are held to (`problems_max`, `high_max`, `FLEX_RATCHET_SHARE`), and `cached()`/`keep()` keep a run per art so `test_skinned_art` (`test_the_strain_of_the_shipped_glbs_is_reported_for_the_gate`) and `test_m4_done_when` (row A7) make it once. Neither shipped GLB meets p99.9 in [0.6, 1.6]; both are printed with `GATE` lines.

**`tests/test_m4_done_when.gd`** holds Part B section B1's rows A1-A14, one test each, on the art the game wears (`punk_art.tres`; the hero through `PlayerCharacter.worn_art(player_art.tres)`), each naming the test that holds its row elsewhere and mirroring it. A row the art meets is asserted at the plan's bar; a row it misses is asserted at a ratchet (a constant at the file's head with the figure it stands over) and printed as `DONE-WHEN <row> GATE ...`; `after_all()` prints the table of every row's state, then lists the [W] rows as printed by the look probe. Currently met: A1, A2, A3, A5, A8, A10, A12, A13, A14. Held at a ratchet: A4 (the hero's walks), A6 (the hero's legs), A7 (strain and the Punk's visible stretch), A9 (framing), A11 (the flash anchor). Three more tests: the player's hull from `outline(true)`, the final probe's source acts by title, and the windowed rows' arithmetic.

**The final look probe** (`tests/bot/acts/look_final_acts.gd`, `Look: final probe`, slug `fp`) plays, in one window, F-look's `Look: tune hull widths`, `Look: tune own hull`, `Look: tune bands at 1 m` and `Look: tune jab flash` and P3-early's `Look: readability with the art drawn`, then `LookProbe.final_rows()` sums Part B's L1, L2, L3 and L5 from look.json and the `LOOK-L5` lines and records them under look.json's `final` key (and as `LOOK-FINAL <row> <state>` log lines): each row a state (`met`, `missed` or `not measured`; headless it measures nothing), a text line and the figures. The bars are in `look_probe.gd`'s constants: L1 within 1 px of `look_outline_px` (the own body of x`look_outline_fp_scale`) and at most 4.5; L2 on the front-lit view 2-3 bands, shadow/lit at least 0.4, the shadow's hue nearer the tint (the back-lit view is reported beside it); L3 at most M2's 4.3% plus 2 points; L5 R8 within 3 points (median) and 5 (95th) of the proxies and no wind-up hidden that the proxies did not see hidden. Record it with `tools/capture_bot.sh res://tests/bot/crowd_bot.tscn captures/final "Look: final probe" 6`. On the shipped art after the final review's fixes it reads L1 missed (the sides at 10 m, 2.59 and 2.04 px; the own body's top, 4.05 px against its 4.0 bar), L2 met (front-lit 2 bands, shadow/lit 0.80; it was one band while the characters' fill was 0.8), L3 and L5 met. L4 (perf) is `tools/perf_compare.sh`.

## Level blockout (Milestone 5)

### Attack arcs (ARC)

Game-boy's attack arcs replace SPEC §7's "spread" (departures M5-1 and M5-2 in README; decisions D1-D6 in `docs/milestones/M5-plan.md`, calls and measurements in `docs/milestones/M5-calls/ARC.md`). Each archetype attacks from an **arc** of the circle round the player; the trickier the enemy, the wider it is.

- **Arcs are data.** `EnemyActor.attack_arc_deg()`: the variant's `arc_deg` when it has one (>= 0), else the tunable `<archetype>_arc_deg` (group "Enemies", subgroup "Attack arcs": Punk 90, Thug 140, Bruiser 180, boss 270, Kicker 360), else 360 (the dummy, a test stub: M3's behaviour). `EnemyVariant` (`game/enemies/enemy_variant.gd`, `id`, `base_id`, `label`, `arc_deg`, `tint`; files `game/enemies/variants/<id>.tres`) and an `EnemyCatalog.ENTRIES` row with `variant = "res://..."` make a variant (`punk_veteran`: the Punk's scene, 140, an orange tint). `EnemyCatalog.variant(id)`, `apply_variant(enemy, id)`; `EncounterSpawner.make_enemy()` calls it, so waves, adds and F4 get variants. The tint is the primitives' `body_color` and the art material's `albedo` (the toon shader multiplies it into the palette), applied in `EnemyActor._ready()` (`set_variant()` when later).
- **The engage book** (D1; `EnemyDirector._engage`, driven by `enemy.ai_state`, never a camera). An enemy engages on its first tick in POSTURE or ENGAGE, or on its first APPROACH tick within `arc_engage_radius_m` (clamped to at least `wait_ring_max_m + 0.5`); an approaching enemy stays engaged at any distance; ENTER, WINDUP, ATTACK, RECOVER, REACT and KO end the engagement (the old line and seat are kept), so it engages again when its recovery ends or it is free after a hit. From the engagement until `engage_commit_s` its line is the player's look (`look_yaw`, world degrees); then the line is a fixed world yaw, the arc centred on the player wherever he walks. Read API: `debug_line_of(enemy)` (NAN when not engaged), `is_committed_line(enemy)`, `debug_seat_kind(enemy)` (`&"inner"`, `&"wait"`, `&""`), `debug_arc_slid(enemy)`, `debug_arc_center_of(enemy)` (line plus slide, what an enemy keeps its strafing inside), `debug_wait_ring()`, `blind_spot_eligible(enemy)`.
- **Seats** (D3; `_assign_arcs()`, reassigned as before: a set change, the player moving `director_reslot_move_m`, every `director_reslot_s`, a stall, and now an engaged line more than `director_slot_retry_deg` from the one its seat was made for). Inner seats lie on the posture ring inside the arc on a grid (as many as fit `director_seat_gap_deg`, evenly across the arc: a 90 degree arc has -45, 0, +45), each enemy at its own radius (a hash of its name), nearest to where it stands. Keepers go first: token holders, then the longest engaged whose seat still lies inside its arc; then the rest, longest engaged first (an enemy that never engaged is last, nearest first), take a free inner seat, else a wait seat (the wait ring `wait_ring_min_m`..`wait_ring_max_m`, inside the arc, the angle it had, no two bodies nearer than two avoidance radii), so the longest waiter takes the next inner seat that frees. A Kicker that is flanking (below) is outside the grid and takes M3's seat nearest directly behind the look; one that is not is an ordinary arc enemy (360 degrees about its line). `EnemyActor.set_slot(pos, kind)` records `seat_kind`; on a wait seat `_tick_posture()` keeps to the wait ring facing the player, and strafing never goes past the arc's edge (`_keep_in_arc()`).
- **Room and rejects.** `_seat_point()` is the one seat test (`_snap_slot()` uses it too): snapped onto the navmesh within `director_slot_snap_tolerance_m` (flat), within `director_slot_max_rise_m` of the player's floor (a seat past a catwalk's lip must not land on the street below), and reachable (a path from the player ends at it; cached per reslot). Crowd-blocked arcs wait; geometry-blocked arcs slide: a seat that fails tries other angles in the arc, then (inner and wait) radii stepping down (to the ring's near edge, or `posture_ring_max_m + 1`), and an arc with no point on the player's floor at all slides round in `director_slot_retry_deg` steps to the nearest stretch with room (`debug_arc_slid`); with nothing anywhere the enemy takes the navmesh point nearest the wait ring.
- **Tokens and the blind spot** (D4, D5). `_grant()` takes only inner-seated waiters (a boss that has just woken in place inside its own arc is served first; outside it, it walks to an inner seat). The blind-spot roll is for waiters whose `arc_deg / 2 > rear_warning_angle_deg` (`blind_spot_needs_wide_arc`): not a Punk, a veteran yes.
- **The A/B switch** `director_even_spread` (Debug, off; presets never hold it) brings all of M3's director back: the even spread (kinds all inner, no wait seats, no line), a token to any waiter, the blind roll for any enemy. `test_enemy_director.gd`'s M3 spread tests and a twin mixed-six soak in `test_m3_done_when.gd` set it. A Debug value is not reset by `Tuning.reset_to_default()`: a test that sets it resets it.
- **F3** (`CombatDebugDraw._add_arc_marks()`): each engaged enemy's arc as two floor lines and a dim center line from the player (`arc_color`; a slid arc in `arc_slid_color`; coinciding arcs once; none for 360), and the wait ring (`wait_ring_color`).
- **Instruments** (D6): the probe's R5 counts inner-seated posturers within `posture_ring_max_m + 0.5`, R6 the inner-seated ones' distance; the M3 populations stay in the JSON as `front_wedge_crowding_all` and `ring_adherence_all` (rows R5a, R6a, information) beside the twins R5w (`wait_seat_inside`: no wait-seated enemy inside 5 m) and R6w (`wait_ring_adherence`: median and 10th percentile within the wait ring plus or minus 0.5 m); R9 counts a waiting enemy only when it holds an inner seat and "out of view" only for a blind-eligible one, n/a when none waited.
- **Tests and acts:** `tests/test_attack_arcs.gd` (stubs under a real director on a flat floor, `tests/fixtures/arc_wall_fixture.tscn` and `two_floor_fixture.tscn` with a baked navmesh, and real Punks), `tests/bot/acts/arc_acts.gd` ("Arcs: six Punks", "turn your back", "mixed six", "quick turns").
- **Opportunistic flanking** (FEEDBACK, Game-boy's playtest: a one-on-one with a Kicker was it running round him the whole fight). `EnemyDirector._track_flanking()` (each tick, after the engage book, before the seats) decides per Kicker (`prefers_blind_side`) and tells it with `EnemyActor.set_flanking(on)`; `is_flanking()` reads it back (false by default, the director the only writer; `debug_is_flanking(enemy)` on the director). A Kicker flanks only while the player is **busy**, meaning busy with an enemy he faces other than this Kicker: at least one other live enemy that is not ENTER or KO and not itself flanking stands inside the player's view (`is_outside_view(pos, rear_warning_angle_deg)` false) within `kicker_flank_busy_radius_m` (4.5, the token reach: `posture_ring_max_m` 4.0 plus `token_grant_margin_m` 0.5; a plain default) **and** is as near the middle of the view as this Kicker or nearer (`_off_middle_deg()`, the absolute look bearing; a tie counts as busy, so two Kickers side by side never both stay in front). A Kicker out of the view is farther from the middle than anything in it, so behind him the rule is the plain one. The Kicker he **faces** is the one inside the view that no such enemy beats (`_is_faced()`: in view and not busy): turning onto a flanking Kicker with a Punk still in view beside it ends the busy-ness on that tick. Hysteresis per Kicker on the director's clock (`_clock_s`): flanking starts once busy has held `kicker_flank_on_s` (0.5, the engage grace, unchanged so a glance away from the Kicker does not send it round) and stops once it has been false `kicker_flank_off_s` (1.0) when the other enemy left the view or the radius or went down, or `kicker_flank_faced_off_s` (0.0, the tick he faces it) when the Kicker is the one he faces: "when he turns on it, it fights". The Kickers are decided one after the other in instance-id order, each seeing the ones before it this tick, so two Kickers never wait on each other: the first stays in front and the second may flank. A change of mode sets `_reslot_pending`, and a Kicker that has just stopped flanking is a seeker for that reslot (it does not keep the seat behind the look). Flanking is today's behaviour exactly (the seat nearest directly behind the look, the edge-ward strafe, `kicker_circle_mult`); not flanking, a Kicker goes through keepers, seekers, `_find_inner()` and `_find_wait()` like a Punk with its 360 degree arc, strafes as the base does and circles at the base pace, so alone it squares up where it stands and keeps its seat as the player turns. A token holder keeps its token and its attack until the attack ends (the holder rule), so a Kicker winding up behind finishes it from there; one that stops flanking while it holds a token is a seeker for the reslot all the same (its old seat is behind a look it may never have reached), so it takes the free seat nearest where it stands at once, the attack unaffected (`set_slot()` resets only the progress counters; tested). `kicker_flank_opportunistic` off, or `director_even_spread` on, is the old rule: a Kicker always flanks. Unchanged: the blind-spot priority and the other archetypes' arcs. Tests: `tests/test_kicker_flank.gd`; acts `Kicker one on one`, `Kicker opportunist`, `Kicker left alone` (the Punk dropped to 1 HP while the Kicker is behind) and `Kicker flanks` in `tests/bot/acts/kicker_acts.gd`.
- **Stub fixture:** `tests/fixtures/stub_enemy.gd`'s `set_slot()` takes the new `kind` argument (an override cannot take fewer parameters than the base).

### Level kit (KIT)

Files: `game/level/kit/` (`level_root.gd`, `level_section.gd`, `arena.gd`, `spawn_point.gd`, `barrier.gd`, `hazard_volume.gd`, `edge_guard.gd`, `pickup_spot.gd`, `level_block.gd`, `level_stairs.gd`), `game/ui/go_cue.gd`, the finite mode of `game/level/encounter_spawner.gd` and `encounter_table.gd`, the level rule of `nav_baker.gd`, the test strip `tests/fixtures/level_strip{,_a,_b}.tscn` with `tests/fixtures/strip/arena_{a,b}.tres`. Tests: `tests/test_level_kit.gd` and the finite-mode tests at the end of `tests/test_encounter_spawner.gd`. Tunables: "Level" (Arena locks, Drop-offs, GO cue).

**Sections** (`LevelSection`): a scene in its own space, `Entry` at the origin, the walk along local -Z, its first floor at y 0. Children by name: `Entry`, `Exit` (Marker3D), optional `Start` (the player's place; the first section's is the level's), `Geometry`, `Route` (Marker3Ds in walking order through every arena and up every stair), the `Arena` nodes (anywhere below), optional `ExitBarrier` (the seam barrier at `Exit`); export `floor_heights` (local y of each walkable floor). `LevelRoot` lays them end to end, section k+1's `Entry` on section k's `Exit`, no rotation. Every number of a layout is a node transform or an export of a kit node; no kit script holds a layout number.

**`LevelRoot`** (group `level_root`): exports `section_paths`, `start_arena`, `with_presentation`, `waves_enabled_on_start`, `rng_seed`, the nav numbers (`nav_*`, as the crowd room's) and `void_depth_m`. `_ready()`: environment; sections (a path with no scene, or no `LevelSection`, is skipped with a warning; one section is a one-section level); the arenas wired (a first arena with no behind barrier takes the previous section's `ExitBarrier`, a last arena with no ahead one its own); every barrier closed and every `EdgeGuard` on, the navmesh baked from the `Sections` node, barriers back to their start state; the player at `Start` (`RoomKit.spawn_player`: auto-revive, so a KO stands him back up in place and the arena stays locked); `EnemyDirector`; **the one `EncounterSpawner`** (stopped, an empty table); pickups; the presentation layer. API: `player`, `director`, `spawner`, `nav_region`, `sections`, `player_start`, `player_yaw_deg`, `enemies()`, `arenas() -> Array[Arena]`, `current_arena()` (the locked one, else the first not cleared, else the last), `route_points() -> PackedVector3Array`, `first_route_point(arena) -> Vector3` (INF if none), `next_target(arena) -> Vector3` (the next arena's first route point, INF after the last: the GO cue's target), `start_at_arena(index)` (earlier arenas cleared and open with no signals, the rest waiting at their start state, the spawner stopped, AI enemies removed, the player at the arena's first route point looking along the route, at `Start` for arena 0). An AI enemy that falls `void_depth_m` below the lowest floor is removed. The room contract for bots is the crowd room's: `player`, `player_start`, `with_presentation`, `rng_seed`, `director`, `spawner`, `nav_region`, `enemies()`.

**Arenas and locks** (D9): `Arena` has `state` (`WAITING`, `LOCKED`, `CLEARED`), `armed`, signals `locked` and `cleared`, `lock()`, `clear()`, `contains(point)` (inside the `LockTrigger` Area3D's `BoxShape3D`, a geometric test, no physics frame needed), `past_inset(point)`, `respawn_position()`, `spawn_point_list()`. While WAITING and armed it watches the player each physics frame: inside the volume and `Tuning.arena_lock_inset_m` past the behind barrier (measured along the barrier's walk direction) locks it. `lock()` closes the behind barrier and emits `locked` and `CombatEvents.arena_locked`; `LevelRoot` then calls `spawner.play_table(arena.table, arena.spawn_point_list())`. When the spawner says `table_finished`, `LevelRoot` calls `clear()`: the ahead barrier opens, `cleared` and `CombatEvents.arena_cleared` fire, the next arena is armed, and the last arena's clear emits `CombatEvents.level_cleared`. Ahead barriers have `start_closed`, behind ones start open; adjacent arenas share the barrier between them (arena 1's ahead is arena 2's behind). Where a stair joins two arenas the barrier is at the stair's head and the stair is in the arena below: its volume runs up the flight to the barrier. Every `SpawnPoint` and its `LanePoint` lie inside its own arena. `SpawnPoint` (Marker3D): `tag`, a `LanePoint` child, `as_point()` -> `{position, yaw, tag, lane_point, name}` (`name` is the node's name, what a table's `wave_points` calls it).

**Barriers** (`Barrier`, three kinds): the opening spans local X (`width_m`), rises `height_m` from local y 0, local -Z is the walk through it (a barrier in a wall running the other way is rotated 180 degrees about Y). `SHUTTER` rolls up above the opening, `GATE` slides sideways by its width (`open_side`), `CAR` is parked cars nose to tail (`car_length_m`) that slide aside. Closed: a world-layer collider `thickness_m` deep; open: none (`close()`/`open()` set it at once and the panel follows over `barrier_close_s`/`barrier_open_s`; `moved(closed)` fires on arrival; `snap()` jumps). `is_closed()` is logical (the collider), `progress()` the panel. `past(point)` is how far past it a point is along the walk.

**Edges and falls** (D8): `EdgeGuard` (a `LevelBlock`, never drawn) is on layer 9 only; `Tuning.level_edge_guard` puts its collider on no layer (it listens to `Tuning.changed`; the bake forces it on with `set_baking()`). `HazardVolume` (Area3D, `kind &"drop"`, `size`, a catch floor slab at its bottom) polls its overlaps every physics frame: an `EnemyActor` in it in any state is KO'd (a killing `HitInfo` with no attacker, or, for a flying body, its health taken: it lands KO'd on the catch floor), once, and `CombatEvents.hazard_ko` fires; an invulnerable (getting up) or guarding one is retried next frame. **Make the volume cover the whole void past the lip** (a launched body flies far) and put its catch floor at a depth no floor height is within the bake's tolerance of (the bake would keep it as an island). The player is the fall rule's: with `level_edge_guard` off, entering the volume costs `level_fall_damage` (none in god mode, never his last health), puts him at the `respawn` node's or the current arena's `Respawn` marker and makes him invulnerable `level_fall_invuln_s`.

**Level geometry** (`LevelBlock`, `@tool`): `size`, `look` (`wall`, `floor`, `accent`, `dark`, `prop`, `fence`), `solid`; a solid block is a world-layer `StaticBody3D` through `RoomKit.add_box`, otherwise `RoomKit.box_mesh`. `LevelStairs` (`@tool`): `rise_m` (the whole climb), `run_m`, `width_m`, `max_step_rise_m` (0.18): the smallest step count that keeps every rise at or under it, each step a box from the floor up (world layer: bodies lying on the flight rest on them). A capsule cannot climb a box step (a 0.18 m edge is a 60 degree wall to a 0.35 m capsule), so a hidden **glide ramp** on layer 9, laid along the tips of the steps a hair above them (`glide_lift_m`), carries every walking body; `footprint()` is the AABB the nav bake keeps. `PickupSpot` (Marker3D): `weapon_scene` (the pipe), `place(host)` at level start.

**Navigation** (`NavBaker.bake` settings `floor_heights`, `floor_tolerance`, `floor_lift`, `stair_footprints`; `max_floor_y` still works for the crowd room): the parse takes layers 1 and 9; a polygon is kept when its vertices lie within `floor_tolerance` of a floor height (plus `floor_lift`, the height the baked polygons sit above the floor) or its centre is inside a stair footprint; prop and wall tops go. The bake runs with every barrier closed, so each arena is its own island (the barrier gap is more than the edge-connection margin plus two agent radii), and the guard keeps the mesh an agent radius short of every edge.

**Spawner finite mode** (D10): `EncounterTable.loop` false plays once (`wave_at` never wraps and never inserts the boss wave), `wave_points` names each id's spawn point (`points_for(index)`). `EncounterSpawner.play_table(table, points)`, `stop()`, `signal table_finished()`, `static remove_enemy(enemy)`; point dictionaries gain `name`. A named spawn is at its point when free; if it is not (the player is nearer than `spawn_min_player_distance_m`, or there is no room) it waits `spawn_named_wait_s`, then takes the nearest free point with the same tag, and after a further `spawn_named_any_wait_s` the nearest free point of any tag (a unique-tag point held by the player cannot stall the wave), and retries every 0.1 s meanwhile. Named spawns are outside the behind share (its quota counts the spawns that choose). `waves_enabled` is still the global switch. The crowd room is unchanged. **Behind points** (FEEDBACK, Game-boy's playtest: the hold made a lull and then a hit from behind): a point flagged `out_of_view` (`SpawnPoint.out_of_view`, the six "behind" points of the street and roofs) is used like any named point, at once when it is free and not nearer than `spawn_min_player_distance_m`, with the tag fallback after `spawn_named_wait_s` only when the point itself is not free. Only with the tunable `spawn_behind_waits_out_of_view` on (Level group, default off) does `_spawn_named()` hold it while the point is inside the player's view and fall back to the nearest free point outside the view after `spawn_named_wait_s` (any tag, then `spawn_named_any_wait_s` more to any free point): M5's behaviour, exactly. `wave_behind_share` (which picks a point, never waits) is unchanged.

**`GoCue`** (`game/ui/go_cue.gd`, built by `GameHud._ready()`, stepped by `GameHud.advance()`): a comic panel above the crosshair. On `CombatEvents.arena_cleared` it reads `LevelRoot.next_target()` and shows "GO" with an arrow for `go_cue_s`, the arrow turned by `Player.look_bearing_deg()` (up: ahead, right: to his right, down: behind); on `level_cleared` it shows "CLEAR!" and stays (`reset()` hides it). The arrow is two pieces of triangles (`arrow_triangles(angle, length, scale, grow)`, drawn with `draw_primitive`): one polygon failed the engine's triangulation at a few turns. Presentation only: it subscribes and reads.

**The strip**: `level_strip_a.tscn` (a 16 m street: a parked-car barrier behind arena 1, three spawn points (a doorway recess near the car, an alley, a vehicle), a 25-step stair up 4.5 m along the right wall to a landing, a shutter at the stair head as the `ExitBarrier`) and `level_strip_b.tscn` (a terrace on the building: a chain-link gate ahead of arena 2, a stairwell and a doorway recess and a vehicle spawn, a pipe, an edge guard and a 40 x 60 m hazard volume along the open right edge, the street drawn far below), chained by `level_strip.tscn`. Tables: `strip/arena_a.tres` (two Punks, then two Punks and a Thug, the Thug named at "Alley") and `strip/arena_b.tres` (three Punks), both finite. Play it: `godot --path . tests/fixtures/level_strip.tscn`.

### Level-run bot (BOT)

Files: `tests/bot/level_bot.{gd,tscn}`, `tests/bot/level_acts/run_acts.gd`, `tools/record_acts.sh` (`BOT_SCENE`), `tests/test_level_bot.gd`. No tunables: every number is an `@export` of the bot (groups Run, Walk, Fight, Knock-off).

`level_bot.gd` extends `crowd_bot.gd` (captions, scripted player, probe, autopilot, the log lines) and plays a `LevelRoot` arena by arena. Its acts come only from `tests/bot/level_acts/*_acts.gd` (`_collect_acts()` is overridden); `_reset()` is the crowd bot's rewritten for a level (it cannot call it: that one casts the room to `CrowdRoom`): every act starts with `start_at_arena(from_arena)`, the waves off, the pilot knobs, god mode and the GO cue reset. The HUD's help and combat-log panels are hidden by the crowd bot's `_run()` (they are children of the level), so recordings show the plain first-person view.

Arguments after `--` (`CAPTURE_ARGS` for `capture_bot.sh` and `record_acts.sh`): `--level=<res:// path>` (a `LevelRoot` scene, or a lone `LevelSection` scene which is wrapped as a one-section level; default `tests/fixtures/level_strip.tscn`), `--from=<arena>`, `--to=<arena>` (0-based; default the first and the last), `--presentation=0` (no cameras or HUD). `BOT_SCENE=res://tests/bot/level_bot.tscn` makes `tools/record_acts.sh` record this bot (default the crowd bot); the plain overview sheets for acts without LOOK-MARK lines are kept.

API (for act files and tests): `walk_route(arena_index = -1) -> bool` (along `LevelRoot.route_points()` until that arena locks; the look eases round to each point at `walk_turn_deg_per_s` with `walk_turn_ease`, forward is held once the look is within `walk_forward_within_deg`; a closed barrier on the straight line to the next point stops the player `barrier_stop_m` short and the walk gives up after `barrier_wait_s`, logging `BLOCKED`), `walk_to(point, tolerance, budget_ticks, until = Callable()) -> bool`, `run_arena(held_back = false, measured = true) -> bool` (walks in until the current arena locks, then the autopilot fights until it clears or `arena_budget_s` runs out; with `measured` the readability probe, lock meter and crowd meter run per arena, titled "<act caption>: <arena id>"; the held-back pilot starts walking up after `stall_approach_s` without a hit), `run_level(from = 0, to = -1, held_back = false, measured = true) -> bool` (waves on; each arena in turn, a `go_pause_s` stand between them so the gate and the GO cue are seen; `finish_pause_s` after the last), `knock_off(from = 0) -> bool` and `first_hazard(from = 0) -> Dictionary` (`{arena, hazard}`: the first arena from `from` on (the act's `--from=`) with a hazard of its own section within `knock_hazard_reach_m` of its lock box that reaches its floor height (the catwalk's drop above the terrace is not the terrace's), and that arena's hazard nearest its first route point). The act `Look: seams` (`level_acts/seam_acts.gd`) stands the player at 17 spots round the street/roofs and roofs/boss-roof seams, each barrier closed and open (`SEAM <n> <label> f<frame>`).

Log lines, each with its physics frame: `ARENA+ f<frame> <id>`, `WAVE f<frame> <id> <n>`, `ARENA- f<frame> <id> <seconds>` (since the lock), `LEVEL f<frame> <seconds>` (since `run_level` began, pauses included), `HAZARD f<frame> <enemy> <hazard>`, and the failure lines `STALL`, `BLOCKED`, `NOTE`. Acts (level-agnostic): "Run: held back" (held-back autopilot, real damage, probe per arena), "Run: knock-off" (in the first arena with a hazard volume a Punk is launched over the edge), "Run: timing" (god mode, full-speed autopilot, no probe; run it headless with `--presentation=0` to log `LEVEL`).

### Mission 1: the street (STREET)

Files: `game/level/mission1/street.tscn` (a `LevelSection`), `game/level/mission1/encounters/a{1,2,3}.tres`, `tests/test_m5_street.gd`. No tunables.

**Layout** (section space, `Entry` at the origin, the walk along -Z, floors at y 0 and 4.5): a 12 m wide street (x -6..6) between building walls (x +-6..8) running z +3 to -53; `Start` (3, 0, 0.8) in a pocket behind the first car; `Exit` (3, 4.5, -53), the landing under the seam shutter (`ExitBarrier`, a SHUTTER 6 x 3.5, x 0..6). `floor_heights` [0, 4.5]. Every barrier is 6 m wide in a neck on the x 0..6 half, the other half closed by a barricade or fence panel:
- `StartCar` (CAR, z -1.5, A1 behind, starts open, slides right into a parking lot in the right wall), `Gate` (GATE, z -14, A1 ahead / A2 behind, slides left along a fence panel), `YardCar` (CAR, z -30, A2 ahead / A3 behind, a lot in the right wall), `ExitBarrier` (SHUTTER, z -53, y 4.5, A3 ahead).

**Arenas** (lock volumes, level space z): A1 -1.5..-14 (`street_a1`), A2 -14..-30 (`street_a2`), A3 -30..-53 and up to y 4.5 (`street_a3`; the stair and the landing belong to it). Spawn points (each with a `LanePoint`, inside its own arena):

| Arena | Points (tag) | Table (`loop = false`, `wave_points`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | `Doorway` (doorway, dock shutter recess, right), `Alley` (alley, left) | punk Doorway, punk Alley |
| A2 | `Alley` (alley, rear left), `Office` (doorway, right), `VanL`, `VanR` (vehicle, the van's back doors, left) | wave 1 punk Alley, punk Office, punk VanL; wave 2 punk VanL, punk Office, thug VanR |
| A3 | `Brick` (doorway, right, rear), `WarehouseA`, `WarehouseB` (doorway, the warehouse shutter, left), `LandingA`, `LandingB` (stairwell, on the landing) | wave 1 punk LandingA, punk LandingB; wave 2 punk Brick, punk WarehouseA, thug WarehouseB |

**Stair 1**: a `LevelStairs` at (4.5, 0, -41), rise 4.5, run 7.5, width 3 (x 3..6, head at z -48.5); the landing is a solid block x 0..6, z -48.5..-53. Its open edges (the landing's -X edge, its front edge beside the head, the stair's -X side) each have an `EdgeGuard` and a non-solid rail; there is no hazard volume (the drop is to the street, in the same arena).

**Set dressing** (all `LevelBlock`s): dock and office doorway recesses with dark backs, a roller-shutter recess for the warehouse, two crate alleys, two parked-car lots, crate stacks along the walls, a van, a chain-link fence along A2's left, non-solid skyline and the view beyond the fence. Solid blocks lie inside z +4..-53, x -11.4..13.4; nothing is solid past the `Exit`.

**Tests**: `tests/test_m5_street.gd` (17).

### Mission 1: the roofs (ROOF)

Files: `game/level/mission1/roofs.tscn`, `game/level/mission1/encounters/a{4,5,6}.tres`, `tests/test_m5_roofs.gd`. No tunables, no script: the scene is a `LevelSection` built only of kit nodes, written by a throwaway generator (the `.tscn` is the source). `Entry` is the origin, level (3, 4.5, -53) (the seam shutter's centre, so local = plan - that); `Exit` is local (19, 7.5, -30), the A6/A7 gate's centre; `ExitBarrier` is that gate (`GATE`, 4 m, closed). `floor_heights` [0, 1, 4.5, 7.5]. A `Start` marker (local (-3, 0, -2), on the terrace) serves the lone section; in the level only the first section's `Start` is used.

**Arenas** (ids `a4_terrace`, `a5_catwalk`, `a6_top_roof`; each `Arena` has `LockTrigger`, `Respawn` and three or four `SpawnPoint`s with a `LanePoint`; every point and lane point is inside its own box, and the boxes overlap no other arena's point):
- **A4**: 10 m wide, 28 m long, floor 0 then a 6-step `LevelStairs` flight (1 m) at plan z -65.2..-67, floor 1.0. Behind: STREET's seam shutter (bound by `LevelRoot`). Ahead: the gate at stair 2's head (`GateStair2`, plan (5, 9, -79.5), turned -90 degrees to walk +X, sliding into the far wall). Stair 2 (3.5 m, 6 m run, 3 m wide, plan x -1..5, z -78..-81) is in A4's box, so the arena below owns it. Spawns, all door recesses in the right wall (3 m wide, 1.5 deep): `RoofDoorBehind` (z -57.5), `DoorAhead` (-63.5), `DoorPastStep` (-74). Open edge: the whole -X side.
- **A5**: 10 m wide, walked +Z, floor 4.5. Behind: `GateStair2`. Ahead: `ShutterStair3` at stair 3's head (plan (15, 12, -62.5), turned -90). Stair 3 (3 m, 6 m run) is in A5's box. Spawns: `DoorFar` (+Z end wall), `DoorSide` (+X wall), `DoorBehindStair` (the far wall by stair 2's head). Open edge: the -X side from z -78 to -61, over the terrace.
- **A6**: 14 m wide, 24 m long, floor 7.5. Behind: `ShutterStair3`. Ahead: the exit gate. Spawns: `BigShutter` (a penthouse's recess, shutter doorway), `Alley` (between two crate stacks against the parapet), `BehindDoor` and `RoofAccess` (recesses in the +Z wall). A solid parapet (1.4 m) closes the -X side over the catwalk. Open edge: the +X side.
Boxes in plan coordinates: A4 x -5.5..6.8, y 4..9.8, z -81..-52.5; A5 x 5..16.7, y 8.5..11.7, z -82.8..-59.2; A6 x 15..29, y 11.8..18, z -82.8..-57.3 (A5 and A6 are split by height where they share x and z).

**Tables** (`a4..a6.tres`, `loop = false`, `wave_points` parallel to `waves`): A4 [punk@DoorAhead, kicker@RoofDoorBehind], [punk@DoorPastStep, punk@DoorAhead, kicker@RoofDoorBehind]; A5 [punk@DoorFar, punk@DoorBehindStair], [bruiser@DoorFar, punk@DoorBehindStair]; A6 [thug@BigShutter, punk@BigShutter, punk@Alley, bruiser@Alley, kicker@BehindDoor, punk_veteran@RoofAccess], [punk@BigShutter, punk@Alley, kicker@BehindDoor]. That is 5, 4 and 9 enemies, the encounter plan's order and spawn sides.

**Drops**: each open edge has an `EdgeGuard` just outside the floor edge (layer 9 only), a drawn curb and a low rail with gaps (non-solid posts and bars), and a `HazardVolume` over the whole void past the guard. `DropFront` (-X of the terrace, from the guard out 55 m, up to plan y 12) and `DropEast` (+X of the top roof) have a catch floor at plan y -0.8, the street's ground, which no floor height is within 0.3 m of; non-solid ground drawn there and skyline blocks beyond. `DropCatwalk` (the catwalk's -X side, over the terrace) has no catch floor of its own: it starts at plan y 7.9 so a standing or lightly launched enemy on the terrace below never touches it, and a body knocked off the catwalk is KO'd at the lip and lies out its KO on the terrace.

**Nav links**: four `NavigationLink3D`s (`Links`: the step flight's foot and head, stair 2's foot, stair 3's foot) join the flights to their floors. The nav bake drops a polygon that straddles a flight's foot (its centre outside the stair footprint, its top more than a floor-band above the floor), and whether the join survives depends on where the bake's cell grid falls, which moves with the level's bounds; a flight failed to connect in one bake and not in another. The links make the join independent of the grid. Stair 2 and stair 3's heads join no floor (the gates are there).

**Pickup**: one `PickupSpot` (the pipe) 1.4 m in front of the big shutter's recess.

**Tests**: `tests/test_m5_roofs.gd` (13): the section contract, the tables against the plan, every point inside its own arena and on the navmesh, islands and flights, every floor height on the navmesh, the route, no solid block outside the footprint, each arena's table on the probe stub (named points, never over six), a Punk launched off each edge is KO'd, the guard holds the player at every edge, the pipe, A6's six real enemies on the roof, and the lone section under the level bot (god mode, three arenas, no STALL or BLOCKED, no hazard KO).

### Mission 1: the boss roof and the level (FINALE)

Files: `game/level/mission1/boss_roof.tscn` (section 3), `game/level/mission1/mission1.tscn` (the level), `game/level/mission1/encounters/a7.tres`, `ROOM_SCENES` in `game/autoload/game_input.gd`, `run/main_scene` in `project.godot`. Tests: `tests/test_m5_done_when.gd`, the two F6 tests (`test_f6_switches_rooms_and_the_help_lists_it` in `test_m3_contracts.gd`, `test_f6_cycles_level_crowd_street_training_room` in `test_crowd_room.gd`). Calls: `docs/milestones/M5-calls/FINALE.md`. No tunables.

**`mission1.tscn`** is a `LevelRoot` whose `section_paths` are `street.tscn`, `roofs.tscn` and `boss_roof.tscn`; it is the main scene. `ROOM_SCENES` is now level, crowd room, training room, and F6 cycles them (the list wraps; a room whose scene does not exist is skipped by `room_after()` giving ""). The level is the only scene that holds the three section paths: a missing one is skipped by `LevelRoot` with a warning.

**`boss_roof.tscn`** (A7, a `LevelSection`; `Entry` at the A6/A7 gate's centre, local -Z is the walk, floor y 0 = level y 12, 20 x 20 m: x -10..10, z 0..-20). One arena `a7` whose table (`a7.tres`) is finite with one wave `["boss"]` named at `RoofDoor`. It has no barrier of its own: the gate behind is the roofs' `ExitBarrier` (a first arena with no `behind_barrier` takes the previous section's), which closes when the lock is on (the player 2 m past it) and **stays closed**; nothing ahead. The lock volume covers the whole roof (z 0..-20.5), so a lone boss roof locks at `Start`. Geometry: a deck on a building block down to street level; the `-X` side a bulkhead wall with two doors (`GateDoor` at z -3 by the gate, `ServiceDoor` at z -11); the far end a roof-access bulkhead with the `RoofDoor` (the boss's entrance) between two chain-link fences; the entry end chain-link fence either side of a 4 m opening for the roofs' gate (the roofs' `ExitBarrier` must be at least 4 m wide); the `+X` side the one open edge: a low rail in two runs with a 4 m gap (z -8..-12), one `EdgeGuard` along all 20 m, a `HazardVolume` (x 10..50, z +20..-40, y -11..+3: it covers the whole void, its catch floor at local y -11, 1 m above the street ground drawn at -12.5) and a non-solid skyline. Spawn points: `RoofDoor` (ahead, tag `doorway`), `ServiceDoor` (side), `GateDoor` (behind), each with a `LanePoint` 4 m into the roof; the doors are dark non-solid slabs on the wall faces. `Route`: `R0` (0, 0, -3), `R1` (0, 0, -12). The boss's adds come through `spawn_adds()` at the free points nearest him (the `ServiceDoor` and `GateDoor` when he stands at the middle). Last arena: its clear emits `level_cleared` ("CLEAR!").

**The z -83 seam (FIX2)**: the boss roof owns the fence along it (`EntryFenceL` x 15.5..20, `EntryFenceR` x 24..32 in level space, 3.5 m high, no post); the roofs' `ExitBarrier` (4 m, x 20..24) is the only opening, and the roofs' terrace tower (`FarWall1`, x 11.5..15.5) closes the rest to its left. The roofs have no fence of their own there; `ParapetWest` and `PenthouseBody` start at z -82.4, clear of it. `test_no_two_sections_solid_blocks_overlap` holds it. A4's first Route point (R0) is 2.5 m past the seam shutter, so `start_at_arena(3)` (and `level_bot --from=3`) locks A4; `start_at_arena(0)` stands the player at the street's `Start`, outside A1, and the done-when test walks him in (`_walk_into`).

**Done-when** (`tests/test_m5_done_when.gd`): the real level, seeded, god mode and `one_hit_ko`, `wave_gap_s` 1.0 and `wave_spawn_interval_s` 0.2 (restored by the test), the autopilot with `approach_max_m` 40, each arena through `start_at_arena(k)`: it locks, `spawner` spawns exactly its table (plus `boss_adds_count` for A7), never over 6 alive, its behind barrier closed all through the fight, it clears inside 1800 frames and opens its ahead barrier, in level order; the readability probe's hard rows hold per arena; the boss is set to half health by the test, calls his two Punks in at A7's points and the last clear emits `level_cleared` once; all seven arenas and 34 enemies when every section exists; no two sections' solid blocks overlap (an `EdgeGuard` is not solid scenery and is left out); the boss roof alone (door, edge guard at the rail and at the gap, a launched Punk KO'd); the level is the main scene and F6's first room. The file's wall-time budget is 120 s.

## Full art and audio (Milestone 6)

The plan is [milestones/M6-plan.md](milestones/M6-plan.md). The art only draws (its D1): colliders, the navmesh, hurtboxes and the hidden hit-test proxies are M5's, so every M3-M5 number holds. Each package fills its own heading below with the contract as it landed.

### Step 0: shared declarations (the lead)

- `CombatEvents.section_entered(section)` (LevelRoot emits it: KIT), `barrier_moving(barrier, closing)` (Barrier emits it at the top of `close()` and `open()`, only when the state changes) and `weapon_landed(weapon)` (WEAPONS emits it).
- `LevelSection.ambience: StringName` (the bed's pool; empty is the default bed) and `MoveData.impact_sfx: StringName` (the weapon-hit pool, `weapon_<impact_sfx>_hit`; empty for fists and feet).
- The palette ranges of the plan's §2 "Palette slots" (`punk.json` 16-21, `player.json` 32-37; no swatch moved).

### The art chain for every character (CHAIN)

M4's chain and tests are per-character: a new character adds a folder, a roster file and a strain file, and edits no test.

- **The roster** (`tests/art_roster.gd`, `tests/fixtures/art/roster/<id>.json`): `{id, label, art, actor_scene, height_m, budget, meshes, moves, ratchets, notes}`. `ArtRoster.ids()` (the Punk first), `row(id)` (defaults filled: height 1.8, budget 15000, `["Body"]`, the plan's bars in `ratchets`), `rows()`, `problems(id)`, `moves_of(row)`, `lens_bar(row, "<move> pitch +8")`, `glb_of(row)`, `strain_file(id)`, `shipped_row(id)`. `ratchets` holds only what departs from the plan's bar: `a5_extent`, `a6` ({torso, legs, head, axis_outside}), `a9_cells`, `a9_lens_pp` (a number, or `{"*": bar, "<pose>": bar}`), `a9_strike_art_share_min` (the twin of a loosened lens gap), `anchor_m`, `stretch_m`, `variant_stretch_m`, `slide_m`; a ratchet only moves down and each has its reason in `notes`. The player is not in it: his row and the `*_PLAYER` constants stay in `test_m4_done_when.gd` (ARM changes them).
- **Strain** (`tests/fixtures/strain/<id>.json` = `{id, whole, clips}`; `strain_ratchet.json` keeps only the hero's table). `ArtStrain.SHIPPED` is a `static var`: `[punk (from his file), player (PLAYER_ROW)]`, so `test_skinned_art` is untouched; a roster character's row is `ArtRoster.shipped_row(id)` and its moves `ArtStrain.roster_moves(id)`. `tests/fixtures/strain/gen_strain_ratchet.tscn -- <id>...` writes the files (a new file's whole-run figures are today's plus 5%; an existing file's are kept).
- **The rows**: every A1-A12 row of `test_m4_done_when.gd` loops over `_arts()` (the roster, then the hero), at each row's `height_m`, with its own moves and ratchets (`_rig(row)` no longer hard-codes 1.8); A9's framing and A7's visible stretch run per roster character over idle and its `moves`. `test_art_punk.gd` reads the Punk's scene, height, budget, moves and ratchets from his file. `test_art_contract.gd`'s `budgets()` and `_id_of` read the roster (`BUDGETS` keeps the hero and the probe), and its report-keys test compares every roster report with the Punk's. `tests/test_art_roster.gd`: each file complete, its art attaches at its height (`has_art()`), its actor scene wears it, its report and pipeline exist and hash its GLBs, its strain file has a figure for every clip; `tests/test_m6_tripo_ledger.gd`: the M6 rows (every task finished, at most 900, balance at least 700; A13 still reads M4's).
- **Warm-up (D27)**: `RoomKit.catalog_arts()` reads the `art` of every `EnemyCatalog` scene (no instantiation); `warm_catalog_arts()` makes each art's `SkinnedArt` entry and holds the arts for the run (`held_catalog_arts()`, `release_catalog_arts()`); `warm_up_art()` calls it in a window, headless it still adds nothing. A new character's art is warmed by having its scene in the catalog.
- **The dummy (D17)**: `tools/pipeline/make_probe_body.py` gains `--regions <json>`, `--out <dir>` and `--name <name>` (none given: the probe, byte for byte, so its report hash and `test_the_probe_report_has_...` hold). `assets/source/characters/dummy/` (`regions.json`, `source/dummy_source.glb`, `pipeline.json`, `report/report.json`), `assets/source/palette/dummy.json` (slots 56-59: charcoal, padding, limb, canvas; none red), `assets/export/characters/dummy/dummy{,_rest}.glb`, `game/actors/art/dummy_art.tres` (15000, no detail mask), `training_dummy.tscn`'s `art`. No first-person split. `python3 -I tools/pipeline/run.py --asset dummy --check` says YES. `TestRoom.dummy_colors` and the per-dummy colour multiply are gone; `TrainingDummy.color`/`accent` still colour its hidden primitives.
- **Look acts**: `tests/bot/acts/roster_acts.gd` makes "Look: <label> at 1 m" for every roster id (C1's face view then C2's body view; the dummy is placed by hand, the spawner makes EnemyActors only).
