- **New character checklist**: the Gemini views and Tripo files under `assets/source/characters/<id>/`, `pipeline.json` (run.py `--asset <id> --check` YES), `assets/source/palette/<id>.json` in its slot range (regenerate the atlas), `game/actors/art/<id>_art.tres`, the scene's `art`, the catalog entry, `tests/fixtures/art/roster/<id>.json` (with a `label`), `tests/fixtures/strain/<id>.json` (run the generator), ASSETS.md rows (the roster and strain files too).

### Damage classes, the veteran's palette, the inventory (LOOKS)

**Damage class by atlas alpha (D6).** A palette swatch may say `"blood": false` (`tools/pipeline/gen_palette.py`): it scuffs and darkens but never takes blood. The atlas alpha is the class: 255 clothes (scuffs, then blood), 230 clothes without blood, 204 skin, 128 none; a glossy swatch is one step lower (254, 229, 203, 127). The decode is one place, mirrored twice: `toon.gdshader` (`takes_marks *= step(0.75, a)`, `skin_marks = 1 - step(0.85, a)`, `no_blood = step(0.85, a) * (1 - step(0.95, a))`, `swatch_gloss()`) and `ToonMaterials.swatch_glossy()`. `blood: false` needs marks and never goes on a skin swatch (gen_palette.py raises). The Punk's slot 17 (cap, laces, strap) carries it, so there is no Punk rebuild.

- **Shader.** One class per swatch, so one triplanar (`damage_triplanar()`, `textureGrad` with derivatives taken before the class branch): the skin tile, the scuff tile (`damage_scuff_tex`, new) or the clothes tile (`damage_tex`). `blood_edge` (new uniform, 0 = off) cuts the look's alpha with a one-pixel edge, for every class but no-blood.
- **`DamageLook.apply_art(material, level)`** sets `damage_tex`, `damage_skin_tex`, `damage_scuff_tex` (the scuffed tile from BRUISED on) and `blood_edge` (`vfx_blood_edge_cut` at BLOODIED, else 0). `DamageLook.art_tile_for_alpha(alpha, level) -> Texture2D` is the CPU mirror of the class branch, for tests. `DamageLook.TUNABLES` gains `vfx_blood_edge_cut` and `look_marks_per_swatch`. Primitives are unchanged (a paint is one class).
- **Tunables.** "VFX" > "Damage looks": `vfx_blood_edge_cut` (0-0.95, 0.5; 0 = M4's soft blend). Debug: `look_marks_per_swatch` (default on; off = M4: the cap takes the blood tile, soft edge).

**The veteran's palette (D4).** `assets/source/palette/variants/<id>.json` = `{"id", "base": "<palette id>", "swatches": [{"slot", "name", "srgb"}]}` writes `assets/export/palette/<id>_atlas.png` (the shared atlas with those slots recoloured). Only rgb changes; a variant never sets `marks`/`skin`/`gloss`/`blood`, and its slots must be in the shared palette or its base's. `gen_palette.py` writes and `--check`s every variant with the shared atlas. `EnemyVariant.palette: Texture2D` (null = the archetype's atlas) is set on the rig's art material's `albedo_tex` by `EnemyActor._apply_variant_look()` (one material per rig, so no other enemy changes); `tint` stays on the resource for a graybox variant but no shipped variant has one (`test_no_shipped_variant_uses_a_tint`). `punk_veteran.json`: vest (slot 16) `#8a3fb0`, trousers (slot 18) `#b8651b`.

**The placeholder inventory.** `tests/test_m6_no_placeholders.gd` builds `mission1.tscn`, `crowd_room.tscn` and `test_room.tscn` headless and counts, per scene, `primitive_bodies`, `tints`, `boxes`, `cylinder_weapons`, `second_weapon`, plus `generated_sfx` and `empty_slots` under `global`, against `tests/fixtures/m6/placeholder_ratchet.json` (`{scene: {category: n}}`, a count may not exceed it). Enemy ids come from the encounter tables (the boss's adds included), the crowd waves and the F4 catalog, each instanced once with its variant. `M6_WRITE_RATCHET=1 tools/run_tests.sh -gselect=test_m6_no_placeholders.gd` re-measures and writes the ratchet; every run prints `PLACEHOLDERS <scene> <category> <n>`. A package that clears a stand-in lowers its numbers in the same commit; the lead re-measures at each merge. Allowed by name: `music_loop`, F3's debug draws, hidden proxies and hidden-layer meshes, `EdgeGuard`, `EdgeQuad`, the warm-up meshes (`ToonWarmUp`, `FlashWarmUp`) and the audition room's legacy bank.

### The environment kit (KIT)

The art only draws (D1): every collider, the navmesh, the hurtboxes and the hidden proxies are M5's, held by `tests/test_m6_colliders.gd` to `tests/fixtures/m6/m5_colliders.json` (231 level colliders, 38 in the crowd room, 14 in the training room, snapshotted from the unchanged level by `tests/m6_collider_snapshot.gd`; `M6_WRITE_COLLIDERS=1` rewrites it on purpose).

- **The sheet.** `assets/export/kit/trim_sheet.png` (2048², mipmaps, alpha border fix off) and `trim_strips.json` come from `assets/source/kit/trim_layout.json` by `godot --headless -s tools/gen_trim.gd` (`-- --check` compares a repaint). 16 strips of 128 px: brick, plaster, concrete, asphalt, kerb, roof_tar, gravel, metal, shutter, chainlink (alpha scissor), crate, trim, ink, glass, dark, facade. Each is seamless both ways; the RGB is the surface, the alpha the ink mask (the wire's coverage on the chain-link). Colours are HSV in the layout and are clamped into the bible's band at paint time (saturation at most 0.30, value 0.25 to 0.6); no ink within a few px of a strip's border or of U = 0. DRESS retunes `hsv` and repaints. `TrimSheet` (`game/level/kit/art/trim_sheet.gd`) reads the table: `index(strip)`, `period_m(strip)`, `uniform_rows()`, `texture()`.
- **The material.** `ToonMaterials.trim()` (`Kind.TRIM`, `game/fx/trim.gdshader`) is ONE shared opaque `ShaderMaterial`, lit by `toon_light.gdshaderinc` like the grid (two bands, matte, no rim, `ROUGHNESS = edge_tag` = 1). A face's UVs are in metres and its vertex colour is the strip (alpha = row / 255) and a tint (rgb); the shader picks the row from the `strips` table, repeats it every `period_m` in V and every `tile_m` in U, and samples with `textureGrad` and a mip cap (`look_trim_lod_cap`) so one wrapped strip never blends into the next. The ink (alpha) is cut at `look_trim_ink_cut` with a one-pixel edge, like `toon.gdshader`'s `detail_cut`. It keeps a flat `albedo` tint (white) a test can read. It is drawn once in `warm_up`. `ToonMaterials.trim_scissor()` (`game/fx/trim_scissor.gdshader`, the same body from `trim_body.gdshaderinc` with `TRIM_SCISSOR` defined) is the one variant: it writes `ALPHA_SCISSOR_THRESHOLD`, for the chain-link's wire only. A shader that writes it, even on a branch, is alpha-clip to the renderer (no depth prepass, the full fragment in every shadow cascade), so no other face wears it (fix-final-perf, `test_m6_perf.gd`).
- **The modules.** `KitMesh.build(kind, size) -> ArrayMesh` (`game/level/kit/art/kit_mesh.gd`), cached per (kind, size), one surface on the shared material (`chainlink` and `gate`: two, the posts and rails on surface 0 and the scissor strip's faces on surface 1 with `trim_scissor()`), at most 5000 triangles, centred on the box `size` it fills. Kinds: `wall`, `floor`, `slab`, `slab_dark`, `deck`, `curb`, `crate`, `skyline`, `rail`, `post`, `housing`, `shutter`, `chainlink`, `gate`, `door_frame`, `window`, `cornice`, `stair_flight` (`KitMesh.build_stairs(width, rise, run, count)` draws exactly LevelStairs' count). `&"box"` and `&""` are not kinds (M5's plain box). UVs are metres on every triangle (tested).
- **The hook.** `RoomKit.add_box(parent, pos, size, material, overlay, art = &"")`, `add_visual_box(..., art)`, `box_mesh(size, material, overlay, art)` and `RoomKit.kit_art(art)`: the same collider, and a kit kind draws the module instead of the box. The crowd room and the training room get it through these (DRESS passes `art`). `Tuning.kit_draw_graybox` (Debug) makes `kit_art()` false everywhere.
- **`LevelBlock`** exports `module: StringName` (`&""`: `MODULE_FOR_LOOK`, wall to `wall`, floor to `floor`, accent to `slab`, dark to `slab_dark`, prop to `crate`, fence to `rail`; `&"box"`: M5's box; any kind) and `draw_box: bool = true` (false hides only the mesh; the editor and the graybox switch draw the box regardless). A prop block's hull goes on the mesh's overlay.
- **Dressing.** `Dressing` (`Node3D`, group `kit_dressing`) holds `KitPiece`s (`MeshInstance3D`: `kind`, `size`) and `PropSpot`s; it sits beside `Geometry` and is never a solid `LevelBlock`; nothing under it collides. `PropSpot` (`prop_id`, `size`, `hull`) stands on the floor at the middle of its footprint and draws `res://game/level/props/<id>.tscn` when it exists, scaled per axis to `size`, else a box of `size` (ZERO: the nominal box of `PropSpot.PROPS`: car 4.2 x 1.4 x 1.9, dumpster 2.0 x 1.3 x 1.0, ac_unit 1.0 x 0.8 x 0.7, water_tank 2.0 x 2.6 x 2.0) in the prop colour with the hull. `PropSpot.scene_path(id)` is "" while PROPS has no scene.
- **Barriers and stairs.** `Barrier` keeps `Panel`, `Collider`, its API and tunables; the panel now holds a `shutter` piece (the `housing` piece stays), a `gate` piece, or one `PropSpot` car per `car_length_m` fitted to the opening's share and the barrier's height. `LevelStairs` hides its steps' boxes (`draw_box` false, they stay the colliders) and adds one `Flight` mesh.
- **`section_entered`.** `LevelRoot` emits `CombatEvents.section_entered(section)` once at the end of `_ready` and then when the section owning the route point nearest the player changes; a section wins only by `section_switch_margin_m` (export, 1 m), so a seam does not flicker. `LevelRoot.current_section()`.
- **Tuning.** "Level" > "Kit": `kit_trim_m_per_tile` (4). "Level" > "Draw cost": `draw_prop_shadow_error_m`, `draw_prop_shadow_range_m`, `draw_prop_lod_merge_deg`, `draw_prop_lod_split_deg` (StaticArt's shadow twins). "Look" > "Trim": `look_trim_ink_cut` (0.5), `look_trim_lod_cap` (4). "Debug": `kit_draw_graybox`.
- **Acts.** "Perf: level walk" (`tests/bot/level_acts/perf_acts.gd`, run by `tests/bot/perf_level_bot.tscn`, printed as `PERF-BENCH` by the shared `tests/bot/perf_recorder.gd`, with the renderer's counters; `tools/perf_compare.sh --bot res://tests/bot/perf_level_bot.tscn`), "Perf: counters" (the arena tour's poses held still, AI frozen: counters that compare across loads) and "Perf: inventory" (diagnostic: the biggest meshes in view per pose) and "Look: kit strip" (`kit_acts.gd`).

### Ingest, manifest, voices and the audition room (AUDIO)

Every sound is an ElevenLabs take (plan D19) except the Milestone 2 placeholders still shipping and the music loop (D25). The game reads `assets/export/sfx/` through `SfxBank`'s glob exactly as before (`<pool>_<nn>.wav`, the pool being the name up to the last underscore) and Tuning's dB; the manifest is the ingest record, not a runtime file.

**Tools** (`tools/audio/`, ffmpeg and the standard library only, run as `python3 -I`):
- `eleven_sfx.py <jobs.json> --label <text> --ceiling <credits> [--dry-run] [--only <id>...]`, `--balance`, `--reconcile`. A jobs file is one group, `{"group", "jobs": [{"id", "text", "duration_seconds" (required, 0.5 to 30), "candidates", "prompt_influence", "loop"}]}` or a bare list; takes land in `assets/source/sfx/eleven/<group>/<id>_<nn>.mp3` (LFS, never overwritten; a take already on the ledger is skipped, so a run resumes). The model is pinned in `eleven_policy.json` (`eleven_text_to_sound_v3`, `mp3_44100_128`), with the hard `stop` (30000), `max_call` (1200) and the projection rate `credits_per_second` (**10**, measured; the docs say 40). The ledger `assets/source/sfx/eleven_ledger.jsonl` (append-only) has a `call` line per take (`id` = `<group>/<id>_<nn>`, which the manifest's `source` names as `elevenlabs:<id>`) and a `settle` line per run: the account's counter lags a call by many seconds, so the run reads it at the start, every 20 calls and after it settles, and `credits_remainder` makes the ledger's total the account's. A 429 is retried after a wait (nothing was charged); the key is read from `ELEVEN_LABS_API_KEY` or `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` (the main checkout's `.env` in a worktree) and never printed.
- `ingest.py`: `--init` (the manifest from the shipped wavs), `--analyze <group>` (length, onset, active length, peak, rms, crest, zero-crossing rate of each take), `--picks <file>` (ingest the chosen takes: the picks file lists each pool's take ids in variant order, with `max_s`, a loudness target and `slots`), no argument (rebuild every ElevenLabs row), `--check [--only <pool>] [--tolerance n]` (the gate: every wav has a row and the reverse, is 16-bit mono 44.1 kHz, a `gen_sfx` row is the byte-identical legacy copy, an ElevenLabs row's PCM is rebuilt from its MP3 and must match, and the MP3's sha256 is the ledger's), `--assets` (writes the "Voices (ElevenLabs)" rows of ASSETS.md and drops the placeholder rows of re-sourced files). The arithmetic is trim, linear fades (4 ms in; 8 ms out, 60 ms when `max_s` cut a sound still going), then one gain: `peak_db`, `rms_db` (voices, -16, capped at a -1 dBFS peak) or `lufs` (beds); a loop is not trimmed or faded and `loop_xfade_ms` crossfades its tail into its head. Every number found is written to the row, so a rebuild is exact.

**The manifest** `assets/source/sfx/manifest.json`: `slots` (`pool` -> `min_variants`, `long`, `loop`, `space` 3d or 2d) and `files` (`file`, `pool`, `source` = `elevenlabs:<ledger id>` or `gen_sfx`, `licence`, `trim_s`, `fade_ms`, `peak_db`/`rms_db`/`lufs`, `gain_db`, `loop_xfade_ms`, `notes`). A slot with fewer files than `min_variants` is an empty slot (the placeholder inventory's `empty_slots`); `long` and `loop` pools are exempt from the 1.5 s one-shot cap and loops from the click test. The 39 placeholders are copied to `tests/fixtures/sfx_legacy/` and `tools/gen_sfx.gd` writes there now.

**The pools:** `grunt_|effort_|ko_` + `punk|kicker|thug|bruiser|boss` (3, 2 and 1 variants at least; 4, 3 and 2 shipped), the shared `grunt` (4) and `effort` (3), `player_grunt` (3), `boss_phase` (2, long). Declared and empty until SFX: `barrier_<shutter|gate|car>_<close|open>`, `weapon_pickup|throw|break|land`, `weapon_pipe_hit` and `weapon_bike_chain_hit` (the pools of `MoveData.impact_sfx` values `pipe` and `bike_chain`), `sting_go`, `sting_clear`, `ambience_street`, `ambience_roof`.

**`AudioDirector`** (`play()` unchanged):
- `play_stream(pool, stream, at, volume_db, pitch)`: `play()` for a stream already picked. `play_2d(pool, volume_db) -> AudioStreamPlayer` and `play_stream_2d()`: non-positional one-shots on `STING_PLAYERS` (4) players on the SFX bus, not the pooled voices; `signal played_2d(pool, player)`.
- `voice_pool(base, actor) -> StringName`: `<base>_<archetype>` (`EnemyActor.archetype`, read duck-typed) when that pool has files, else `base`. Grunts, efforts and KO lines use it; the dummy's archetype is empty, so it keeps the shared pools. `voice_pitch_mult` stays a multiplier on top.
- Slots and their signals: `ko` (the enemy's KO line at its eyes, cutting its grunt; nothing when `ko_<archetype>` and `ko` are empty, and not for the player), `boss_phase_changed` (`boss_phase`, `boss_line_db`), `barrier_moving` (`barrier_<kind>_<close|open>` at the barrier, `Barrier.kind` read duck-typed), `weapon_picked_up`/`weapon_thrown`/`weapon_broke`/`weapon_landed` (`weapon_pickup|throw|break|land` at the weapon), `arena_cleared`/`level_cleared` (2D `sting_go`/`sting_clear`), and on a clean hit `hit.move.impact_sfx` adds `weapon_<impact_sfx>_hit` (`AudioDirector.weapon_hit_pool()`) to the tier's stack, at `weapon_hit_db` plus the tier's gain. An empty pool plays nothing and logs nothing.
- The bed: `set_ambience(pool)` crossfades over `ambience_fade_s` on two dedicated `AudioStreamPlayer`s (`BED_PLAYERS`), forced to loop, at `ambience_db`; `ambience` is the pool asked for, `bed_pool()` the one sounding, `ambience_changed(pool)`. `section_entered(section)` calls it with `section.ambience` (duck-typed), the default `ambience_street` for a section naming none, a room with no section, and at `_ready`.
- `static pool_db(pool)`: the tuned volume of any pool by name (hit and block layers via `LAYER_DB`, the voices, the world, stings, the bed).
- `bank_for(pool)` and `legacy_bank`: with Debug `audio_legacy_bank` on, a pool the placeholders had plays from `tests/fixtures/sfx_legacy/` (every sound in the game, so a real punch can be A/B'd).

**Tuning** ("Audio", new): Voices `ko_db`, `boss_line_db`; World `barrier_db`, `weapon_pickup_db`, `weapon_throw_db`, `weapon_break_db`, `weapon_land_db`, `weapon_hit_db`; Stings `sting_go_db`, `sting_clear_db`; Ambience `ambience_db`, `ambience_fade_s`. Debug: `audio_legacy_bank`.

**`AuditionRoom`** (`game/audio/audition_room.gd`, a child of the training room's presentation only): `]`/`[` page the banks (every pool of the bank and the placeholders, sorted), `1`-`9` and `0` play that variant 2 m ahead of the listener at `pool_db()` (a sting 2D, a bed becomes the level's bed), `-` toggles `audio_legacy_bank`, `=` plays the whole bank; a label at the bottom left shows the page. Ignored while `GameInput.menu_open`; its three `HELP_ROWS` are in `HelpOverlay.KEY_ROWS`.

**Tests:** `tests/test_audio_m6.gd` (manifest and files both ways, format, the ledger within the stop, every voice pool at its minimum, ASSETS rows and LFS for every source file, `ingest.py --check`, the voice fallback, each slot on its signal with streams injected, the bed, the legacy bank, the audition room); `tests/test_audio.gd` has the new subgroup list and exempts `long` and `loop` pools.

### The static-prop path and every rigid model (PROPS)

A rigid model (a weapon, a car, a dumpster, a tank) goes through its own small chain and is drawn by one node. It has no armature, no skin and no detail mask, and it is outside every character art test (`CharacterArt`, `SkinnedArt`).

**The pipeline: `python3 -I tools/pipeline/run.py --asset <id> --kind prop [--check]`.**
- It reads `assets/source/props/<id>/pipeline.json` (`"kind": "prop"`; `tools/pipeline/make_prop_config.py` writes every one) and runs three Blender steps:
  - `inspect` (`blender_prop_inspect.py`): imports `source.mesh` (a Tripo GLB) or builds `source.builder` (`prop_models.py`: `pipe`, `bike_grip`, `bike_link`, `fixture_crate`, every face already on a slot), flattens and welds it, turns it (`orient.yaw_deg`, `rotate_x_deg`), scales it (`target.size_m` on one Godot axis, or `target.dims_m` per axis, a stretch to a pinned box), puts the origin (`target.origin`: `base_center`, `center`, `keep`; `offset_m`) and writes the file the palette step reads.
  - `palette` (`blender_palette.py`, `palette.armature: false`): the characters' labelling by Lab distance, swatch pruning, majority filter and border-protected decimation to `palette.budget_tris`, with `palette.border_cell_m` thinning the protected colour borders to one vertex per cell (a rust patch's border at the dense mesh's millimetres would hold more than the budget). `palette.unfold` relaxes folds.
  - `export` (`blender_export_prop.py`): the Body named `export.mesh_name`, scripted `export.parts` added (`{name, builder, args, offset_m, yaw_deg}`), UV1 `palette` on swatch centres, the smooth hull normal in COLOR (`hull_normal`, the characters' encoding), shading by Smooth-by-Angle (`export.smooth_angle_deg`) or, for lumpy generated surfaces, the characters' crease-preserving blend (`export.smooth_normals`); GLB with no skin, no materials, no animation.
- It writes `assets/export/props/<id>/<id>.glb`, `assets/source/props/<id>/report/report.json` (the hash of the config, the step reports and the GLB's sha256; `--check` rebuilds in `assets/source/_cache/check_<id>` and compares) and the Godot `.import` (the GLB contract's params).
- Slots (`assets/source/palette/weapons.json` 28-31, `props.json` 60-63, no red): chain_steel, padlock_brass (matte: the Punk's lens stays the one glossy swatch, test_toon.gd), grip_tape, pipe_paint; car_orange, dumpster_green, tank_blue, rust_brown. Greys, near-black and cream come from the shared slots.

**`StaticArt`** (`game/actors/art/static_art.gd`, a `Node3D`).
- Exports: `scene` (the GLB), `palette` (the atlas), `triangle_budget` (3000), `hull`, and `repeat_mesh` / `repeat_range_m` / `repeat_pitch_m` / `repeat_twist_deg` (the bike chain draws its `Link` again along Y, turned 90 degrees on each, until WEAPONS' sim takes over).
- `_ready()` makes one `MeshInstance3D` per GLB mesh, named as the mesh is, with `ToonMaterials.art_prop(palette)` (kind `ART_PROP`: the atlas read per swatch, the hard highlight and the glint on glossy swatches only, no rim, no fill, no marks) and, with `hull` and `Tuning.prop_art_hull` on, `ToonMaterials.outline()` as `material_overlay`. The surfaces are rebuilt with the vertex normal in CUSTOM1 so that hull mode 4 pushes along the baked smooth normal (D14: never `Hull.BOX`). With the hull off, `edge_tag` is 1 and the screen edge pass outlines it.
- API: `check() -> PackedStringArray` (empty = ok: a scene and an atlas, no skin, UV1 and COLOR on every surface, at most `triangle_budget` triangles, unique mesh names), `bounds() -> AABB` and `triangles() -> int`, `mesh_names()`, `instances()`, `set_tint(Color)`, and the static `check_art`, `bounds_of`, `mesh_names_of`. It draws and nothing else: a prop scene is visual only, its collider is the block or barrier it dresses.
- **Shadow twins (fix-final-perf).** Per mesh, the build makes levels of detail with `ImporterMesh.generate_lods` (`draw_prop_lod_merge_deg`, `draw_prop_lod_split_deg`) and keeps the coarsest within `draw_prop_shadow_error_m` (0.12 m; 0 = none) as `shadow`. A mesh with one draws with `cast_shadow` off, and a `SHADOWS_ONLY` twin (`shadow_casters()`) on the coarser mesh, the same material and transform, casts for it, up to `draw_prop_shadow_range_m` (60 m; 0 = unlimited). `ArrayMesh.shadow_mesh` is not used: the renderer ignores it for a material with a vertex function.

**Prop scenes** (`game/level/props/<id>.tscn`, a `StaticArt` with the hull on): `car`, `dumpster`, `ac_unit`, `water_tank`. The frame is the base centre (origin on the floor, the box centred on x and z) and the long axis along X, which is how the barrier's cars and the crowd street's boxes lie (`car_size`, `dumpster_size`); the car's nose points +X. The sizes are the pinned boxes (4.2 x 1.5 x 1.9, 2.0 x 1.3 x 1.0, 1.0 x 0.7 x 0.7, 2.0 x 2.6 x 2.0); `test_static_art.gd` holds length and depth within 12% of the fallback box. DRESS places them through `PropSpot` and may scale them to the spot's `size`.

**Weapon art** (`game/weapons/art/<id>.tscn`, a `StaticArt`): the frame is centred on the weapon's origin with its long axis on local +Y, `Weapon.length` (0.85) long within 2 cm, the grip 0.12 m from the -Y end.
- `pipe` (scripted): one mesh, `Pipe`.
- `bike_chain`: three meshes, `Lock` (the Tripo padlock, brass, at the +Y end, its shackle toward the grip), `Grip` (the taped handle, at the -Y end) and `Link` (one link at the origin, in the XZ plane).
- `Weapon.art_scene: PackedScene` (`weapon.gd`): set, the art draws and the cylinder does not. The cylinder is kept, invisible and without its hull, because the floor look and the M1-M5 tests read it. While the weapon lies on the floor the art takes `Tuning.weapon_art_floor_tint` (Level > Prop art, 0.45) of `weapon_floor_color`; in a hand or in the air it keeps the atlas's colours. `pipe.tscn` sets `art_scene`, so the Thug's pipe and the training room's draw art. `bike_chain.tscn`, the `WeaponDef` and the sim are WEAPONS'.

**Tunables:** Level > Prop art `weapon_art_floor_tint`; Debug `prop_art_hull` (the D14 A/B).

**Tests:** `tests/test_static_art.gd` (the fixture crate proves the path with no Tripo; every scene's `check()`; the weapon frame; `Lock`, `Grip` and `Link`; footprints; the hull mode; the committed reports; the ledger). **Look:** `tests/bot/acts/prop_acts.gd`, "Look: props at 1 m and 5 m", "Look: props hull A/B", "Look: props weapons" (the crowd bot).

### The Bruiser and the boss (HEAVIES)

Both go through the CHAIN's art chain at their own heights and wear the art on their scenes (`bruiser.tscn`, `boss.tscn`: `art = game/actors/art/<id>_art.tres`). Authored at 1.8 m like every GLB, worn at 2.05 m and 2.3 m by the rig's uniform scale (D15); the bulk is girth, clamped by the chain's `fit.girth_clamp`.

- **The Bruiser** (`assets/source/characters/bruiser/`, concept A): three Gemini views on the mannequin (front, left, back; the right view is not needed, the Punk's Tripo run used the same three), Tripo multiview (H1, 30 credits, seed 6101) and rig (H1r, 25), a procedural fist. Palette `bruiser.json`, slots 44-46 (purple, steel with `blood: false`, black-violet) plus the shared cream and skin; 14999 triangles, 4 colours.
- **Ox Pellerin** (`boss/`, concept C): the same views with the hands bare (the gloves are modelled apart), Tripo multiview with `geometry_quality detailed` (H2, 50, seed 6201) and rig (H2r, 25), a generated boxing-glove fist (H3, 50, from two Gemini views; the Punk's T9 route: `parts.fists.source generated`, a collar). Palette `boss.json`, slots 50-53 (charcoal, orange with `blood: false`, tights, shorts), the shared tan skin and the near-black glove slot 54 (fix-final-art; slot 2 before); 29298 triangles against the roster's 30000 (SPEC section 10's Boss row). The HUD bar, the enemy scene and the catalog read "Ox Pellerin"; `EncounterSpawner.make_enemy` keeps a boss scene's own `display_name` (it used to write "Boss" over it).
- **Chain knobs the heavies needed** (all opt-in, none changes the Punk's, the hero's or the dummy's report hash):
  - `fit.sole_offset_m`: added to the sole height the ankle landmarks are put on; negative lifts the whole foot (the warp's blend of shin and foot weights left both heavies' heels 0.5-2.3 cm under the floor).
  - `fit.sole_flatten {height_m, bins}`: per foot, the underside is read in slices along the foot and every vertex lowered by its slice's rise above the lowest, fading to nothing `height_m` above the sole (the Bruiser's steel-capped boots had their toes sprung 3 cm).
  - `fit.sole_from "height"`, `fit.sole_height_m`: take the lowest vertex under that height as the sole whoever owns it (tried; not needed in the end).
  - `rig.clamps[].below_z_m`: the mirror of `above_z_m`, moving a bone's weight onto `to` below a height (a generated rig hangs a heavy boot's heel half on the shin, which left the soles with no foot-bound vertex for A1/A4).
  - `tools/pipeline/views_fit.py <views_dir>`: crops each generated view to its subject and scales it to the mannequin's height and feet row (stdlib and ffmpeg; the raw views are kept in `views/raw/`); no Gemini image is spent on a 3% height miss.
- **Test rows** (CHAIN's rows run over the roster at each height): `test_m4_done_when.gd` A4 builds its walk and idle clips at `rig.rig_scale()` and A5 compares the fist with the table's box and centroid tolerance scaled by it (both were 1.8 m only; the Punk, the dummy and the hero are unchanged at scale 1). Roster files `tests/fixtures/art/roster/{bruiser,boss}.json` and strain files `tests/fixtures/strain/{bruiser,boss}.json` carry their ratchets and reasons. `tests/test_art_heavies.gd`: the rows, the boss's name (scene, catalog, spawner), the palettes (ranges, red band, no gloss, `blood: false`, the charcoal darker than the slate, the orange clear of the dummy's), and the lens rule (no art vertex in view nearer than `FPView.NEAR` in the Bruiser's smash and grab/headbutt at 0.95 m, the boss's charge, pound and grab/slam at Tuning's `boss_read_m`).
- **Look acts**: the roster's "Look: Bruiser at 1 m" and "Look: Ox Pellerin at 1 m"; `tests/bot/acts/heavies_acts.gd` "Look: heavies beside a Punk" (the Punk, the Bruiser and the boss on an arc at 2.5, 5 and 10 m, level view).
- **Inventory**: `primitive_bodies` 4 -> 2 in the level, the crowd room and the test room (the Kicker and the Pipe Thug remain).
- **VFIX-HEAVIES (M6 playtest feedback, verification pass; config, mask and tests only, no chain or shader change)**: the Bruiser's palette regions are `helmet_back`, `helmet_top`, `helmet_front` (slot 47 from slot 44, z 1.64-1.71: the no-blood swatch reaches the visor frame), `under_visor` (slot 44, z 1.5-1.575) and `visor_fill` (slot 45 from skin and cream, z 1.575-1.66), none with `bisect_z` (a cut makes sliver edges that raise every clip's stretch ratchet by about 60%). Ox's `face_skin` zone starts at z 1.58 with the ink pair 0.012 / 0.04. `test_m6_feedback_detail.gd` now needs each `test_m6_feedback_detail_<id>.gd` to hold a `func test_` and the character's `pipeline.json` path on a code line (`_roll_out_file_problem`). `tests/fixtures/strain/bruiser.json` differs from its figures at `2afbe4d` in one row: `held.min` 0.1489 -> 0.1168. Calls: `docs/milestones/M6-calls/VFIX-HEAVIES.md`.
- **VF2-ART (M6 playtest feedback, vfinal fix pass; config, masks and tests only, no chain or shader change)**: the Kicker's `palette.regions` gain `brow` after `eyes` (slot 24 from the skin slot 6, x +-0.13, y 0.05-0.3, z 1.665-1.71), so no slot-6 face shows under the brim (brim, shade, mask). Ox's `detail_bake.zones` gain `jaw` after `face_skin` (slot 7, x +-0.13, z 1.45-1.58, ink pair 0.05 / 0.15, `crease_deg` 181): the skin under the face zone's floor draws no ink, so the mouth's corner hook is gone (`boss.glb` is unchanged, only `boss_detail.png`). `test_m6_feedback_detail.gd`'s `_roll_out_file_problem` now needs the character's `pipeline.json` path read inside a test function's body (written there, through a const the body names, or through a helper it calls), with trailing comments stripped. Calls: `docs/milestones/M6-calls/VF2-ART.md`.

### The Kicker and the Pipe Thug (CREW)

The two enemies through the CHAIN pipeline at 1.8 m, procedural fists, the game's own animation set (M6 D3). Nothing in `game/` changed but the two scenes' `art` and two art resources; everything else is data, as CHAIN's checklist says.

- **Files per character** (`kicker`, `thug`): `assets/source/characters/<id>/{views/, tripo/, pipeline.json, report/report.json}`, `assets/export/characters/<id>/<id>{,_rest}.glb` and `<id>_detail.png`, `game/actors/art/<id>_art.tres` (15000, detail mask, `detail_sharp`), `art = ` in `kicker.tscn` and `pipe_thug.tscn`, `tests/fixtures/art/roster/<id>.json`, `tests/fixtures/strain/<id>.json`, `assets/source/palette/<id>.json`. `run.py --asset <id> --check` says YES for both.
- **Views**: three Gemini views per character (front, back, left; the right view is only a `views_check.py` row and Tripo does not take it), drawn on the mannequin. Gemini draws the figure 5-10% taller than the mannequin with the soles low, which fails `views_check.py`; `tools/pipeline/views_normalize.py <views_dir>` (Pillow, run without `-I`) scales each figure uniformly onto the mannequin's height and feet row, centres it, keeps the original as `view_<x>_raw.jpg` and so adds no generation. Both sets pass `views_check.py`.
- **Tripo**: multiview front+left+back with M4's `gen_options.json` (v3.1, texture v3.5, `orientation align_image`, seed 4101), then rig-check (free) and rig v3.0 unity, as the Punk. The Kicker's accepted model is the seed-4102 retry (the first one's right-hand cut was 17 cm up the forearm); the first is kept, unused, with its ledger rows.
- **Palette**: `kicker.json` (slot 22 lime `#86c734`, 23 black `#3b3a43`, `blood: false`), `thug.json` (38 olive `#7b7a3e`, 39 yellow `#e0d83c` and 40 black `#3b3a43`, both `blood: false`); skin (6 for the Kicker, 7 for the Thug) and the cream wraps (14) are shared slots. Four colours each, none in the red band. The Thug's two shin bands are `palette.regions` with `bisect_z` (boxes at z 0.575-0.598 and 0.628-0.651), otherwise the decimation turned them into blotches.
- **Fists**: the chain's procedural block fist (`glove_slot` 14, strap 14 for the Kicker's wrapped hands; skin 7 with a yellow strap 39 for the Thug's bare hands and wrist wraps). A5's box holds at the plan's 0.2. The Thug's hands hold PROPS's pipe at its grip offset; the fist box is 0.1 x 0.118 x 0.116 m, wider than the pipe's 0.064 m, so the pipe passes through the fist as the cylinder did.
- **Detail mask** (`palette.detail_bake`): the Punk's recipe with zones for the face skin, the headwear and the boots; the Thug's bandana and buckle are `no_ink` (the generated pattern is noise, not a line) and its wrist wraps ink at the headwear's thresholds.
- **Two opt-in additions to `tools/pipeline/blender_fit.py`** (no report key, the probe and the Punk unchanged): `fit.sole_lift_m` raises the finished mesh (not the armature) by that many metres, and `fit.bulge[].direction: "front"` mirrors `"back"` (only the front half of the vertices a bone owns moves forward). The Kicker's chunky boots use both (`sole_lift_m` 0.0065, a 6 cm front bulge on each foot) so A1's toe rule (z < -0.2 at the sole) and A4's sole rule (5 mm) hold; the Thug needs neither.
- **Ratchets** (in each roster file, with the reasons): the Kicker's jump kick at 1 m frames 7.6% of cells and 7.1 points of lens share against the proxies (a9_cells 0.08, lens 0.07/0.075 for the two poses, `a9_strike_art_share_min` 0.1 as the twin), torso flash anchor 5.9 cm, stretch 3.3 cm; the Thug's pipe swings frame 5.2% of cells (a9_cells 0.057), flash anchors 2.8/4.8/3.1 cm, stretch 7.7 cm (the right upper arm in the third swing's raise). Everything else is at the plan's bar. The Punk's own ratchets are larger on every row.
- **Evidence**: `captures/crew/` (never committed): "Look: Kicker at 1 m", "Look: Thug at 1 m", "Look: lineup" (the Punk, Kicker, Thug and Bruiser on an arc), "Kicker jump kick in front", "Thug three swings".

### The level, the crowd street and the training room (DRESS)

The level and both code-built rooms draw in the kit and the colliders stay M5's (D1: `tests/test_m6_colliders.gd` is unchanged). `tests/test_m6_dress.gd` holds the rest.

- **Section light (D12).** `LevelSection` exports (group "Light") `light_enabled`, `sun_elevation_deg`, `sun_yaw_deg`, `sun_color`, `sun_energy`, `fog_color`, `fog_density`, `sky_top_color`, `sky_horizon_color`; `light()` returns them as a Dictionary (`sun_elevation`, `sun_yaw`, `sun_color`, `sun_energy`, `fog_color`, `fog_density`, `sky_top`, `sky_horizon`). `LevelRoot` aims a blend at the light of the section `section_entered` names (`_retarget_light`, from `_track_section`) and steps it in `_physics_process` over Tuning's `section_light_blend_s` (Level > Section light, 2.5 s; 0 snaps; the first section snaps), easing with a smoothstep and turning the sun's yaw the short way (`LevelRoot.blend_light(a, b, t)`, static). It writes the `Sun`'s rotation, colour and energy, the `WorldEnvironment`'s fog colour and density and the `ProceduralSkyMaterial`'s top and horizon. A section without `light_enabled`, and every section with Debug `section_light_enabled` off, takes `default_light()`: Tuning's sun (Look > Toon), white, and the rooms' fog and sky (`RoomKit.DEFAULT_*`). Tuning's sun moving live is not a section change: a default-to-default retarget snaps. API: `section_light_applies()`, `current_light()`, `section_light_progress()` (0 to 1), `light_for(section)`, `default_light()`.
- **LookPost's sun rule.** `LookPost._apply_sun()` leaves the `Sun`'s direction, colour and energy to the level while `section_light_applies()` (the room has it and the current section has a light); Tuning's shadow blur applies everywhere. The crowd street and the training room have no such method, so they keep Tuning's sun, as in M5.
- **The three sections' light.** Street: a dusk sun at 38 degrees, yaw -50, a warm (1, 0.88, 0.72), a violet haze (0.52, 0.5, 0.6) at 0.011. Roofs: 27 degrees, yaw -68, copper (1, 0.8, 0.62), fog (0.5, 0.42, 0.46), a copper horizon. Boss roof: 24 degrees, yaw -100, (0.98, 0.74, 0.6), a dark blue-violet fog (0.31, 0.29, 0.4) at 0.015 and a dark sky, so the far street below the gap fades to a canyon floor and not to a pale plane (`final-sees-03`). `ambience` is `&"ambience_street"` in the street and `&"ambience_roof"` in the roofs and the boss roof.
- **Dressing.** Each section has one `Dressing` (`DressingStreet`, `Dressing`) beside its `Geometry`, holding `KitPiece`s and `PropSpot`s; nothing under it collides.
  - The street adds to KIT's A1 set: windows over A2 and A3 and over every opening, cornices, door frames at the other doors, the alleys and the lots, the warehouse's roller shutter and housing (the 11 ribs' boxes hide), a chain-link side fence (the block hides), the van as a `car` spot, the yard barricade as a `dumpster` spot and three wall-hung `ac_unit` spots.
  - The roofs and the boss roof add door frames at every door (nine on the roofs, three on the boss roof), the penthouse's shutter, `water_tank` spots on StackA and StackB, `deck` (roof tar) on the roof floors, the six boss-roof cars as `car` spots, and `module = &"skyline"` on every skyline block (a facade with windows; the boss roof's far `Ground` is `slab_dark`).
  - A spot sits on a block's top or on a wall face, never on a walkable cell with nothing below. A doorway's frame stands round its opening and the interior stays `slab_dark`/`dark`: the test checks the dark strip is at least 0.08 darker than brick, plaster, concrete and the facade, and that no spot or piece other than a frame, a shutter or its housing overlaps a spawn point.
- **The rooms.** `CrowdRoom` and `TestRoom` pass an art kind to every `RoomKit.add_box` and `add_visual_box`: `floor` (grid floors), `wall` (fronts, end walls), `slab` (lintels, lane dashes, the alternating front of the training room), `slab_dark` (doorway backs, alley walls, the backdrop ground), `window`, `curb`, `crate` (with the prop hull on the overlay only while a module draws, since a box's own material carries it), `skyline` (the backdrop). The crowd street's car and dumpster are `PropSpot`s in a `Dressing` (`CrowdRoom.dressing`) over colliders with no mesh (with `kit_draw_graybox` the colliders carry the M5 boxes again).
- **Warm-up.** `ToonMaterials.warm_up` draws one `StaticArt` (`WARM_PROP`, the AC unit) beside the trim module so its pipeline is built at load (windowed only).
- **Trim sheet.** `trim_layout.json`'s hsv retuned to the moods (warmer brick and crate planks, blue-grey asphalt, concrete, roof tar and metal, a darker facade), still inside the band.
- **Tuning.** Level > Section light `section_light_blend_s`; Debug `section_light_enabled` (A/B: off is M5's one sun and fog).
- **Acts.** "Look: arena tour" (`tests/bot/level_acts/tour_acts.gd`, level bot, 20 poses through the three sections) and "Look: rooms tour" (`tests/bot/acts/rooms_tour_acts.gd`, crowd bot: six poses in the crowd street, four in a training room built beside it).

### The catalog, the second weapon and the glint (WEAPONS)

**`WeaponDef`** (`game/weapons/weapon_def.gd`, a `Resource`; files `game/weapons/defs/<id>.tres`) and **`WeaponCatalog`** (`weapon_catalog.gd`, static: `get_def(id)`, `all()`, `is_swing(move_id)`, `is_throw(move_id)`, `clear()`; ids `pipe`, `bike_chain`).
- Fields: `id`, `display_name`, `scene: PackedScene`, `move_set: MoveSet`, `throw_move: MoveData`, `swing_ids: Array[StringName]` (chain order), `impact_sfx: StringName`; `owns_swing(id)`.
- **`Weapon.def_id`** (an export, default `pipe`) names the def and `Weapon.def` resolves it (the pipe's def if the id is unknown). The def points at the scene and the scene at the id, so the two resources never refer to each other. The scenes are `game/weapons/pipe.tscn` (path kept) and `game/weapons/bike_chain.tscn`; both keep the capsule collider, the grip 0.12 m from the -Y end and the hidden proxy cylinder (the amber path and the M1-M5 tests read it).
- **`player.gd` reads the held weapon's def**: `_carry_set()` is the def's id (registered in `_sets` at pick-up) or `ground`; the swing refusal, the durability cost per body hit and the LB throw use `def.swing_ids` and `def.throw_move` (`WeaponCatalog.is_swing/is_throw` where no weapon is held any more). `MOVE_SETS` holds the ground, air and hold sets only; `WEAPON_THROW_PATH` stays as the pipe's throw.

**The bike chain's moves** (`gen_moves.gd`, the spec's numbers, D5): `chain_swing_1/2/3` (LIGHT/FLINCH 6/3/12, HEAVY/STAGGER 6/3/12, FINISHER/KNOCKDOWN 10/4/22, 10 damage, `guard_damage_mult` 1.5, `WeaponTip` spheres r0.2, r0.2 and r0.22 moved +0.06, +0.06 and -0.10 m along the weapon: a short wide lash twice, the padlock out past the tip last) and `chain_throw` (6/1/14, 15, HEAVY/KNOCKDOWN); the set `bike_chain` (`movesets/bike_chain.tres`: P, stomp, front kick, back elbow like the pipe's). The poses are the pipe's three recipes and the throw (`PlaceholderPoses` dispatches both ids to them; `MOVE_IDS`, `_WEAPON_MOVES` and `_FREE_ARM` list the chain's). Every weapon move names its `impact_sfx` (`pipe` on the pipe's swings, throw and the Thug's three swings; `bike_chain` on the chain's), which the director turns into `weapon_pipe_hit` / `weapon_bike_chain_hit`.

**`ChainSim`** (`chain_sim.gd`, a `Node3D` child of a weapon whose art repeats a mesh): presentation only.
- `weapon_chain_points` Verlet points, the first pinned at the end of the `Grip` mesh, each pulled toward the straight line along the weapon's +Y at `weapon_chain_follow_rate` (1/s), with gravity, damping, `weapon_chain_iterations` length passes. It steps in world space by `delta` times the game-time scale (`TimeController.scale_for(holder)`, so the holder's hitstop freezes it; `sim_scale()` when nobody holds it) from the weapon's own transform; a grip that moves more than `weapon_chain_snap_m` in a step (a pick-up, a reparent) snaps it straight.
- It draws the art's own `Link` as one `MultiMesh` (the art's material and hull, a link every `weapon_chain_link_pitch_m` along the points, each turned 90 degrees on the one before; the GLB's link is flat in XZ with its long side on Z, so Z is turned onto the chain) and moves the `Lock` mesh to the last point (turned along the last segment). StaticArt's straight repeats are hidden while it runs (`Debug` `weapon_chain_sim` off brings them back).
- It never writes a hit, a body or a transform of the weapon: the hit is the rig's `WeaponTip`, as for the pipe. API: `step(dt)`, `points_local()`, `links_drawn()`, `rest_length()`, `is_ready_to_draw()`.

**The glint (D28)** (`weapon_glint.gd`, `glint.gdshader`): a `WeaponGlint` `Node3D` named `Glint` on every weapon, top-level, `weapon_glint_height_m` above the weapon's position, one camera-facing quad with an additive four-point sparkle; its width is `max(weapon_glint_size_m, distance x tan(weapon_glint_min_angle_deg))` computed in the vertex shader, so it never covers less than that angle (3 degrees, 0.52 m at 10 m, with the defaults). Visible only while `weapon.is_available()` and `weapon_floor_amber` is off. `Debug` `weapon_floor_amber` brings M5's look back (the floor colour, the thicker radius, the art tint of `weapon_art_floor_tint`) and no glint. Statics for tests: `WeaponGlint.wanted(weapon)`, `drawn_size_m(d)`, `angular_size_deg(d)`, `position_above(weapon)`.

**`CombatEvents.weapon_landed(weapon)`** fires once at a weapon's first contact after a throw or a drop (not for a pickup spot's placement, and not for a throw that breaks the weapon: `weapon_broke` plays then). `weapon_picked_up`, `weapon_thrown` and `weapon_broke` keep carrying the weapon node.

**Tunables** (Level > Weapon look): `weapon_glint_height_m`, `weapon_glint_size_m`, `weapon_glint_min_angle_deg`, `weapon_glint_pulse_hz`, `weapon_glint_pulse_depth`, `weapon_glint_spin_dps`, `weapon_glint_intensity`, `weapon_glint_color`, `weapon_chain_points`, `weapon_chain_iterations`, `weapon_chain_follow_rate`, `weapon_chain_gravity`, `weapon_chain_damping`, `weapon_chain_snap_m`, `weapon_chain_link_pitch_m`. Debug: `weapon_floor_amber`, `weapon_chain_sim`.

**Tests:** `tests/test_weapons_m6.gd` (the catalog, the spec's numbers per def, pick-up, the three-hit chain at 10, the break after 8 hits, the throw of 15, the guard drain, durability, `weapon_landed` once per landing, the glint from 10 m at eye height, the sim). **Look:** `tests/bot/acts/weapon_acts.gd`, "Look: weapons" (the crowd bot).

### The near upper arm (ARM)

M6 D7. The hero's GLB has a fourth mesh, `Body__fp_arm`: the shoulder half of each upper arm (the faces the upper arm bone owns on the shoulder side of `parts.split_fp.upper_arm_cut`), separated from `Body__fp_hidden` in `player_parts.py` (`split_fp`, `parts.split_fp.arm`).
- **Pipeline.** The shoulder-side loop gets an inward cap and so does the elbow-side one (`caps.Body__fp_arm`, `wall_normals`, so the shading normal on its ring is the wall's and the hull-normal test holds); an add_caps pass that leaves a loop open would cut the faces round it. The piece is swollen radially by `arm.push_m` (0 at the cut's ring, ramping over `elbow_ramp_m`; 0 at its shoulder loop, ramping over `shoulder_ramp_m`), and Body's upper-arm dome is sunk `inset_m` below the wall it is tangent to: together they keep the dome from showing through the wall in ragged patches. `pipe_blender.body_objects()` lists `BODY_ARM`, so detail, normals and export see it. `player_run.py --asset player --check` says YES (report hash in `report.json`).
- **Runtime.** `SkinnedArt.FP_ARM_SUFFIX` (`__fp_arm`) is first-person-hidden like `__fp_hidden` (same layer, same `fp_hidden` meta). `MoveData.fp_show_upper_arm` (Camera group) is set by `gen_moves.gd` on the haymaker and the uppercut only. `FirstPersonCamera._update_arm()` (after `_update_torso()` in `update_camera()`): while it is the current camera, `look_near_arm` (Tuning, Debug, default on) is on and the player's `current_move` runs (ATTACK or WINDUP) with the flag, the `*__fp_arm` meshes go to the default layer; any other frame puts the layers back (`_restore_arm()`; also on `set_player()`). Cameras with the default mask (side view, free fly) draw the mesh always, because it only ever sits on the hidden layer or the default one. Primitives (no art) are unchanged.
- **Budget.** 29,732 of 30,000 triangles (Body 19,868, hidden 4,820, torso 3,280, arm 1,764).
- **Ratchet.** The strain run reports each mesh's own percentiles, so the arm's triangles moved the hero's `PLAYER_ROW` in `tests/art_strain.gd` (reasons there); `tests/fixtures/strain/strain_ratchet.json` regenerated for the player.

### The generated sound effects (SFX)

Every pool that is not a voice is an ElevenLabs take now (plan D19), ingested through AUDIO's tools; the game, the director and the tests are unchanged, and the music loop (D25) is the one placeholder left. `ingest.py --check` rebuilds all 114 rows from their MP3s with no empty slot.

**Source** (`assets/source/sfx/eleven/`, MP3s in LFS): one jobs file per group, `sfx_<group>.jobs.json`, run through `eleven_sfx.py` (label `SFX`), takes in `sfx_<group>/<job id>_<nn>.mp3`:
- `sfx_hits`: `whoosh`, `thud`, `crack`, `crunch`, `body_fall`, `sub_thump` (each pool one layer of the tier stack; 4 candidates a prompt);
- `sfx_blocks`: `block_clack`, `parry_ting`, `guard_break`;
- `sfx_barriers`: `barrier_<shutter|gate|car>_<close|open>`;
- `sfx_weapons`: `weapon_pickup|throw|break|land` and `weapon_pipe_hit`, `weapon_bike_chain_hit`;
- `sfx_stings`: `sting_go`, `sting_clear`;
- `sfx_beds`: `ambience_street`, `ambience_roof` (25 s, `loop: true`).
A job id is `<pool>_v<n>`, one variant of its pool; each has 3 or 4 candidate takes and the runners-up stay on disk.

**The choice** is `sfx.picks.json` (beside the jobs files), written by `python3 -I tools/audio/sfx_pick.py --picks <groups...>` and ingested by `ingest.py --picks`. `sfx_pick.py --table <group>` prints every take with its measured features (active length, time of the loudest 5 ms window, spectral centroid, tail share, flat-top samples, rms; a bed's spread, events and seam) and its penalty against its pool's role (`ROLES`); `--legacy` prints the placeholders' features and `--levels` the loudness of the shipped pools. To swap a variant, edit its take in the picks file and run `ingest.py --picks assets/source/sfx/eleven/sfx.picks.json` (exact; `--check` verifies). `sfx_assets.py` writes the ASSETS section "Sound effects (ElevenLabs)".

**What ships:** whoosh 3, thud 4, crack 3, crunch 3, body_fall 3, sub_thump 3, block_clack 3, parry_ting 3, guard_break 3, each barrier pool 2, weapon_pickup, throw, break, land and pipe_hit 2, weapon_bike_chain_hit 2, each sting and bed 1. Stings are declared `long` in the manifest (`sting_clear` is 1.6 s); beds are `lufs` -20 with a 1000 ms loop crossfade.

**Mix** (Tuning > Audio, values only): the hit stack is peak-normalised at -1 dBFS and the voices RMS -16 dBFS, so the dB values keep a voice 4 to 7 dB under a light hit, measured over the first 0.4 s (light -26.0, heavy -21.3, finisher -16.3 dBFS; the voices -29 to -34). The values and the reasoning are in `docs/milestones/M6-calls/SFX.md`, call 9.

**Tests:** none added. `test_audio.gd` (the tier ranking by the real samples, whooshes loudest within 50 ms, ends at silence, one-shot cap) and `test_audio_m6.gd` (manifest and files both ways, the ledger, ASSETS rows, `ingest.py --check`) cover every pool; `test_m6_no_placeholders.gd`'s ratchet `global` is 0 and 0.

### Playtest feedback: FB-FEEL

Game-boy's playtest of the finished M6: no sting on GO, and the player feeling the hits he takes. Calls in `docs/milestones/M6-calls/FB-FEEL.md`.

- **GO is silent.** `AudioDirector._on_arena_cleared()` plays `sting_go` only with Debug's `audio_sting_go` (default false; on gives M6 back). `level_cleared` still plays `sting_clear`. The pool, its files, the audition room's entry and the placeholder ratchet (it counts files) are unchanged.
- **The victim's stack** (`FirstPersonCamera._on_hit_landed`): a clean hit with the player as victim, with Debug's `hurt_feel_enabled` on, adds `Tuning.data.hurt_shake(tier)` trauma (the tier's `shake_*` x `hurt_shake_mult`, never under `hurt_shake_min`), a punch of `hurt_punch_deg(tier)` (`cam_punch_*_deg` x `hurt_punch_mult`, the way `_away()` pushes: a blow from ahead tips the head back) and a head roll of `hurt_roll_deg(tier)`. The hits he lands and every blocked hit keep SPEC §6's table and `blocked_feel_scale`.
- **Roll:** `FirstPersonCamera.add_roll(deg)` and `get_roll_deg()` (positive tips the head to its left, away from a blow on the right); rolls use the punch's envelope and duration, sum to no more than the strongest, and `is_settled()` waits for them. The side comes from `FirstPersonCamera.hit_bearing(view, hit) -> float` (radians off the view's forward: 0 ahead, +PI/2 right, PI behind; FIX-FEEL: the attacker's global position against the view, read only, because the contact point is on the player's own hurtbox a few tenths of a metre from the lens and says nothing of the side; `-hit.direction` stands in with no attacker in the tree or one within `hurt_bearing_min_m` of the lens horizontally). A blow from ahead or behind (|sin| under `hurt_roll_frontal_sine`) rolls `hurt_roll_frontal_share` of full, to alternating sides.
- **`HurtOverlay`** (`game/ui/hurt_overlay.gd`, the HUD's first child, so under every panel; `GameHud.advance()` steps it with game time). `on_player_hit(hit, view)`, `edge_strength(HurtOverlay.Edge)`, `stroke_strength(edge)`, `depth_share()`, `is_live()`; static `strength_for(damage)` and `edge_weights(bearing)`. It subscribes to `hit_landed` and reads the viewport camera's transform. Per edge (left, right, top, bottom): `hurt_overlay_even_share` on every edge, the rest on the side the blow came from (left, right, bottom for behind); impact strokes (spikes pointing in, `hurt_overlay_strokes`) on that edge, the top edge for a blow from dead ahead. Strength and depth grow with `hit.damage` up to `hurt_overlay_full_damage`; fade over `hurt_overlay_fade_s`. Colour: the ink (`hurt_overlay_color`) with paper strokes (`hurt_overlay_stroke_color`), not red (SPEC §10); both are Color tunables. A blocked hit is `hurt_overlay_blocked_scale` as strong, with no strokes.
- **Panel jolt:** `PlayerHealthPanel.on_health_changed` jolts the panel when health falls (`hurt_panel_jolt_px_per_hp`, capped at `hurt_panel_jolt_max_px`, over `hurt_panel_jolt_s`); `jolt_offset()` is applied with `draw_set_transform`.
- **Tuning:** Hit feel > Hurt (all the `hurt_*` values above); Debug `hurt_feel_enabled` (true) and `audio_sting_go` (false), neither held by presets. `hurt_feel_enabled` off gives M6's view back: no victim multipliers, no roll, no vignette, no jolt.
- **Act and tests:** "Look: player hurt" (`tests/bot/acts/look_hurt_acts.gd`: a Punk, a Kicker and a Bruiser each throw one attack from the front, the left and behind; record with `--tune=hurt_feel_enabled=0` for the before); `tests/test_m6_feedback_feel.gd`.

- **Hurt shape tunables (FIX-FEEL):** the overlay's gradient (`hurt_overlay_mid_stop`, `_mid_alpha`), stroke geometry (`_stroke_len_min`, `_len_range`, `_len_floor`, `_width`, `_lean`, `_jitter`, `_gain`), the top-stroke threshold (`_top_threshold`) and the blocked depth factor (`_blocked_depth_gain`), the panel's rattle rates (`hurt_panel_jolt_rate_x/y`), and the camera's `hurt_bearing_min_m` and `hurt_roll_frontal_sine` are all in Tuning > Hit feel > Hurt; defaults are the old literals.

- **VFIX-FEEL (the verification round's fix).** `FirstPersonCamera.hit_bearing(view, hit)` is measured on the floor plane from the view's yaw only (`Basis(UP, view.basis.get_euler(YXZ).y)`; the attacker's floor-level origin is 1.6 m under the lens, so a pitched view put a Punk behind a player looking down at a stomp ahead of him). `HurtOverlay._on_hit_landed` passes `FirstPersonCamera.view_transform()` (the view without the last hit's kick and roll) when the viewport's camera is a `FirstPersonCamera`, and `global_transform` otherwise; the roll and the overlay still call the one function. A rear hit reads as plainly as a frontal one on the pale ground: every stroke has an ink rim (`hurt_overlay_outline_width`, a share of the screen's height, and `hurt_overlay_outline_color`, drawn with the stroke's fade), and a clean hit's bottom edge is inked `hurt_overlay_bottom_ink_gain` heavier (`edge_strength(BOTTOM)` may then exceed 1; the draw clamps the alpha) with its strokes `hurt_overlay_bottom_stroke_gain` longer, so they rise above the health panel. Blocked hits take neither gain. Four new Hit feel > Hurt rows (51 -> 55). Act: "Look: player hurt looking down" (a Punk's jab from behind, the left and the front at 45 degrees down). Calls in `docs/milestones/M6-calls/VFIX-FEEL.md`.
- **VF2-FEEL (the vfinal round's fix).** The bottom edge's extra ink is drawn, never stored: `_edges` stay at 1 or below, `_bottom_extra` (the struck share of the bottom edge x `hurt_overlay_bottom_ink_gain` - 1, fading with the edges) is added in `drawn_strength(edge)`, which `_draw` uses (the alpha clamp stands). So frontal and side hits ink the bottom like any unstruck edge, and a blow from behind more the nearer it is to dead behind. `_alive_share()` is at most 1 and `_depth` at most `hurt_overlay_depth_max`: before, each heavy rear hit multiplied the depth by up to 1.5 and nothing lowered it. `hurt_overlay_bottom_stroke_gain` is replaced by `hurt_overlay_bottom_stroke_floor` (0.2 of the screen's height: no bottom tooth is shorter, so a light jab clears the health panel; a heavy blow's teeth are as long as on any edge). `stroke_lengths_px(edge)` and `drawn_strength(edge)` are public for tests; the tooth shapes come from one function, `_stroke_shapes()`. Act: "Look: player hurt rear run". Calls in `docs/milestones/M6-calls/VF2-FEEL.md`.

### Playtest feedback: FB-GROUND

Game-boy's playtest of M6: the street's floor flashed and read as noise. **The cause was geometry, not the texture**: the boss roof's `Ground` (a 200 m `slab_dark` block, drawn only) had its top at world y 0, the street `Floor`'s plane, and covered the whole street, so two faces fought for every pixel. It is now 0.5 m lower (`boss_roof.tscn`, `Ground` y -13), and `roofs.tscn`'s `PenthouseFrontFloorShutter` (an asphalt slab with its top on the top roof's deck) stops drawing (`draw_box = false`; its collider is unchanged). `tests/test_m6_feedback_ground.gd` instantiates the level and fails when two ground-like blocks (floor, deck, `slab_dark`, a slab at most 1 m thick) share a top plane over any area.

- **The ground tile.** `assets/export/kit/ground_sheet.png` (2048 px, 8 m a side; `trim_layout.json`'s `ground`, painted by `tools/gen_trim.gd`) holds flat tones and bold tapered ink: branching cracks, patched squares, a manhole, two drain grates. No grain, and no ink under 5 px wide (`gen_trim.gd`'s `MIN_INK_PX`). The strip table carries `ground` (a bool on the asphalt strip, flag 4 in the shader's `strips` row) and a `ground` entry (`px`, `tile_m`); `TrimSheet.is_ground()`, `ground_tile_m()`, `ground_texture()`.
- **The shader** (`trim_body.gdshaderinc`, both trim shaders). A face on a ground strip samples `ground_tex` at `(world x, -world z) / ground_m` (a `world_m` varying from `MODEL_MATRIX`), so two floor blocks butt without a seam, with the surface's own gradients: every mip level in reach, no `lod_cap`, since the tile is one picture and not a stack of wrapped strips. `ink_mask()` cuts the ink as before; on the ground the cut's edge widens with the mip level (`ink_soft`: from level 2, 0.12 a level, at most 0.45), so a crack thinner than a pixel dims smoothly and never pops. On the strips the ink fades out between `ink_fade` texels a pixel (16 to 64), where the capped mip would alias it. `debug_view` (0 picture, 1 strip, 2 wanted mip, 4 sampled alpha) is the sampling's debug view; it is not a Tuning value.
- **The strips.** `concrete`, `kerb`, `roof_tar` and the sheet's own `asphalt` are flat tones with bold ink (joints 10 to 12 px, cracks at least 5 px), no per-texel grain or marks.
- **Tunables.** Level > Ground: `ground_m_per_tile`, `ground_ink_soft_from_lod`, `ground_ink_soft_per_lod`, `ground_ink_soft_max`, `ground_ink_fade_start_texels`, `ground_ink_fade_end_texels`. Debug: `ground_sheet_off` (the asphalt strip's own paint, per face, mip capped). `ToonMaterials.apply_look` feeds the trim material; `Tuning.changed` for any `ground_` value re-applies it.
- **The look act.** `tests/bot/level_acts/ground_acts.gd`, "Look: ground drift" (`-- --ground=<variant>`: control, no_shadow, hard_shadow, no_edges, no_ink, lod_free, sheet_off, flat, dbg_strip, dbg_lod, dbg_alpha, diag, meshes), measured by `tools/ground_flicker.py`.


**FIX-WORLD (the review's fix wave).**

- **No two drawn faces in one plane, anywhere.** The boss roof's `Ground` is now at top -0.25 (y -12.75: above the roofs' skyline walk strips at -0.5, which it hides as in M6, and 0.25 m under the street's floor), clear of every drawn plane under it. `tests/m6_coplanar_scan.gd` is the guard: it takes every drawn box of a scene (a `LevelBlock`'s mesh, or the mesh of a world body a room builds), compares each pair on the five faces that can be seen (never the underside), drops what a third drawn box covers just outside the plane and what has no world collider below a point 0.4 m off the face, and reports the rest over 0.02 m2. `tests/test_m6_feedback_ground.gd` runs it over Mission 1 (no pair at all), the crowd street and the training room (over 0.5 m2: their boxes are built in `crowd_room.gd` and `test_room.gd`, see the calls file's requests).
- **`InsetBlock`** (`game/level/kit/inset_block.gd`, a `LevelBlock`; reworked in VFIX-WORLD, see below): the collider stays the box.
- **The ground tile** now has broad worn pools, a paler base, a few ink-outlined dark patches, two squares and five cracks (see the calls file), and is **mirrored on every other tile down the street** in the shader (`gtm`, a triangle wave of the tile's z with the gradient's sign turned the same way), so the manhole and grates repeat every 16 m, not 8, with no seam (a mirror is continuous at its fold).
- **The strips' ink fade** measures the footprint by its mean side (`sqrt(|det|)` of the two gradients, `fade_texels`), not its longer side, so a roof deck's or a kerb top's seams keep their ink as far as a wall's. `ink_mask(a, texels, fade_texels, ground)`.
- **Look act**: `ground_acts.gd`'s "Look: ground drift" also holds `HELD` views (the boss roof's gap, the roofs' deck, and the deck looked along) for `still` and `creep`; `-- --ground=held` runs only those.

**VFIX-WORLD (the verification round's fix pass).** The first answer to the z-fight, pulling a whole face back 2 to 4 cm, hid the fight and drew a step wherever the partner covered only part of that face (the catwalk's long wall, the terrace's door pillars, the penthouse's side, a pilaster, a building corner): the edge pass inks a step, full height or in dashes that crawl at distance. The contract now:

- **`InsetBlock` takes the overlap out of its own face; it never pulls a face back.** `yields_to: Array[NodePath]` (paths from the block) names the blocks, or kit pieces, that win a plane against it. Over the rectangle it shares with each (on every face that lies in one plane with the partner's face looking the same way, within `Tuning.kit_coplanar_m`) it does not draw: `MeshClip.clip_box` cuts the rectangle out of the kit mesh's triangles on that plane, and out to `Tuning.kit_clip_standoff_m` from it (a wall's plinth board stands 5 cm off its face), re-triangulating the triangles it cuts with their UV (metres), normal and strip colour interpolated, so the picture continues unbroken. There is then one face in that plane, the partner's: no fight, no step. The collider is the box, so `tests/test_m6_colliders.gd` is untouched. `clipped_rects(axis, side) -> Array[Rect2]` reports what a block leaves out (world space, the plane's `((axis + 1) % 3, (axis + 2) % 3)` coordinates). A kit piece is not a box (a cornice is three boards, each standing out further than the one below), so against a piece the block gives up only the rectangles the piece's own faces cover on that plane (`MeshClip.face_rects`), never its bounds' whole face. A partner that is not in the tree (a section built alone) is skipped; across two sections the later-built block yields, since a block only finds a partner already in the tree when it builds.
- **Who yields is data, written by a tool.** `tools/solve_insets.gd` (a scene: `godot --headless res://tools/solve_insets.tscn --quit-after 900`) finds every coplanar pair of the level with every `yields_to` empty and prints `YIELD <section>|<node>|<path to the winner>`; `tools/apply_yields.py <output>` writes them into `street.tscn`, `roofs.tscn` and `boss_roof.tscn` (a block the output names becomes an `InsetBlock`, any other goes back to a plain `LevelBlock`). The order is one total order, so no two blocks yield to each other: a kit piece (it cannot yield) over a block, then the bigger box, then the path. 97 yield paths on 89 blocks (27 in the street, 59 on the roofs, 3 on the boss roof).
- **The code-built rooms** (`crowd_room.gd`, `test_room.gd`): `RoomKit.resolve_coplanar(geometry)` runs once the room's boxes are in, with the same rule (the bigger box, then the earlier, wins), and records what each box leaves out in the meta `RoomKit.CLIPPED_META` (the scan reads it). Colliders and the navmesh never change.
- **The guard** (`tests/m6_coplanar_scan.gd`) now also takes each `KitPiece` (the Dressing's cornices, windows and frames) as a box, and a `LevelBlock` as the box it draws (its size, not its mesh's bounds: the plinth boards would make every tall wall 5 cm bigger). `pairs()` subtracts what a face leaves out; `holes()` finds a taken-out rectangle that no other box draws in that plane; `steps()` finds two overlapping boxes whose faces of one direction are 5 to 50 mm apart where the nearer covers part of the farther and leaves part of it in view (a rail's post inside its curb and a barrier's panel are by design and skipped). `steps()` finds 27 on `d582d21`, the catwalk's wall, the pillars and the penthouse among them, and 0 now. `test_m6_feedback_ground.gd` holds the level, the crowd street and the training room to all three at 0.02 m2 (the rooms' `ROOM_MIN_AREA_M2` is gone).
- **Tunables.** Level > Ground: `kit_coplanar_m` (0.004) and `kit_clip_standoff_m` (0.06), read when a block or room is built. `INSET_STEP_M` is deleted (there are no tiers).

**VF2-WORLD (the vfinal round's fix pass).**

- **Trim wins over walls.** `tools/solve_insets.gd` ranks a kit piece, then trim (a block whose name holds `Jamb`, `Lintel` or `Post`: `TRIM_NAMES`), then the bigger box, then the path. A jamb, lintel or rail post that shares its wall's plane now draws its face, and the wall takes the overlap out of its own; no trim block yields to a wall. At 82bab12 the two fought and the trim won in most frames. Rails: a post's top wins the bar's, so every rail top shows its plain olive face (the dark slash was the less usual of the old two looks).
- **No hollow ledges.** `MeshClip.clip` also cuts a standoff board's other faces (a plinth's top ledge and end caps, `Kind.BOARD_EDGE`, and its back) with the rectangle, so nothing stands 5 cm in front of the face the partner draws. `tests/m6_coplanar_scan.gd` `orphans()` counts them (0).
- **Guards.** `InsetBlock.unresolved_yields()` lists a `yields_to` that does not resolve and does not climb out of its section; `_make_mesh` `push_warning`s each, and `tests/test_m6_feedback_ground.gd` asserts none in the level. `steps()` exempts only a post against its bar, a curb or a barrier panel, and the pair `ACCEPTED_STEPS` names (the catwalk's `ShutterStair3` panel against `Lintel3`). The scan reads `Tuning.kit_coplanar_m`.
- **MeshClip robustness.** It carries the vertex, normal, tangent, UV, colour and index, drops zero-area fan triangles (`MIN_AREA_M2`, a documented numerical epsilon) and `push_error`s on a surface whose format has a UV2, custom, bones or weights (`MeshClip.uncarried`). A `KitPiece` builds its mesh on demand (`ensure_mesh()`), so a block's clip does not depend on whether the piece comes earlier in the tree.

### Playtest feedback: FB-DETAIL

Game-boy found the Bruiser "blobby" and asked for every enemy to be upscaled toward its concept. The pilot changed the chain, not the model (the raw Tripo mesh holds the plates and quilting; the loss was in the palette and ink stages). Three opt-in config keys, each defaulting to today's behaviour (every other character's `run.py --check` is unchanged):

- `palette.detail_bake.crease` `{angle_deg, width_m, ramp_m, spacing_m, min_chain_m}`: the dense mesh's sharp edges (dihedral angle over `angle_deg`, in runs of at least `min_chain_m` of connected edges) are drawn as ink on the baked texels (`pipe_detail.crease_edges`, `crease_field`), merged with the texture's ink before the stroke cleanup. A `detail_bake.zones[]` entry's `crease_deg` replaces the angle on its faces (over 180 = off). The report gains a `crease` block only when the key is set.
- `palette.min_island_area_m2`, `island_passes` (default 2): a connected patch of one label under that area takes the label its border shares most (step 3-island of `blender_palette.py`, after the majority filter). The report gets an `island_merge` entry in its `regions` list only when set.
- `palette.border_relax_iterations` is not new, but the Bruiser sets it to 0: the relax smoothed the colour borders, which are the plate edges.
- Review tool: `tools/pipeline/blender_stage_still.py <work.blend> <out.png> tex|swatch|solid <view> [px] [cx cy cz ortho_m]`.

The Bruiser stays at 15,000 triangles. Tests: `tests/test_m6_feedback_detail.gd`; his roster and strain ratchets moved with their reasons in `docs/milestones/M6-calls/FB-DETAIL.md`, which also holds the stage attribution and the rollout recipe for the Kicker, the Pipe Thug, Ox Pellerin and the Punk.

### Playtest feedback: DETAIL-2

Phase 2a of Game-boy's "less blobby" ruling: the Bruiser's colour borders, quilting and line weight. Three more opt-in chain changes; every default is today's behaviour, so every other character's `run.py --check` (and `player_run.py --check`) is unchanged.

- **Texel swatches** (`palette.detail_bake.texel_swatch` `{blur_px, passes, fill_px, rings}`). Each baked texel of the detail mask also gets a palette slot, in **B** as `slot + 1` (0 = none), and the PNG is RGBA with **alpha 128/255 as the marker** (`pipe_detail.CODE_MARK`; Godot's import drops an alpha of 250 and over as opaque). The slot is the Lab-nearest swatch of the dense texture's colour at the texel (blurred `blur_px`), chosen only among the slots the texel's dense face and its neighbours (`rings` rings) carry, so a stray patch the island merge removed stays removed; a 3 x 3 majority (`passes`) cleans the speckle, and `fill_px` pads the islands. Faces a region painted or a fixed part (the fists) keep their face's slot (`pipe_detail.texel_labels`, report block `detail_bake.texel_swatch` only when set).
- **`toon.gdshader`**: when `use_detail_tex && use_texel_swatch`, one `texelFetch` of `detail_tex` at `UV2` (no filtering: an index must not be interpolated); if its alpha is under 0.9 and B is above 0 the fragment takes that slot's atlas swatch (`ATLAS_GRID` 32 x 2, mirrored by `ToonMaterials.texel_swatch_uv(code8)`): the colour, the damage class (`takes_marks`, skin, no-blood) and the gloss all follow it, so `marks` and `look_marks_per_swatch` follow the border. A uniform, never a variant. From `texel_swatch_fade.x` to `.y` texels per pixel the colour fades to the face's swatch (the class switches at half), so a border at texel size (4 mm) does not crawl at 5-10 m. Every other mask is opaque RGB (the hero's B is a grey), so no other art changes by one pixel.
- **Valley ink** (`palette.detail_bake.crease.valley` `{angle_deg (999 = off), min_chain_m, width_m, ramp_m, spacing_m}`, a zone's `valley_deg`): the concave edges of the dense mesh (the far corner of one face in front of the other's plane: folds, quilt seams, a plate's foot) at their own, lower, angle and chain length, merged (max) with the crease field. `pipe_detail.crease_edges(..., concave=None|True|False)` is the signed version of the pilot's dihedral.
- **Tunables**: "Look" > "Toon" (beside `look_detail_cut`; the subgroup list is held by `test_m4_contracts`): `look_texel_swatch_fade_near` (1.0), `look_texel_swatch_fade_far` (3.0); "Debug": `look_texel_swatch` (on; off = M6's face swatches, an A/B the presets never hold).
- **Review tools**: `tests/bot/acts/detail2_acts.gd` ("Look: Bruiser jacket": the Bruiser turned 0, 90 and 150 degrees at 1.6 m, where the guard does not hide the jacket).
- **Config**: the Bruiser's crease line is `width_m` 0.0035 + 1.7 `ramp_m` 0.0025 = 7.8 mm across (10.1 in the pilot); `scarf` and `helmet` zones switch the crease and valley ink off on the scarf and the helmet; `palette.regions` paints the helmet's back and top purple (only from steel and black). The GLB is the pilot's (15,000 triangles, unchanged).

Tests: `tests/test_m6_feedback_detail.gd` (codes per texel, other masks unmarked, the decode, the material's switch and fade, the helmet, the line weight and quilting config). Calls and the rollout recipe, part 2: `docs/milestones/M6-calls/DETAIL-2.md`. FIX-ART-B (the Bruiser): the helmet's back and top are their own swatch, slot 47 `bruiser_helmet` (the jacket's purple with `"blood": false`, alpha class 230), so a helmet scuffs and never bleeds; the zone `visor` (slots 45 and 46) turns the crease off and the region `under_visor` puts the scarf's purple (slot 44) where the texture had a cream wedge. No API change; the atlas gains slot 47.
