## Look profiles (M7)

A look profile is one complete look the presentation can draw: `m6` (Milestone 6's, the default and the one every M6 test pins) or `trailer` (the look of the cinematic trailer, which Milestone 7 builds up in data). The switch is Tuning's `look_profile` (group "Look profile", before Audio: 0 = m6, 1 = trailer), and **F7** flips it live (`GameInput` action `look_profile`, listed in the help overlay), so any build is a side-by-side. The default becomes `trailer` only at sign-off.

**Data.** Each profile is a `LookProfileData` resource (`game/fx/look_profile_data.gd`) in `game/fx/look_profiles/<name>.tres`, named by `LookProfile.NAMES`. Its defaults are M6's values, so `m6.tres` is empty and a profile lists only what it changes:

| Field | What it drives | Consumer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `light_overrides` | entries of a light (`LevelSection.light()`'s keys: sun elevation, yaw, colour, energy, fog colour and density, sky top and horizon) that replace the section's own, the room's default, or the crowd street's and training room's sky and fog | `LevelRoot.light_for()`, `LookPost._apply_room_light()` |
| `grade_lut_path` | the grade LUT | `LookPost._apply_grade()` (re-reads the strip when the path changes) |
| `bloom_allowed` | the Environment's `glow_enabled` | `LookPost._apply_grade()` |
| `ink_width_scale` | a multiplier on `look_outline_px` (the hull) and `look_edge_px` (the edge line) | `ToonMaterials.apply_look()`, `LookPost._apply_edges()` |
| `vfx_art_dir`, `hud_style` | the effect art folder and the HUD style set | accessors only so far; `VfxArt` and `HudStyle` are not wired to them yet |

For now `trailer.tres` differs from M6 in one marker alone, three colours in `light_overrides` (a deep blue sky top, an orange horizon, a plum fog), which proves the path end to end; later builders add data to it (and wire the two accessors), not script constants.

**Code.** `LookProfile` (`game/fx/look_profile.gd`) is the one helper the presentation asks: `current()`, `profile_name()`, `data()`, `resolve_light(light)` (the light with the overrides on it, the same dictionary when there are none), `cycle()` and one accessor per field. A flip is `Tuning.set_value(&"look_profile", n)`: `look_` names already make `LookPost` re-apply (edge pass, grade, bloom, the room light) and `ToonMaterials` re-apply every live material, and `LevelRoot` snaps its light blend to the new profile's light (no 2.5 s blend, no reload). Flipping back restores every M6 value exactly (`tests/test_look_profile.gd` compares the Environment, the sky, the sun and every shader uniform of every material on Mission 1's street, and the whole crowd street).

**Layer rule.** The profile is presentation: simulation code (`game/combat`, `actors`, `enemies`, `player`) never reads `LookProfile` or `look_profile`, and a test scans for it.

**Per-profile character art (T05b).** A `CharacterArt` may carry an optional trailer variant (another `CharacterArt`, `metadata/trailer` in the `.tres` like `detail_sharp`, so M4's pinned fields are unchanged; `CharacterArt.trailer_art()`): the art the character wears under the trailer profile, with its own GLB (a different mesh, even a new UV2, bound to the same 23 bones), palette, detail mask, `detail_sharp` flag and triangle budget, so its material is its own. A character with no variant keeps its m6 art under every profile, and a variant's own variant is never read. `CharacterArt.variant(profile)` picks it; **`ArtLook`** (`game/fx/art_look.gd`) is the bridge, so the rigs (in `game/actors`, which never read the profile) ask `ArtLook.worn(art)` when they build and `ArtLook.is_profile_change(property)` on Tuning's `changed`:

- `HumanoidRig.art` stays the m6 art; `worn_art()` is what it draws now. `swap_art(new_art)` (called by the rig itself on a flip, and by nothing else) removes the old art's meshes and attaches the new art's on the same skeleton, so the pose, the animation, the hurtboxes and the ragdoll carry on untouched; then it re-applies layers and the art/primitives A/B and emits `art_swapped(worn)` (a camera holding old meshes drops them). A variant that breaks the art contract (`SkinnedArt.check()`) is never worn: the rig keeps its m6 art. A rig spawned after a flip builds in the running profile's art. `art_follows_look = false` (the warm-up rigs) wears exactly `art`.
- `RigPaint` keeps one art material per art the rig has worn, so flipping back restores the very same material object. A new one starts in the rig's flash and damage look; `set_damage_look`, `set_flash` and `show_flash_overlay` reach every one, so the one not shown is current when it comes back. The hull is the shared `ToonMaterials.outline()`. `base_art_material()` is the m6 art's material, where an enemy's palette swap or tint goes (`EnemyActor._apply_variant_look()`), so it never overwrites a variant's own palette.
- Warm-up: `RoomKit.build_warm_rigs()` builds one throwaway rig per art, the Punk's and the trailer variant of it and of every catalog art (`variant_arts_of()`), each wearing its own art whatever the profile, and `ToonMaterials.warm_up_with(parent, skinned, variants)` (`warm_up(parent, skinned)` is it with none) draws their skeletons with their own materials and hulls in the same pass, so a flip compiles no pipeline mid-fight. `warm_catalog_arts()` also reads and holds each variant's GLB.
- The first-person camera caches the player's arm and torso meshes (`set_player()` clears the cache) and listens to the player's rig's `art_swapped` (M7 T14): a flip frees the old art's meshes, so it forgets the torso, upper-arm and head caches and anything it had lifted, and the next update finds the new art's. The hero's trailer variant (`player_trailer_art.tres`, below) is the first shipped one.
- Proof: `tests/test_art_profile.gd` with a fixture under `tests/fixtures/m7/` (the Dummy's GLB twice, the variant with an inverted palette: no new art).

**The trailer kit (T15).** Under profile `trailer` the street's walls, windows, ground and chain-link wear a second sheet pair, `assets/export/kit/trailer_trim_sheet.png`, `trailer_ground_sheet.png` and `trailer_trim_strips.json`, written by `python3 tools/gen_trailer_kit.py` (Pillow; `--check` repaints and compares; `tools/gen_trim.gd` and the M6 files are never touched). The strips keep M6's names, rows and flags (so `KitMesh`'s vertex-colour indices hold under both profiles) but have their own heights, `period_m` and a per-strip `u_m` (the metres of U one repeat covers: 8 m for brick and glass, 16 m for the facade), so a 17 m wall shows 2 repeats of the brick row instead of 4. `TrimSheet` answers for the running profile (`is_trailer()`, `table()`, `strip()`, `texture()`, `ground_texture()`, `uniform_rows()`); `TrimSheet.apply_profile()` (wired once from `KitMesh.Builder.finish()`, called on every `look_profile` change) puts the profile's texture, ground tile and strip rows on the two shared trim materials and touches no other uniform.
- **Variants.** `TrimSheet.variant_for(position)` (a block's or piece's `position` in its parent, a 32-bit integer hash, modulo Tuning's `trailer_kit_variants`; -1 under M6) selects an alternate: `KitMesh.build(kind, size, variant)` keeps a mesh per (kind, size, variant) and `TrimSheet.variation(strip, variant, face_n)` moves every face on the sheet: a U offset (snapped to whole panes on the glass strip), a V offset (brick and facade), a U mirror, and a tint nudge of `trailer_kit_tint_jitter` where the strip allows. Under M6 the variant is ignored and the cache key is M6's, so M6's meshes are the same objects as before. `LevelBlock` and `KitPiece` swap their mesh in place on a flip (the collider and the body are untouched; `tests/test_trailer_kit.gd` compares every static collider of Mission 1 before and after).
- **Tunables.** Trailer kit: `trailer_kit_variants`, `trailer_kit_tint_jitter`.
- **Open requests to the shader owner (docs/milestones/M7-calls/t15.md):** an unlit/emissive strip flag so the lit window panes glow in the night light.

## The painted material (M7)

The trailer profile's surface language for a character (T11). It is a second material path beside the palette atlas's, chosen by the art, never by a shader switch, and it exists only for an art that carries paint: m6's materials, shaders and uniforms are untouched (`tests/test_toon_painted.gd` compares every m6 material's uniforms across a flip and back).

**The art contract (what T12 to T14 build to).** A trailer variant `CharacterArt` is painted when its `.tres` carries two metadata textures, `metadata/paint_tex` and `metadata/ink_tex` (`ToonMaterials.PAINT_META`, `INK_META`; `ToonMaterials.is_painted()`). Both are **2048 px, on UV2** (the mesh's second UV set, one island per part, no overlap), imported **lossless, mipmaps on, `detect_3d/compress_to` 0 and `fix_alpha_border` off for the ink map** (a compressed or colour-bled ink map breaks the class bytes). `ToonMaterials.art_for(art)` makes the material and `ToonMaterials.outline_for_art(art)` the hull; `RigPaint` and `SkinnedArt.attach()` call them, so a painted variant needs no other code.

| File | Channel | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `paint.png` | RGB (sRGB) | the flat local colour with the shadow shapes drawn in (a designed shadow, one per form); no lighting, no lines |
| `ink.png` | R | the line: inner folds, seams, faces (cut at `look_paint_line_cut` with a one-pixel edge, so it stays crisp; the outer line is the hull's) |
| | G | the damage class, as the palette atlas's alpha bytes: **255** clothes (scuffs, then blood), **230** clothes without blood, **204** skin (bruises, then blood), **128** none; read at texel precision (nearest), so a class border never blends |
| | B | gloss: **255** where the hard highlight and the glint may show (the lens's streaks), 0 elsewhere; at or above one half is glossy, and the value gates the glint (a soft edge is cut at 0.5) |
| | A | the hull's line weight hint, 0 (the thinnest, `look_paint_hull_weight_min`) to 1 (the heaviest, `weight_max`): taper ends toward 0. **A must be equal on both sides of a UV seam** (the hull's two vertices of a seam share a position and must share a width, or the shell cracks); it is read per vertex at mip `look_paint_hull_weight_lod` |

`tests/fixtures/m7/` holds a generated placeholder pair at 512 px (`gen_painted_fixture.gd`) on the Punk's GLB (`punk_with_painted_art.tres`); `painted_stage.tscn` photographs it.

**Shading (`toon_painted.gdshader`, ported from the video branch's `cine_toon.gdshader`).** Two flat tones, no mid band: the paint above `look_paint_cut` of the light term, below it a shadow tone (`look_paint_shadow_level` of the paint, pulled toward a **per-area hue** by `look_paint_shadow_hue_amount`, luminance kept, so never black), the edge at least `look_paint_term_soft_px` wide so a shadow map's steps are inside it. **At night nothing is added inside the shadow band:** a directional light carries the shadow tone itself (at no less than `look_paint_dir_floor` of a full light, in its hue, so a dim moon never drops the paint to black), and a lamp (a point or spot light, its term times `look_paint_point_gain`) adds only `max(shade - shadow, 0)`, so its pool has a hard edge and floods nothing (`ToonMaterials.painted_lamp_add()` mirrors it). **The rim** is one-sided: from the directional (key) light alone, a hard band on the grazing angle plus `look_paint_rim_key` times the key's facing, narrowed by `look_paint_rim_curve` times the screen-space turn of the normal (so its width follows the form's curvature, with no noise), in the area's warm colour, nothing on the side that turns from the key. **Grain** on the flat fill (never on a line) is a value noise in the rest-pose position (`look_paint_grain`, `look_paint_grain_cells`), gone where a cell is under a pixel. The flash, the damage look (`DamageLook.apply_art`: the same uniform names, the classes from the ink map's G), the edge tag, the hard highlight and the glint keep the atlas material's names and tunables, so every rig path treats it as the atlas art.

**The area's colours.** `ToonMaterials.set_area_look(shadow_hue, rim_color)` gives every painted material (live and future) the area's shadow hue and warm rim colour, `clear_area_look()` returns to Tuning's `look_paint_shadow_hue` and `look_paint_rim_color`. The level's light code (T10) calls it when it applies an area's light (shadow hue: the sky's indigo, plum or lilac; rim: the lamp, the sun or the fire).

**The hull (`toon_outline_painted.gdshader`).** The m6 hull's mode 4 with: the ink map's A as the weight, ink that weighs under a form (`look_paint_hull_under_bias`: heavier where the surface faces down, so the line changes along any bending limb with no map at all), and a distance gain, heavier than m6 near the lens (`look_paint_hull_near_gain` at `look_outline_near_m`, back to 1 by `boost_to_m`) and thinning from `thin_from_m` to `look_paint_hull_far_gain` at `look_outline_far_m`. One hull per ink map and first-person flag (`ToonMaterials.outline_painted()`); it takes the profile's `ink_width_scale` like the m6 hull. `ToonMaterials.painted_hull_width_m()` mirrors the width.

**The trim's grain.** Under the trailer profile the trim's flat fill (under the ink) gets a fine noise: a **global** shader uniform `paint_trim_grain` (project.godot `[shader_globals]`; x the share, `look_paint_trim_grain`, y the cells per metre, `look_paint_trim_grain_cells`; x is 0 under m6), set by `ToonMaterials.apply_trim_grain()`, so no trim material's own uniforms differ between the profiles (T05a's test pins that).

**No compile mid-fight.** All of it is uniforms of two new shaders (the painted material and its hull); `warm_up_with()` draws a painted copy of the skeleton with a blank paint, ink and painted hull in the load-time pass (any real painted variant is also drawn by its own throwaway rig, `RoomKit.build_warm_rigs()`), so flipping to the trailer profile compiles nothing in a fight. The Tuning group is "Painted look (M7)", before Audio.

**The trailer Punk (M7, T12).** `game/actors/art/punk_art.tres` carries `metadata/trailer` = `punk_trailer_art.tres`: the Punk's own mesh and rig in `assets/export/characters/punk/trailer/punk_trailer.glb` (same bones and bounds, a fresh UV2) with `punk_paint.png` and `punk_ink.png` (2048, to the contract above). `tools/pipeline/make_trailer_art.sh <name>` makes such an art from `assets/source/trailer_art/<name>/paint.json` (regions by palette cell, bone and box; shadow shapes; folds; hull weight; `features.svg` for the drawn face) through `trailer_asset.py` (Blender) and `trailer_paint.py`; T13 and T14 reuse it. A variant's recolour of the same maps is `metadata/trailer_paint` on its `EnemyVariant` (`EnemyVariant.trailer_paint()`), put on the worn painted material by `EnemyActor._apply_trailer_paint()` now and after every art swap. Notes: `docs/milestones/M7-calls/t12.md`.

**The trailer hero (M7, T14).** `game/actors/art/player_art.tres` carries `metadata/trailer` = `player_trailer_art.tres`: the hero's own four parts and rig (`assets/export/characters/player/trailer/player_trailer.glb`: the same triangles, weights and bounds, so hand poses, hurtboxes and hit timing are untouched) with a fresh UV2 (m6's left 26 % of the arm part's triangles as slivers, the black holes), relaxed arms, smoothed shading normals (heavy on the arms, light on the fists) and `player_paint.png` / `player_ink.png` to the contract above (grey sleeves, a dark leather cuff and wrist strap, dark gloves with knuckle creases, finger lines, stitching and a buckle). Built by `tools/pipeline/make_trailer_player.sh` (`trailer_player_asset.py`, `trailer_player_paint.py` with the extra region rule `"along": {"bone", "t": [a, b]}`, `trailer_player_features.py`) from `assets/source/trailer_art/player/paint.json`. Notes: `docs/milestones/M7-calls/t14.md`.

| Area | Files |
| --- | --- |
| Contracts (change only by agreement) | `game/autoload/*`, `game/combat/{combat_types,move_data,move_set,combo_branch,hitbox_spec,hit_info,hurtbox}.gd`, this doc |
| Combo parser | `game/combat/combo_parser.gd`, `tests/test_combo_parser.gd` |
| Move data | `game/combat/moves/*.tres`, `game/combat/movesets/*.tres`, `tests/test_move_data.gd`, `tools/gen_moves.gd` (optional) |
| Rig and placeholder animation | `game/actors/humanoid_rig.gd`, `game/actors/placeholder_*.gd`, `tests/test_humanoid_rig.gd` |
| Cameras | `game/camera/*`, `tests/test_cameras.gd` |
| HUD and debug UI | `game/ui/*`, `tests/test_ui.gd` |
| Actor core, player, dummies, hit detection, test room | `game/actors/combat_actor.gd`, `game/combat/hit_detector.gd`, `game/player/*`, `game/enemies/*`, `game/level/*`, `game/weapons/*`, integration tests |
| Enemy base, archetypes and grabs (M3) | `game/enemies/{enemy_actor,enemy_attack,enemy_catalog,enemy_hold}.gd`, `game/enemies/{punk,kicker,thug,bruiser,boss}/*`, `game/actors/poses/*`, `tests/enemy_moves/*`, `tests/enemy_move_checks.gd`, `tests/fixtures/*`, `tests/test_{enemy_actor,enemy_grab,enemy_move_checks,enemy_moves_*,kicker,pipe_thug,bruiser,boss,armor_presentation,player_grabbed,magnetism_crowd}.gd` |
| Director (M3) | `game/enemies/enemy_director.gd`, `tests/test_enemy_director.gd`, `tests/fixtures/stub_director.gd` |
| Crowd room, spawner and nav baker (M3) | `game/level/{crowd_room,room_kit,nav_baker,spawn_room_check,encounter_spawner,encounter_table}.gd`, `game/level/crowd_room.tscn`, `game/level/encounters/*`, `game/ui/cheat_menu.gd` (spawn any enemy, waves, freeze AI), `tests/test_{crowd_room,encounter_spawner,cheat_spawns}.gd` |
| Rear warning, pick-up cue and grab prompt (M3) | `game/ui/{rear_warning,pickup_cue,grab_prompt,boss_health_bar}.gd`, the AI layer of `game/ui/debug_draw.gd`, `tests/test_{rear_warning,pickup_cue,debug_draw_ai,audio_crowd}.gd` |
| View geometry (M3) | `game/combat/view_geometry.gd`, `tests/test_view_geometry.gd` |
